> “我心里怕得要命，因为房里存着相当一批偷来的财物，我知道下一回定罪，就得起判一个极长的刑期。我奔上楼回房，把东西收拾到一处，三点差几分就出了宅子。开门时，我看见雷克斯·兰德先生站在园门边。兰德先生我以前见过，我上工一个月之后，他曾在宅里小住过些时日。他一向待我很和气，是位我十分敬重的先生。
> “他叔叔，已故的特拉斯米尔先生，不喜欢他。他有一回对我说，雷克斯先生花钱大手大脚，人又懒。一看园门边站着雷克斯先生，我的心一下子沉到靴底里去，心想他必定立刻就瞧出事情不对头。他问我他叔叔是不是病了，这一问倒给了我定一定神的工夫，我便告诉他我正要出去办一件万分紧急的事，跑到街上，运气好，拦着一辆出租汽车，把我送到了中央车站。不过我并没有离城，而是摸到里德街一幢我熟识的房子里、我从前住过的一个房间，打那以后就一直藏在那里。午饭后我再也没有见过特拉斯米尔先生。电报到的时候，他也没有出来问是谁来了；宅里来往的人不断，送货的、各色人等都有，除非有什么要紧事，或是来了他的信件电报，我从不向他回禀。我从来没有进过那间保险库，也没有进过通往保险库的过道；手枪，我无论什么时候都不曾有过一支。
> “本供词是我自愿所作，未受任何逼迫；对卡弗探长向我提出的问题，我已逐一作答，他从未暗示过我该怎样回答。”

## XV / 十五

===Original===

In the course of his professional duties Tab Holland had been brought into contact with the master of the Golden Roof on two occasions. The first followed a small scandal, which only remotely touched the restaurant (the woman who was the subject of Tab’s investigation had dined there at an important date) and once in connection with a dead-season topic dealing with the nutritive values of food.
He had found the Chinaman reserved to a point of taciturnity, monosyllabic in speech; a most unsatisfactory person.
Tab knew nothing about him except that he was a successful Chinaman who had gravitated into the restaurant business. He asked Jacques for enlightenment, well knowing that if the news editor could not satisfy his curiosity, it was because Yeh Ling was altogether uninteresting. Jacques was one of those rarities, to whom reference is so frequently made that it might be imagined they were as common as straws in a stable. He was a veritable “mine of information.” The genus occurs sometimes in newspaper offices. Jacques knew everybody and everybody’s wife. He knew why they married. He also knew why stars twinkled and the chemical composition of tears. Quote him a line from any classic and he would give you its predecessor and that which followed. He knew the dates of all important earthquakes and was an authority on the Mogul Emperors. He could sketch you with equal facility the position of Frossard’s second corps at Rezonville on August 17th, 1870, or the military situation at Thermopylae﻿—and dates.

===Chinese===

因职务之故，塔布·荷兰曾与“金顶楼”的东家打过两回交道。头一回是在一桩小小的丑闻之后——那桩丑闻同这家餐馆只有极疏远的一点牵连（塔布调查的那位女子，在一个紧要的日子曾在那里用餐）；另一回则是为一篇应付新闻淡季的稿子，谈的是食物的营养价值。
他发觉这个中国人缄默得近于一言不发，开口净是单字片言；是个极令人不得要领的人物。
塔布对他一无所知，只知道他是个发了迹的中国人，自然而然做进了餐馆这一行。他去请教雅克，心里早有盘算：若是这位新闻主编都满足不了他的好奇心，那便只能说明叶凌这个人压根儿无可足谈。雅克属于那种稀罕人物——人们把他们挂在嘴边提得如此频繁，倒仿佛他们像马厩里的干草一样遍地都是。他是货真价实的“活情报库”。这一属人物偶尔会在报馆里出现。雅克谁都认识，连每个人的太太都认识。他们为什么结的婚，他也知道。他还知道星星为什么眨眼、眼泪的化学成分。随便引哪部经典的一行诗给他，他都能报出上一行和下一行。历次大地震的日期他一概记得，论起莫卧儿诸帝来更是权威。他能同样轻巧地给你勾画出 1870 年 8 月 17 日弗罗萨尔第二军在雷宗维尔的方位，也能勾画温泉关的军情——连日子都一并奉告。

===Original===

The only serious students of the *Megaphone* reference library were the reporters who went there to confound Jacques. They never succeeded.
“Yeh Ling? Yes﻿—queer bird. An educated Chink﻿—got a son who is quite a swell scholar by Chinese standards. He ought to make a good story some day; that house he is building at Storford﻿—it is on the way to Hertford; says that one day his son will be the Chinese Ambassador here and he wants him to have a house worthy of his position. That is what he told Stott. Know Stott? He is a dud architect who knows it all. Weird little devil who looks as if he might have been clever with a different kind of brain. Stott laid out the ground work, sort of Chinese temple with two enormous concrete pillars that are going to stand halfway down the drive. The Pillar of Cheerful Memories and the Pillar of Grateful Hearts. That’s what he is going to call them. Stott thought it was heathenish and wondered if the Bishop would like it. Yes, you ought to see that place, Tab. No, it isn’t built. Yeh Ling has nothing but Chink labour. The Secretary of the Builders’ Union went to see him about it. Yeh Ling said his ancestors had a union of their own which put the bar upon non-Taoist labour. Taoism﻿—”

===Chinese===

《扩音器报》资料室里正经用功的学生，只有那些专程去难倒雅克的记者。他们从未得手过。
“叶凌？认识——是个怪家伙。一个受过教育的中国佬——有个儿子，按中国的标准算得上一号响当当的饱学之士。这家伙早晚能写成一条好新闻；他在斯托福德（Storford）造的那座宅子——就在去赫特福德的路上；他说他儿子早晚要当上中国驻此间的大使，他要让儿子有一座配得上那身份的宅子。这话他是跟斯托特说的。认识斯托特么？一个自认无所不晓的蹩脚建筑师。古怪的小魔头，瞧那副样子，若是换一副别样质地的脑子，本可以挺聪明。底子是斯托特布的置，一座半像中国庙宇的宅子，门前车道走到一半，要立两根大得出奇的水泥柱子。一根‘欢愉记忆之柱’，一根‘感戴之心之柱’——他就要这么给它们命名。斯托特觉得这未免异教气，还拿不准主教大人见了喜不喜欢。对了，那地方你真该去看看，塔布。不，还没盖成。叶凌用的清一色是中国佬的劳工。营造业工会的书记为此去见过他。叶凌说，他的祖上自己就有一个行会，立过禁例，把非道教徒的劳工拒之门外。说到道教——”

===Original===

“I hate to wade into the foaming torrent of your eloquence,” said Tab gently, “but how did you come to meet Stott?”
“Same lodge,” said Jacques. “It is not for me to talk down a brother craftsman﻿—are you one of us by-the-way?”
Tab shook his head.
“Ought to be. Get a little respect for authority into your system. As I was saying, I don’t want to knock Stott, but he’s not everybody’s meat. Go and see that temple or whatever it is, Tab. Might be a good story.”

===Chinese===

“我不大舍得蹚进你这片泡沫翻飞的雄辩激流，”塔布温和地说，“可你是怎么认识斯托特的？”
“同一个分会，”雅克说，“贬损一位会中弟兄，轮不到我——顺便问一句，你也是我们会里的么？”
塔布摇了摇头。
“该入的。也好让你那副筋骨里进去一点对权威的敬畏。我方才说了，我并不想说斯托特的坏话，可他不是人人都消受得了的那碟菜。去瞧瞧那座庙吧，不管它究竟是什么，塔布。说不定是条好新闻。”

===Original===

On the first idle day he had, Tab took his motor bicycle and went out to Storford. He was not entirely without hope that he would see Ursula﻿—her house was only seven miles beyond Storford Hill, and he had reason to know that she had withdrawn herself to her country home. In a letter telling this she had told him in so many words that when she wanted him she would send for him.
He saw the building from a distance.
He had noticed it before﻿—it was hardly possible to miss seeing it, for it stood on the crest of one of the few hills the country boasted. The walls were half finished and heavy wooden uprights rose like the palings of a fence above the queerly laid courses. And one of the pillars already lifted its lofty head. It flanked on one side a broad pathway which was half the width of the house, and stood some fifty feet above the ground, being crowned by a small stone dragon.

===Chinese===

赶上头一个空闲日子，塔布骑上摩托车，出城去了斯托福德。他并非全然不存见到乌苏拉的指望——她的宅子过了斯托福德山再走七英里才到，而他也有缘由知道，她已遁居到乡下的家里。她在讲明此事的信里，还明明白白告诉他：等她要见他时，自会遣人来请。
他远远便望见了那座建筑。
他早先就注意过它——想漏看它几乎不可能，因为它立在这一带屈指可数的几座山头之一的顶上。墙砌了一半，粗大的木立柱像栅栏桩一样，从排布古怪的砖层间竖起。两根柱子已有一根昂起了高高的头。它扼守在一条宽阔走道的一侧——那走道足有宅子一半宽——离地约莫五十英尺，顶上蹲着一只小小的石龙。

===Original===

Tab wondered if this was the Pillar of Grateful Hearts or that which stood, or would stand, for cheerful memories.
Its diameter must have been fully five feet. Near at hand was one of the wooden moulds in which it was cast, and a Chinese workman was scraping the interior.
Tab walked through a break in the low hedge which separated Yeh Ling’s new home from the road and now stood regarding with interest the activities of the blue-bloused workmen. Their industry was remarkable. Whether they were running bricks and mortar, or cutting out the garden (already taking shape) or walling up the terraces, they moved quickly, untiringly, wholly absorbed in their occupations. Never once did they stop to lean upon their spades and picks to discuss the chances of the new administration, or to tell one another how Milligan got his black eye.

===Chinese===

塔布拿不准，这是“感戴之心之柱”，还是那根代表着——或者说将要代表——欢愉记忆的柱子。
它的直径足有五英尺。近旁搁着一副浇铸它用的木模，一个中国工人正在刮模子的内壁。
塔布从把叶凌的新宅同大路隔开的矮树篱的一个豁口走进去，站在那儿饶有兴味地打量那些穿蓝布短褂的工人们的活计。他们的勤勉可观得很。无论是递砖运浆，还是开辟园圃（已见雏形），或是给层层台地垒墙，他们都手脚麻利，不知疲倦，整个儿沉浸在各人的活计里。谁也不曾停下来拄着铁锹镐头，议论一番新政府的胜算，或是互相说说米利根那只乌青眼是怎么来的。

===Original===

Nobody seemed to notice Tab. He strolled further into the land and there was none to challenge his right. A gang of men were gravelling and rolling the broad path and one of these said something which sent the others into a fit of that chittering laughter which is peculiar to the East. Tab wondered what was the joke.
Turning to walk back to the road, he saw that a car had stopped at the break in the hedge, and his heart gave a leap, for its occupant was Ursula.
“What do you think of it?” she asked.

===Chinese===

似乎谁也没有留意塔布。他信步往园子深处走，也没有人出来查问他的来路。一伙人正在给那条宽道铺砾石、碾平，其中一个说了句什么，惹得其余人迸出一阵东方特有的那种叽叽喳喳的笑。塔布纳闷，笑料究竟是什么。
转身往大路走回去时，他看见一辆汽车停在树篱的豁口处，他的心猛地一跳——车里坐的是乌苏拉。
“你觉得它怎么样？”她问。

===Original===

“It is going to be rather wonderful﻿—how do you like the idea of having a Chinaman for a neighbour? I forgot﻿—you rather like the Chinese?”
“Yes,” she said shortly. “There could be worse neighbours than Yeh Ling.”
“You know him?”

===Chinese===

“将来会相当堂皇——有个中国人做邻居，这主意你受用么？我倒忘了——你不是对中国人向来有几分好感？”
“是的，”她简短地说，“比叶凌更糟的邻居也多的是。”
“你认识他？”

===Original===

He wondered if she would deny acquaintance or evade the question.
“Very well,” she said calmly, “he is the proprietor of the Golden Roof. I often dine there. You know him too?”
“Slightly,” said Tab, looking back at the unfinished house. “He must be rich.”
“I don’t know. One never really knows what money is required to build a place like this. The labour is cheap and it seems a very simple kind of house.”

===Chinese===

他揣想她会不会否认相识，或者把话岔开。
“很熟，”她平静地说，“他就是‘金顶楼’的老板。我常在那儿吃饭。你也认识他？”
“略有往来，”塔布说，一面回头望那座未完工的宅子，“他必定很有钱。”
“我不知道。盖这样一处宅子究竟需得多少钱，谁也说不出个准数。劳工便宜，房子瞧着也是顶简单的一种式样。”

===Original===

And then with a wave of her hand, she drove on. She might at least have asked him to lunch, he thought indignantly.
A week went past, a drab week for a discontented Tab Holland, for now there was neither a likelihood of, nor an excuse for, a chance meeting.
A sedative week for hiding Walters. References to the murder seldom appeared now in the newspapers, and he had found a man who had offered to get him a job as a steward on an outward bound liner.

===Chinese===

说罢她一挥手，驱车前行。她总该至少请他吃顿午饭吧——他愤愤地想。
一星期过去了。对满腹不平的塔布·荷兰，这是个灰暗的星期，因为如今既无邂逅的可能，也无邂逅的借口。
对藏匿的沃尔特斯，这是一个安神镇静的星期。如今报上已很少提及那桩凶案，他又寻着一个人，答应替他在一艘外航邮轮上谋一份侍应生的差事。

===Original===

A week of drugged sleep for a besotted man, curled up on a mattress at the top of Yo Len Fo’s house.
But for Inspector Carver an exceptionally busy week though there was no newspaper record of his activities.
Tab no longer spent his evenings at home. The flat seemed horribly empty now that the lovesick Rex had gone. He had had a radio from him saying that he was improved in health. The message was cheerful enough, so that Ursula’s refusal could not have bitten very hard.

===Chinese===

对那个烟昏了头的人——蜷在尤连福屋子顶楼一张床垫上的那一个——这是药力昏睡的一星期。
唯独对卡弗探长，这是格外忙碌的一星期，尽管他的种种动作报上一字未记。
塔布不再在家消磨夜晚。害相思的雷克斯一走，这套公寓空得怕人。他收到过他一封无线电报，说身体见好。电文欢快得很，可见乌苏拉的回绝还不曾咬得太狠。

===Original===

By the end of the week, life had become an intolerable dreariness, and to make matters worse, nothing was happening in the great world that called for Tab’s intervention and interest. He was in that condition of utter boredom when there happened the first of those remarkable incidents which, in his official account of the case, Inspector Carver refers to as “The Second Activity.”
The flats, one of which Tab occupied, had originally been apartments in a private house. With little structural alterations they had been turned into self-contained suites. On each of the landings was a door of one of the four flats. Admission to the house was by the front door, and the landlord had so arranged matters that, whilst the key of each flat was different, all keys opened the street-door. It was, therefore, possible to go in and out without observation, unless by chance one of the other tenants happened to be on the stairs or in the passageway at the time.
On Saturday night Tab knew he would be alone in the house; the other three tenants invariably spent the weekend out of town. One was a middle-aged musician who lived on the top floor. Beneath him was a young couple engaged in literary work; then came Tab’s flat, and the ground floor suite was occupied by a man whose profession was unknown, but who was generally believed to be connected with an advertising agency. He was seldom at home, and Tab had only seen him once.

===Chinese===

到了周末，日子已沉闷得令人难耐；更糟的是，大千世界毫无动静，没有什么事需要塔布插手、引他上心。他正处在这种百无聊赖的当口，那一系列离奇事件中的头一桩发生了——在关于本案的官方报告里，卡弗探长把它称作“第二次活动”。
塔布住着其中一套的这幢公寓楼，原是一栋私宅里的几间屋子。房屋结构稍加改动，它们便成了各自成套的独立套间。每段楼梯平台上，都有一扇门通向四套公寓之一。进楼走前门，房东又把事情安排成这样：各套公寓的钥匙互不相同，临街大门却把把钥匙都开得。因此，进出这幢楼可以不惹人耳目——除非碰巧哪位别的房客当时正在楼梯上或过道里。
礼拜六夜里，塔布知道楼里将只剩他一人；另外三位房客照例都出城度周末。一位是住顶楼的中年音乐家；他楼下是一对舞文弄墨的年轻夫妇；再往下便是塔布的套间；底层那套住着一个职业不明的人，不过一般都相信他同一家广告代理行有干系。他难得在家，塔布只见过他一面。

===Original===

The Saturday night happened to be the occasion of an annual dinner of his club, and Tab dressed and went out early, spent a mildly exhilarating evening and returned home at half-past twelve. There was nothing in first appearance to suggest that anything unusual had happened in his absence, except that the lights in his sitting-room were burning and he had switched them off before he went out.
His first impression was that the waste of current was due to his own carelessness, but then he recalled very clearly that he had turned out the light and closed the sitting-room door before he went out. Now the sitting-room door was open, as also was the door of Rex’s old room.

===Chinese===

这个礼拜六夜里，恰逢他所属俱乐部的年度晚宴，塔布穿戴整齐，早早出了门，过了一个微带兴奋的夜晚，十二点半回的家。乍看之下，没有任何迹象表明他不在时发生过什么不寻常的事，只有一桩：他起居室里的灯亮着，而他出门前分明已把灯关了。
他的第一个念头是，这点浪费掉的电得怪自己粗心；可他随即清清楚楚记起，出门前他熄了灯，还关上了起居室的门。此刻起居室的门敞着，雷克斯旧日那间屋的门也同样敞着。

## XVI / 十六

===Original===

Tab smiled to himself. He who had investigated so many burglaries had never imagined that he would be favoured by the attention of those midnight adventurers. He went into Rex’s room, turned the switch and had only to take one glance to know that somebody had been very busy indeed in his absence. Under the bed which his companion had occupied, were two shallow trunks, filled with those of Rex Lander’s belongings which he had not taken with him. One of these had been pulled out, placed on the bed and opened. It had been opened unscientifically with a chisel, which Tab knew was his property, and must have been taken from the toolbox in the kitchen. The lock was wrenched off and the contents of the box were scattered on the bed. The other trunk had not been touched. Whether the thief had been successful in his quest Tab did not know, because he was ignorant of the box’s contents. He guessed he must have been disappointed, for beyond a quantity of underlinen, more or less in a state of disrepair, a few books and drawing instruments, and a packet of letters which Tab saw at a glance were from Jesse Trasmere, there was nothing at all valuable in the trunk.

He went to his own room, but none of his things had been touched. And then he began a careful search of the other rooms in the flat. They yielded, however, no clue as to the identity of the mysterious visitor, and Tab got on to the phone to Carver and was lucky to find him.

===Chinese===

塔布暗自笑了。经他手调查过的盗窃案不知凡几，却从没想过自己竟会蒙这些午夜冒险家的光顾。他走进雷克斯的房间，拧开电门，只消一眼便知道：他不在的当口，确曾有人在这里大忙过一阵。同屋那位伙伴住过的床底下，放着两只扁浅的箱子，装的都是雷克斯·兰德没随身带走的物件。其中一只已被拖出来，摆到床上，打开了。开得毫无章法，用的是一把凿子——塔布认得那是他自己的东西，必定是从厨房的工具箱里取走的。锁被生生撬掉，箱里的东西散了一床。另一只箱子没有动过。那贼此行有没有得手，塔布无从知道，因为箱里原先装着什么，他并不了然。他猜那人必定失望了：因为除去一批多少有些破损的内衣、几本书和绘图仪器，还有一沓信——塔布一眼便看出是杰西·特拉斯米尔写来的——箱子里再没有什么值钱的东西。

他到自己房里看了看，东西一样也没被动过。随后他把公寓里其余的房间仔细搜了一遍。然而这一搜没有查出半点线索，足以指明那位神秘来客的身份，塔布便打电话给卡弗，运气不错，人在。

===Original===

“Burglars? That’s poetic justice, Tab,” said Carver’s sad voice. “I’ll come right along.”

The detective was at the house in ten minutes.

===Chinese===

“进贼了？这可真是绝妙的报应，塔布。”卡弗的嗓音里带着郁郁的愁意。“我这就过来。”

十分钟工夫，侦探便到了这所宅子。

===Original===

“If this had happened in the daytime I could find a fairly simple explanation,” said Tab, “because the front door below is left open until nine, and the tenant who comes in or goes out nearest to nine o’clock, closes it. We keep the door open because it saves a lot of running up and down stairs, but the street-door was closed when I came home.”

“How would it have been a simple matter to burgle the flat?” asked Carver, and Tab explained that there was a window on the landing through which a surefooted and skilful adventurer might emerge on to a narrow ledge by which the kitchen window could be reached.

“He didn’t go that way I should think,” said Carver, after he had inspected the kitchenette. “No, the burglar opened the door like a gentleman. Do you know whether Mr. Lander had anything worth stealing in that trunk?”

===Chinese===

“这事要是出在白天，我倒能给出一个相当简单的解释，”塔布说，“因为楼下的前门一向敞到九点，进出最贴近九点的那位房客负责把它关上。门敞着为的是省得人一趟趟跑楼梯，可我回家的时候，临街的大门是关着的。”

“那怎么会是撬这公寓的便当路数呢？”卡弗问。塔布便解释说，楼梯平台上有一扇窗，脚跟稳、手段巧的冒险家可以打那儿爬出来，踏上一条窄窄的檐台，循着它便能摸到厨房的窗子。

“他走的不该是那条道。”卡弗查验过小厨房之后说，“不是，那贼开门开得像个绅士。兰德先生那只箱子里有没有什么值得一偷的东西，你知道吗？”

===Original===

Tab shook his head.

“I am perfectly certain he hadn’t,” he said. “Poor old Rex had nothing of value except the money he drew from his uncle’s estate just before he left.”

Carver went back to Rex’s room and carefully emptied the trunk item by item.

“It was something at the bottom of the trunk. I should imagine it was in this box.”

He handled a little wooden box with a sliding lid.

“And here is the top,” he said, picking it up from the bed. “Can you get in touch with Mr. Lander?”

“He’ll be at Naples in a day or two. I’ll wire him then, but I shouldn’t imagine he had lost anything worth the thief’s trouble,” said Tab.

===Chinese===

塔布摇了摇头。

“我拿得稳他没有，”他说，“可怜的老雷克斯除了临走前从他叔父遗产里支的那笔钱，再没有值钱的物件。”

卡弗回到雷克斯房里，把箱子里的东西一件一件细细腾空查点。

“要找的是件藏在箱底的东西。依我想，就装在这只盒子里。”

他端起一只小小的木盒，盖子是抽拉式的。

“这儿是盒盖，”他从床上拾起那盖子，说道，“你能联络上兰德先生吗？”

“他一两天内就到那不勒斯了。到时我给他发电报。不过依我想，他丢的东西怕还值不得那贼费这番手脚。”塔布说。

===Original===

They went back to the sitting-room and Carver stood a long time by the table, tapping its covered surface nervously, his long face puckered in thought.

“Do you know what I think?” he said suddenly.

“Generally,” said Tab.

“Do you know what I am thinking now?”

“You think I am giving you a lot of trouble over a happening which wasn’t worth mentioning,” said Tab.

Carver shook his head.

===Chinese===

两人回到客厅。卡弗在桌边站了许久，手指紧张地敲着铺了台布的桌面，长长的脸皱作一团，凝神苦思。

“你可知道我在想什么？”他忽然说。

“大抵知道。”塔布说。

“你可知道我这会儿在想什么？”

“你觉得我为一件不值一提的小事，给你添了好些麻烦。”塔布说。

卡弗摇了摇头。

===Original===

“I am thinking this,” he said slowly and deliberately, “that the man who burgled this flat was the man who killed Jesse Trasmere! If you ask me to give chapter and verse for my conclusion I shall both disappoint you and disappoint myself. I have always found,” he went on, “that when one has an instinctive conviction, it is a mistake to make too close an examination of one’s mind. Every human being was endowed some time or other, with as powerful and potent an instinct as the most sensitive of wild animals. With the growth of reason, the instinctive quality faded, until today, in humanity, we find only the faintest trace of it. Yet,” he said earnestly, “it is possible for humanity to cultivate that germ of instinct so that one can go to a racetrack and pick every winner.”

“You are joking,” said Tab surprised, but Carver shook his head.

“You get flashes of it at times; you call them ‘hunches.’ It is really your atrophied instinct asserting itself. But you won’t give it a chance. You slay it with the hands of logic and smother it with argument, and my instinct tells me that the hand that opened Mr. Lander’s trunk was the hand that destroyed Trasmere. I had a queer feeling when you telephoned to me,” he went on, “a queer feeling as though you or somebody was going to hand to me a ready-made solution of Trasmere’s death.”

“And you are disappointed. My poor old Carver,” said Tab, pityingly. “You think too much!”

“We all think too much,” said Carver, relapsing into his natural gloom.

===Chinese===

“我是这么想的，”他说得又慢又斟酌，“撬了这所公寓的，就是杀死杰西·特拉斯米尔的那个家伙！你要我给这个结论拿出确凿的出处，那我就既叫你失望，也叫我自己失望。我一向发觉，”他接着说，“人一旦有了本能的确信，把自己的心思剖解得太细便是个错误。每一个人，在某个时期都曾禀有一种本能，其强劲灵敏，不亚于最敏锐的野兽。随着理性的增长，这本能的品性便渐渐消褪，到得今天，在人类身上，我们只能寻出它最淡的痕迹。然而，”他恳切地说，“人类是可以把这粒本能的胚芽培育起来的，培育到一个人能上赛马场，场场都挑中头马。”

“你这是说笑。”塔布讶然道。卡弗却摇了摇头。

“你有时能捕捉到它的一闪；你们管那叫‘预感’。其实那是你萎缩了的本能在自我申张。可你不给它机会。你用逻辑的双手扼杀它，用滔滔的争辩闷死它。而我的本能告诉我：撬开兰德先生箱子的那只手，就是断送特拉斯米尔的那只手。你打电话给我的时候，我心里就有种怪怪的感觉，”他接着说，“怪怪的，仿佛你或者什么人，就要把特拉斯米尔之死的一个现成答案，捧到我手上。”

“结果你失望了。我可怜的老卡弗，”塔布怜惜地说，“你想得太多啦！”

“我们都想得太多。”卡弗说，又沉回他天生的郁郁寡欢里去。

===Original===

The next morning the tenant who occupied the flat below came up whilst Tab was dressing and Mr. Holland was a little taken aback to see one who so seldom put in an appearance on any day. He was a red-faced gentleman, somewhat sportily attired.

“I hope you didn’t mind my shouting up at you last night,” he said apologetically, “but I had been travelling night and day and I had had no sleep and naturally I was a little rattled when I heard that noise going on overhead. Did you drop a box or something?”

“To be exact, I didn’t drop a box at all,” said Tab. “In fact, the noise you heard was made by a burglar.”

“A burglar?” said the startled man. “I heard the row and it woke me up. I got out of bed and yelled up, as I thought, to you.”

“What time was this?”

“Between ten and half-past,” said the other. “It was just getting dark.”

“He must have dropped the box as he was putting it on the bed,” said Tab thoughtfully. “You didn’t by any chance see him?”

===Chinese===

第二天早晨，塔布正在穿衣，住楼下那套公寓的房客上楼来了；这位先生不论哪一天都极少露面，荷兰先生不由得有几分错愕。他是个红脸膛的绅士，穿得有几分运动家的派头。

“昨儿夜里我朝楼上你那儿嚷嚷，你别见怪，”他歉意地说，“可我连日连夜地赶路，觉也没睡，听见头顶上闹出那么大动静，自然有些着慌。你是不是摔了只箱子什么的？”

“确切说，我压根儿没摔箱子，”塔布说，“其实，你听见的那阵动静，是一个贼弄出来的。”

“一个贼？”那人吃了一惊。“我就是听见那阵响动才惊醒的。我下了床，朝楼上——我当时以为是你——喊了一嗓子。”

“那是几点钟的事？”

“十点到十点半之间，”那位说，“天刚擦黑。”

“他准是把箱子往床上放的时候失了手。”塔布若有所思地说，“你没有碰巧瞧见他吧？”

===Original===

“I heard him go out about a quarter of an hour after I’d made a fuss,” said the man from downstairs, “and I was feeling so ashamed of myself for losing my temper that I opened the door to apologize for shouting at him.”

“You didn’t see him?”

The man shook his head.

“He shut the door quick, just as I got into the passage. The only thing I saw was his hand on the edge of the door. He was wearing black gloves. Naturally I thought it was you, though the black gloves seemed to be a queer sort of thing for a young man to wear, even if he was in mourning, and taking it for granted that it was you, and that you were mad with me, I thought no more about it.”

All this Tab duly reported to Carver.

===Chinese===

“我发过那一通火之后约莫一刻钟，听见他出去了，”楼下的那位说，“我为自己发脾气正自愧得慌，便开了门，想为方才朝他嚷嚷赔个不是。”

“你没看见他？”

那人摇了摇头。

“我刚走进过道，他就飞快地把门带上了。我唯一看见的，是他搭在门边上的一只手。他戴的是黑手套。我自然以为那是你——虽说黑手套这东西戴在一个年轻人手上未免古怪，即便他正服着丧。我既认定了那是你、认定你在生我的气，便再没多想。”

这些话，塔布一五一十地报给了卡弗。

===Original===

That ended the episode of Saturday. Sunday’s surprise was more pleasant but not less disturbing. It was late in the evening, and Tab was reading by the light of a table-lamp, when the bell which connected with the front door rang urgently. This meant that the front door was closed. On the night of Wellington Brown’s visit it was open. He unconsciously connected the two visitations and wondered whether his instinct was working as well as Carver could wish. Putting the book aside, he went down and opened the door, and nearly staggered in his astonishment, for his visitor was Ursula Ardfem, and her little car stood by the edge of the sidewalk.

“I am on my way to the Central,” she explained. “May I come in?”

He had seen the two suitcases strapped to the back of the car and had wondered to what distant and inaccessible spot she was bound.

“Come in, please,” he said hastily. “I am afraid this room is rather smoky.” He made to pull up the blind, but she stopped him.

“Please don’t,” she said, “I am all nerves and shivers and I feel I could swoon on the slightest excuse. It is rather a pity that that delightful practice of our grandmothers’ days went out of fashion. It would be such a relief to swoon sometimes.” Her tone was half-jesting, but there was a whole lot of seriousness in her face. “I am coming to live at the Central again,” she said, “though I really cannot afford that extravagance.”

===Chinese===

星期六这桩事就此了结。星期日的意外来得比较叫人愉快，却一样搅得人心神不宁。那晚夜深了，塔布正就着一盏台灯的光读书，连通前门的门铃忽然急促地响起来。这意味着临街的大门是关着的。威灵顿·布朗来访的那一夜，门却是开着的。他不知不觉把这两次夜访联系到一处，暗自纳闷：自己的本能不能也运转得像卡弗期望的那样灵。他搁下书，下楼开门，几乎惊得一个踉跄——来客竟是乌苏拉·阿德芬，她那辆小汽车正停在人行道边上。

“我正要上中央饭店去，”她解释道，“能进来坐坐吗？”

他早已望见车尾捆着的两只衣箱，正自纳闷她这是要奔赴哪一处遥远而人迹难至的去所。

“快请进，”他忙说，“只是这屋子怕是烟味儿重了些。”他伸手要去拉起百叶帘，却被她拦住了。

“请别拉，”她说，“我浑身是神经、浑身是寒噤，只觉随便借个由头我就能晕过去。我们祖母辈那桩可爱的风气如今不时兴了，实在可惜。有时候能晕上一晕，该是多大的解脱。”她语气半带玩笑，脸上却满是郑重其事。“我要搬回中央饭店去住了，”她说，“虽说这份阔绰我实在负担不起。”

===Original===

“What has happened?”

“Stone Cottage is haunted,” was the staggering reply.

“Haunted?”

She nodded and a momentary smile came into her eyes, only to fade as quickly as it came.

“Not by a ghost,” she said, “but by a very human man, a mysterious individual in black. The woman who looks after me saw him the other night in the garden: I myself have seen him from my window and challenged him. He has been seen by other people pacing the road outside. Now tell me honestly, Tab Holland, am I under the observation of the police?”

===Chinese===

“出什么事了？”

“石屋闹鬼了。”这一句答话惊得他站立不稳。

“闹鬼？”

她点了点头，眼里掠过一丝转瞬的笑意，来得快，去得也快。

“不是鬼闹，”她说，“是个如假包换的大活人，一个一身黑衣的神秘人物。照应我的那个女人前几夜在花园里瞧见过他；我自己也从窗口望见过他，还喝问过他。旁人也见到他在外面那条路上来回踱步。现在老实告诉我，塔布·荷兰，我是不是被警察监视上了？”

===Original===

The thought had also occurred to Tab.

“I don’t think so,” he said. “Carver does not tell me everything, but he has never mentioned your name to me as being under the slightest suspicion. In black, you say?”

“Yes,” nodded the girl. “From head to foot in black, including black gloves. It was rather spectacular﻿—”

“Black gloves?” interrupted Tab. “I wonder if it is my burglar?” and he told her of the visitor who had come the night before.

“It is extraordinary,” she said, “more extraordinary because he was not seen last night. I am not usually nervous, but I must confess that it is a little worrying to know that somebody is watching me.”

“How did he come? Had he a car or bicycle, or did he come by train?”

On this point she could not enlighten him.

===Chinese===

这念头塔布也曾起过。

“我想没有，”他说，“卡弗不是什么事都告诉我，可他从不曾提过你的名字，说你有一丝一毫的嫌疑。你说，一身黑？”

“是啊，”姑娘点头道，“从头到脚一身黑，连黑手套也一并算上。那派头可真够扎眼——”

“黑手套？”塔布打断她，“莫非就是我那个贼？”于是他把昨夜那位来客的事讲给她听。

“真是离奇，”她说，“更离奇的是，昨儿夜里他倒没了踪影。我平日并不胆小，可知道有人盯着我，我得承认，这多少叫人心慌。”

“他是怎么来的？开着汽车，还是骑自行车，或是搭火车？”

在这一点上，她就说不上来了。

===Original===

“I almost wish you had not come up,” said Tab. “If you had told me, I would have gone to Stone Cottage and stayed the night, especially after my burglar. I should like to meet the gentleman who treats my flat so unceremoniously.”

She made no reply and then:

“Why did I come here, I wonder?” she asked, and it was as though she were speaking to herself, for she laughed. “Poor Mr. Tab,” she said, with that little hint of mockery in her voice which he adored, “I am laying all my burdens upon you. Mystery upon mystery, some of my own, but this, I promise you, not of my making.” She considered, her finger at her lips. “Suppose I return to Stone Cottage on Monday morning and you come down later? My woman will be an efficient chaperon, and I think you should come after dark﻿—that is, if you can spare the time.”

Tab wanted to tell her that all eternity, so far as he was concerned, was at her disposal, but very wisely refrained.

He saw her to her car and went back to his room with a sense of exhilaration that he had not felt all that week.

===Chinese===

“我倒恨不得你方才没上楼来，”塔布说，“你要是早告诉我，我就去石屋守上一夜了，尤其是家里遭了这个贼以后。我倒想会一会这位如此不客气地光顾我公寓的先生。”

她没有作声，过了一会儿才开口：

“我到底为什么要到这儿来呢？”她问，那口气像是自言自语，因为她笑了。“可怜的塔布先生，”她说，嗓音里带着那一丝他所着迷的揶揄，“我把担子全压到你肩上了。一桩谜接着一桩谜，有几桩是我自己的，可这一桩，我向你保证，不是我招来的。”她沉吟着，一根手指抵在唇上。“这么着吧：礼拜一早上我回石屋去，你晚些再下来？我那女佣当陪伴是尽够格的，我想你该等天黑了再来——当然，前提是你抽得出这个空。”

塔布很想告诉她：就他而言，千秋万世尽可以任由她支配。但他很识相，忍住没说。

他送她上车，回到自己房里，心头泛起一种整整一个星期都不曾有过的振奋。

## XVII / 十七

===Original===

It was a delicate matter broaching the subject of police espionage to Carver. In the first place, he did not want to give the inspector the slightest hint that Ursula Ardfern expected to be watched. He compromised by telling that gloomy man, at the first opportunity, that he had seen Miss Ardfern. And then he mentioned casually and by-the-way, the story of her watcher.
“Of course it isn’t a thief,” said Carver promptly. “Thieves do not advertise their presence by alarming the people they hope to rob. Has she complained to the local police?”
Tab did not know, but he guessed that she had not.
“It may be a coincidence,” said Carver, “and the man in black may really have nothing whatever to do with the murder of Trasmere, but I am intrigued. You are going down, you say? I wonder if Miss Ardfern would mind my coming too?”

===Chinese===

要向卡弗提起“警方监视”这个话头，是桩需要拿捏分寸的事。首先，他不愿让这位探长得到丝毫暗示，察觉乌苏拉·阿德芬料想自己会受监视。他折了个中：一有机会，便告诉那位阴郁的人，说他见到了阿德芬小姐。然后才装作漫不经心、顺口一提的样子，说起她被人盯梢的事。
“那当然不是窃贼。”卡弗立刻说。“窃贼决不会靠吓唬自己想偷的人来张扬行迹。她向当地警察报过案吗？”
塔布不知道，不过他猜没有报过。
“也许只是巧合，”卡弗说，“那黑衣人也许当真与特拉斯米尔命案毫不相干，可我起了兴致。你是说你要下乡去？不知阿德芬小姐介不介意我也同去？”

===Original===

Tab was in a dilemma here. To hesitate would be to give the police officer a wholly wrong impression. To accept was to eclipse the happy evening he had in prospect. For to be alone with Ursula Ardfern, to stand to her in the nature of a protector, would be a wonderful experience, which he had no desire to share.
“I am sure Miss Ardfern would be delighted,” he said.
“If I can get away I will come,” said Carver.
Tab fervently hoped that urgent business would keep his friend in town.
He sent a note round to Ursula putting forward Carver’s suggestion and received a reply by return, extending her invitation.

===Chinese===

塔布这下可左右为难了。稍一迟疑，便会给这位警官留下全然错误的印象；一口应承，又要把他指望中的那个良夜搅黄了。因为同乌苏拉·阿德芬单独相处，以类似保护人的身份立于她身旁，将是一段妙不可言的经历，他无意与旁人分享。
“我敢肯定，阿德芬小姐一定非常乐意。”他说。
“要是脱得开身，我就来。”卡弗说。
塔布热切希望有紧急公务把他的朋友留在城里。
他差人给乌苏拉送去一张便条，转达卡弗的提议，随即便收到回音，欣然相邀。

===Original===

After mature thought, Tab decided that it was not at all a bad idea to have Carver with him. It would give the girl an opportunity of making friends with one who might, in certain circumstances, be a difficult man to satisfy. She could not have too many friends, he thought, and was almost relieved when Carver hurried into the station a few minutes before the last train to Hertford left.
It was dark when they arrived and by prearrangement they did not speak in the long walk which separated them from Stone Cottage, but in single file, keeping to the shadow of the road, they marched forward without meeting with a soul.
When at last they came to the highway in which Stone Cottage was situated, they proceeded with greater caution. But there was nobody in sight and they reached the garden unobserved.
Ursula was standing in the doorway to welcome them.

===Chinese===

深思熟虑之后，塔布认定带上卡弗倒也全然不是坏事。这能让姑娘有个机会，去结交一位在某些情形下或许不易讨好的人物。他想，她的朋友再多也不嫌多。于是，当卡弗在开往赫特福德的末班车发车前几分钟匆匆走进车站时，他几乎觉得松了口气。
他们抵达时天已黑透。依照事先的约定，在通往石屋那段长长的步行途中，他们一言不发，一个跟着一个，紧贴路旁的暗影向前走，一路没有遇见一个人影。
等终于走上石屋所在的那条大路，他们愈发小心地行进。但四下里不见人影，他们悄没声息、无人察觉地到了园子跟前。
乌苏拉正站在门口迎候。

===Original===

“I’ve had all the blinds pulled down,” she said, “and Inspector Carver’s coming is rather providential, for my woman has had to go home﻿—her mother has been taken ill. I hope you don’t mind appearing in the role of a chaperone,” she smiled at Carver.
“Even that is not an unusual one,” he replied unsmiling. “Where does she live, the mother of your servant?”
“At Felborough. Poor Margaret only had time to catch the last train.”
“How did Margaret know her mother was ill?” asked the inspector. “Did she have a telegram?”
Ursula nodded.

===Chinese===

“我已叫人把百叶窗全都放下了，”她说，“卡弗探长来得真是天意凑巧，因为我的女佣只好回家去了——她母亲病了。希望您不介意屈尊扮演一回陪护人。”她朝卡弗嫣然一笑。
“就连这个角色也不算少见。”他答话时不带一丝笑意。“您那女佣的母亲住在哪儿？”
“在菲尔伯勒（Felborough）。可怜的玛格丽特（Margaret）只来得及赶末班火车。”
“玛格丽特是怎么知道她母亲病了的？”探长问。“她收到电报了吗？”
乌苏拉点了点头。

===Original===

“Late this afternoon?”
“Yes,” said the girl in surprise. “Why do you ask?”
“She got the telegram in time to catch the train to town; in time, too, to catch a train for Felborough. That was why I asked. You did not see the man last night?”
“I didn’t come down until this morning,” she answered troubled. “Do you think that Margaret has been sent for by﻿—somebody﻿—that it was a ruse to get her away?”

===Chinese===

“今天下午很晚的时候？”
“是啊。”姑娘讶然道。“您为什么问这个？”
“她接到电报，时间刚好来得及赶进城的火车；也刚好来得及再赶去菲尔伯勒的火车。我才这么问。昨天夜里您没见到那个人吧？”
“我直到今天早上才下来的。”她答道，神色不安。“您是不是觉得，玛格丽特是被——什么人——叫走的，那是个调开她的诡计？”

===Original===

“I don’t know,” said Carver. “In my profession we always apply the worst construction and we are generally right. What time do you usually go to bed?”
“At ten o’clock in the country,” she said.
“Then at ten o’clock you will go up to your room, put on your lights, and after a reasonable time, put them out again. You may, if you wish, come down, but you must be prepared to sit in the dark, and if you want to talk, you must carry on your conversation in whispers.” A rare smile softened his face. “We shall probably all be feeling a little foolish in the morning, but I would rather feel foolish than miss the opportunity of meeting the man in black.”

===Chinese===

“我不知道。”卡弗说。“干我们这一行的，凡事先往最坏处解释，而十有八九都想对了。您平时几点就寝？”
“在乡下，十点。”她说。
“那么十点整，您就上楼回房，把灯点亮，过上一段合适的光景，再把灯熄掉。您若愿意，可以下楼来，但得准备摸黑坐着；要想说话，就得咬着耳朵讲悄悄话。”难得的一笑使他脸色柔和了些。“明天早上我们多半都会觉得自己有点儿傻，可我宁可傻一回，也不愿错失会一会那黑衣人的机会。”

===Original===

She gave them supper, and after the men had helped clear away the remains of the meal, Tab, at her request, filled his pipe. Carver said he did not wish to smoke.
Conversation, for some reason, seemed to flag. They sat silently about the table, each busy with his own thoughts. Suddenly Ursula said:
“I am almost inclined to make a restricted confession to you, Mr. Carver. I don’t think I should never have dreamt of doing so if I had ever met you.”

===Chinese===

她给他们备了晚饭。饭后两个男人帮着收拾了残席，塔布应她之请，装上了一斗烟。卡弗说他不想抽。
不知怎的，谈话渐渐冷了下来。他们围桌默坐，各想各的心事。忽然乌苏拉开口道：
“卡弗先生，我几乎想向您做一番有保留的坦白。我想，要不是结识了您，我做梦也想不到会这样做。”

===Original===

“Restricted confessions are irritating things,” said Carver, “so I don’t think I should confess if I were you, Miss Ardfern, especially as I know what the restricted confession is all about.”
Her eyebrows rose.
“You know,” she said.
He nodded.
“You would tell me,” he said, “that you were in the habit of going to Trasmere’s house every night, to leave your jewels with him, though that wasn’t the object of your visit. You went there,” he said slowly and not looking at her, “to act as his secretary. All the letters that were sent away by Jesse Trasmere were typewritten by you on a portable machine, the make of the machine is a Cortona, its number is 29754, it has one key-cap missing, and the letter *r* is a little out of alignment.”

===Chinese===

“有保留的坦白最恼人。”卡弗说。“所以我要是您，阿德芬小姐，就不坦白了——何况我本来就知道这番有保留的坦白说的是什么。”
她的双眉扬了起来。
“您知道。”她说。
他点了点头。
“您要告诉我的是，”他说，“您惯于每天夜里到特拉斯米尔家去，把您的首饰寄放在他那里，虽然那并非您去的目的。您去那儿，”他说得很慢，眼睛不瞧她，“是给他当秘书。杰西·特拉斯米尔寄出去的所有信件，都是您在一架便携式打字机上打出来的；那机器的牌子是科托纳（Cortona），编号 29754，缺一枚键帽，字母 r 稍稍有些错位。”

===Original===

He enjoyed her consternation for a second and then went on:
“Perhaps you weren’t going to tell me that you and Yeh Ling, the proprietor of the Golden Roof, paid a visit to Mayfield the night I nearly caught you? No, I see that you weren’t. So we’ll restrict the confession to your peculiar occupation.”
Tab was speechless.
Ursula Ardfern the old man’s secretary! One of the most successful actresses in London acting as amanuensis to that crabbed misanthrope; it was unbelievable. Yet a glance at the girl’s face told him that Carver had only spoken the truth.

===Chinese===

他把她的那阵惊愕玩味了一秒钟，随即接下去说：
“也许您本不打算告诉我：在我差一点逮住您的那天夜里，您和‘金顶楼’的东家叶凌同去了一趟梅菲尔德？对，我看出来了，您原是不打算说的。那么，这番坦白就只限于您那桩特别的差事吧。”
塔布一时说不出话来。
乌苏拉·阿德芬，那老头的秘书！伦敦最走红的女演员之一，竟给那样一个乖僻的厌世者当抄写员，实在叫人难以置信。然而只消朝姑娘脸上看一眼，他便知道卡弗说的句句是实。

===Original===

“How do you know?” she gasped.
Carver smiled again.
“We have very clever people in the police,” he said drily. “You would never imagine it to read the newspapers. Clever old sixty-nine inch brains, eh, Tab?”
“I never said that you had a sixty-nine inch brain,” avowed Tab stoutly.
“But﻿—” interrupted the girl, and her voice was agitated, “do you know﻿—do you know anything else? Why we went that night?”

===Chinese===

“您是怎么知道的？”她倒抽一口气。
卡弗又笑了笑。
“我们警察里头有的是聪明人。”他干巴巴地说。“读报纸的人是决计想象不到的。聪明老到的六十九英寸大脑袋，呃，塔布？”
“我可从没说过你长着六十九英寸的脑子。”塔布理直气壮地声明。
“可是——”姑娘插口道，声音很激动，“您知道——您还知道别的吗？我们那晚为什么要去，您知道吗？”

===Original===

“You went to show Yeh Ling where the old man kept some of his secret documents, in the fake brick in the fireplace. You went hoping that in that box there were some papers which related to you and you were disappointed. The only thing I am in doubt about is this﻿—was Yeh Ling disappointed too?”
She shook her head.
“I wondered,” mused Carver. “Of course, I guessed that it was in the little lacquer box, and guessed also that the little lacquer box had a false bottom. Am I right?”
She shook her head again.
“No﻿—Yeh Ling thought it was there; the document he sought was in the brick-box.”
“You have the key of Mayfield,” said Carver. “I think you had better give it to me. Otherwise, you may be getting into serious trouble.”

===Chinese===

“您去，是要指给叶凌看，那老头把一部分密件藏在什么地方——藏在壁炉里那块假砖里。您去，是指望那盒子里有几份与您相关的文件，而您失望了。我唯一拿不准的是这一点——叶凌是否也失望了？”
她摇了摇头。
“我原先就纳闷。”卡弗沉吟道。“当然，我猜到东西在那只小漆盒里，还猜到那只小漆盒有个夹层底。我说得对不对？”
她又摇了摇头。
“不对——叶凌原以为东西在那儿；他要找的那份文件，就在那砖匣里。”
“您手里有梅菲尔德的钥匙。”卡弗说。“我看您最好交给我。否则，您可能会惹上大麻烦。”

===Original===

She went out of the room without a word, came back and handed him the small Yale key, which he glanced at and dropped into his pocket.
“If I were a writing man, which, thank heavens, I am not,” he said, “I should carry this story of the Trasmere murder, the ‘Mystery of the Three Keys.’ Here is one solved, and it wasn’t much of a mystery. There are two others. The third is the most difficult of all.”
“You mean the key that was found on the table in the vault?”
He nodded.
“Yes,” he said, and said no more.

===Chinese===

她一言不发地走出房去，回来时把那把小小的耶鲁锁钥匙（Yale key）递给他。他扫了一眼，便揣进了口袋。
“我要是个耍笔杆的——感谢老天，我不是——”他说，“我就该把特拉斯米尔这桩命案写成一个故事，题作《三把钥匙之谜》（the ‘Mystery of the Three Keys’）。瞧，一把已经解开，其实也算不上什么了不得的谜。还有两把。第三把是所有钥匙里最难的一把。”
“你是说保险库桌上发现的那把钥匙？”
他点了点头。
“是的。”他说，再没有多说一个字。

===Original===

In her discretion, Ursula asked no further questions.
Tab was looking at Carver with a new respect.
“Every day, Carver,” he said seriously, “you are getting nearer the fictional ideal of a real detective!”
Carver’s down-turned lips took an upward curve, and then he looked at his watch.
“Ten o’clock, Miss Ardfern,” he said with mock severity, and Ursula made a move to the door. “We must turn these lights out before you leave the room. Everything must be done in order, remembering that somewhere the Black Man is watching.”

===Chinese===

乌苏拉识趣，没有再问下去。
塔布望着卡弗，眼里添了几分新的敬意。
“卡弗，”他一本正经地说，“你一天比一天更接近小说里理想真侦探的典范了！”
卡弗撇着的嘴角向上弯了弯，随即看了看表。
“十点钟了，阿德芬小姐。”他佯作严厉地说。乌苏拉起身朝门口走去。“您离开这间屋子之前，我们必须先把这些灯全熄了。一切都要按部就班，别忘了，那黑衣人正在什么地方盯着呢。”

===Original===

She shivered.
It was Tab who blew out the light in the drawing-room.
“I think we may draw the curtains,” said Carver softly, and pulled back the heavy velvet hangings from the window.
It was a starlight night and there was just sufficient light in the sky to outline the gateway.
“This will do admirably,” he said, settling himself in the window-seat. “If you must smoke, Tab, don’t bring your pipe within sight of that gate.”

===Chinese===

她打了个寒噤。
客厅里的灯是塔布吹熄的。
“我想咱们可以把窗帘收起来了。”卡弗轻声说着，动手把窗前厚重的丝绒帷幔拉开。
那是个星光之夜，天上的光恰到好处，勾得出园门的轮廓。
“这位置再好不过了。”他在窗座上安顿下来，说道。“塔布，你要是非抽不可，千万别把烟斗拿到那扇园门望得见的地方来。”

===Original===

Tab groaned and laid his pipe upon the fender.
Ten minutes later Ursula came into the room.
“May I stay?” she whispered. “I have put out my bedroom light most artistically.”
They conversed in whispers for an hour, and Tab was beginning to feel sleepy when a hiss from Carver stopped him in the middle of a sentence. Looking out of the window he saw a dark figure by the gate. It was impossible to distinguish more than the outlines. It appeared to be a man of considerable height, but this might have been and probably was an illusion. It wore a broad-brimmed hat, presumably dark; more than this they could not see. They waited in silence as the gate opened and the figure stole noiselessly into the garden.

===Chinese===

塔布哼了一声，把烟斗搁在炉栏上。
十分钟后，乌苏拉走进屋来。
“我可以留下吗？”她悄声问。“我卧室的灯已经熄了，熄得艺术极了。”
他们低声絮语了一个钟头。塔布正开始犯困，卡弗忽然“嘘”了一声，把他一句话拦腰截断。往窗外望去，他看见园门口立着一条黑影。再多的便分辨不出，只有个轮廓。那似乎是个身量颇高的人，可这也可能是错觉——多半就是错觉。那影子戴着一顶宽檐帽，想必是深色的；此外他们什么也看不见。他们屏息静候，只见园门开了，那条黑影悄没声地溜进了园子。

===Original===

It was halfway to the door when another figure appeared. It came from nowhere, seeming to rise up from the ground; and then before the man in the wide-awake hat could draw back, the second man had flung himself upon him. The watchers sat paralyzed until Carter, jumping to his feet, ran out of the room, Tab close behind him.
When they flung open the door, both figures had disappeared. Carver sprang toward the gate and stumbled. His foot had struck a soft bulk which stretched across the garden path, he turned back, flashing an electric lamp upon the object. It was a man, and for a moment they did not see his face.
“Who are you?” Carver pulled the man over on his back. “Well, I’m﻿—”
The man at his feet was Yeh Ling!

===Chinese===

那条黑影走到离房门一半的路上，另一条人影冒了出来。它不知打哪儿来的，仿佛从地里钻出来一般；说时迟那时快，戴宽檐毡帽的人还没来得及退避，第二条汉子已经扑到了他身上。两个守望的人僵坐当场，直到卡弗霍地跳起身，冲出屋去，塔布紧随其后。
等他们一把推开门，两条人影都已不见了。卡弗朝园门扑去，脚下绊了一下。他的脚踢着一团软绵绵的东西，横在园中小径上。他回过身，把手电筒的光打到那东西上。是个人。有一刹那，他们没看清那人的脸。
“你是谁？”卡弗把那人翻了个仰面朝天。“好家伙，我——”
倒在他脚边的，竟是叶凌！

## XVIII / 十八

===Original===

The Chinaman was unconscious and Carver looked around for the second visitant. He rushed to the gate, the road was deserted. Flinging himself upon the roadway to secure an artificial skyline, he peered first in one direction and then in the other. Presently he saw his man running swiftly in the cover of the hedges and started in pursuit.

A hundred yards away from the house was a secondary road, and into this the runner turned. As Carver reached the corner he heard a motorcar engine and dimly saw the bulk of a large touring car retreating rapidly.

He came back to the house to find Yeh Ling sitting in Ursula’s room holding his head in his hands.

===Chinese===

那中国人昏了过去，卡弗四下张望，寻找第二个来客。他冲到园门口，路上空无一人。他扑倒在路面上，好压出一条人为的地平线，先朝一头望，又朝另一头望。不多时，便望见他那人在树篱的遮蔽下飞快奔逃，他当即拔腿追赶。
离屋子一百码开外有一条岔路，奔逃的人一拐弯进了岔路。待卡弗赶到路口，只听见汽车引擎的声响，影影绰绰望见一辆大型游览车的车身正迅速退远。
他回到屋里，只见叶凌坐在乌苏拉的房间里，双手抱着脑袋。

===Original===

“This is the second man; it isn’t the wide-awake gentleman,” said Carver. “Now, Yeh Ling, give an account of your actions. How are you feeling?”

“Pretty dizzy,” said Yeh Ling and to Tab’s surprise his tone was that of a cultured man, his English faultless.

He looked up at the girl reproachfully.

===Chinese===

“这是第二个人，不是那位戴宽檐毡帽的先生。”卡弗说。“好了，叶凌，把你今晚的行迹交代交代。你感觉怎么样？”
“晕得厉害。”叶凌说。令塔布诧异的是，他开口竟是有教养人的口吻，英语说得毫无瑕疵。
他抬起头，带着责备的神色望着姑娘。

===Original===

“You did not tell me these gentlemen were coming down, Miss Ardfern, when you wrote to me,” he said.

“I hadn’t any idea when I wrote, that they were coming, Yeh Ling,” she answered.

“If I had been here a little earlier I should have seen him,” he said. “As it was, I am afraid I have spoiled your evening, Mr. Carver.” His expressionless brown eyes looked up at the detective.

===Chinese===

“阿德芬小姐，您写信给我的时候，可没告诉我这几位先生要下来。”他说。
“我写信的时候实在不知道他们会来，叶凌。”她答道。
“我要是早到一会儿，就能见着他了。”他说。“事情弄成这样，我怕是把诸位这一晚给搅了，卡弗先生。”他那双毫无表情的棕色眼睛抬起来，望着侦探。

===Original===

“I see! You were on guard too, were you?” said Carver good-humouredly. “Yes, we seem to have made a mess of it between us. Did you see the man?”

“I didn’t see him,” said Yeh Ling, “but,” he added, “I felt him,” and he rubbed his head. “I think it must have been his fist. I did not notice any weapon.”

“You didn’t see his face?” persisted Carver.

“No, he had a beard of some kind. I felt it as my hands clutched at him. I am afraid I overestimated my strength,” he said apologetically to the girl, “yet there was a time when I was a star performer at Harvard, in the days when Chinese students were something of a curiosity.”

===Chinese===

“原来如此！你也在放哨，是吗？”卡弗和颜悦色地说。“是啊，看样子咱们俩合力把这事情弄砸了。你看见那人了吗？”
“我没看见他，”叶凌说，“不过，”他补了一句，“我摸着他了。”说着揉了揉脑袋。“我想那想必是他的拳头。我没觉出有什么家伙。”
“你没看清他的脸？”卡弗追问。
“没有。他有一种胡子。我的手抓向他时摸着了。恐怕我高估了自己的力气。”他带着歉意对姑娘说，“不过想当年，我在哈佛也是个明星选手——那还是中国学生颇算稀罕物的年代。”

===Original===

“Harvard?” said Tab in surprise. “Great Moses﻿—I thought you were a﻿—” he couldn’t very well finish his sentence.

But the other helped him.

“You thought I was a very ordinary Chink?” he said. “Possibly I am. I hope I am,” he said. “Certainly Miss Ardfern knew me when I was a very poor Chink! We lodged in the same house, she will remember, and she placed me under an eternal obligation by saving the life of my son.”

===Chinese===

“哈佛？”塔布吃了一惊。“我的老天爷——我还以为你是个——”这后半句他不太好说出口。
对方倒替他把话说完了。
“你以为我是个顶普通的中国佬？”他说。“也许我是。我希望我是。”他说。“阿德芬小姐认得我的时候，我可是个穷透了的中国佬！我们同住在一栋房子里，她会记得的。她救了我儿子的性命，叫我背上了永世难报的恩情。”

===Original===

Then Tab remembered the little Chinese boy Ursula had nursed when she herself was little more than a child. Remembering this, a great many things which had been obscure to him, became clear and understandable.

“I had no idea you would come tonight, Yeh Ling, but you begged me if I was in any kind of difficulty to let you know,” she said. “You shouldn’t have taken the trouble.”

===Chinese===

塔布这才想起乌苏拉当年照料的那个中国小男孩，那时她自己说来也还是个半大的孩子。想起了这一层，先前许多令他迷惑的事，忽然都变得清楚明白。
“我压根儿不知道你今晚会来，叶凌。是你求过我，说我若遇到什么难处，务必让你知道。”她说。“你不该费这个心。”

===Original===

“Events seem to prove that,” said the Chinaman drily, “I am merely being consistent, Miss Ardfern. You have been under my personal observation for seven years. Seven years, day and night, either I, or one of my servants have been watching you. You never went﻿—” he stopped and changed the conversation.

“Miss Ardfern never went to Mr. Trasmere’s house but you weren’t watching outside, that is what you were going to say, wasn’t it, Yeh Ling?” smiled Carver. “You need not be reticent, because I know all about it, and Miss Ardfern knows that I know.”

===Chinese===

“眼下的事似乎证明了这一层。”那中国人淡淡地说，“我不过是一以贯之罢了，阿德芬小姐。七年来，您一直在我亲自照看之下。七年，日日夜夜，不是我本人，便是我手下一个仆人在注视着您。您从来没有——”他顿住话头，把话岔了开去。
“阿德芬小姐去特拉斯米尔先生家，从没有一次您不在外头守望——你想说的就是这句吧，叶凌？”卡弗含笑说。“不必讳言，因为我全知道，阿德芬小姐也知道我知道。”

===Original===

“That was what I was going to say,” said the other. “I usually followed Miss Ardfern from the theatre to her hotel; from her hotel to Trasmere’s house, and home again when she had finished working.”

The reporter and detective exchanged glances. This, then, was the explanation of the mysterious Chinaman who had been seen by Mr. Stott’s servant waiting outside Mayfield smoking a cigar in the cold hours of the morning. It explained, also the appearance of the cyclist in the roadway that morning when the tyres of Ursula Ardfern’s car had burst and Tab had been on hand to render timely assistance.

===Chinese===

“我想说的正是这个。”对方说。“我通常从剧院一路跟着阿德芬小姐到她的饭店；再从饭店跟到特拉斯米尔宅，等她把事情办完，再跟着她回家。”
记者与侦探交换了一个眼色。原来如此——斯托特先生家的仆人曾在凌晨寒峭的时辰里，瞧见那神秘的中国人守在梅菲尔德宅子外抽雪茄，谜底便在这里。这也解释了那天早晨大路上出现的骑车人——彼时乌苏拉·阿德芬的汽车车胎爆了，塔布恰好在场，及时伸出援手。

===Original===

“I had no idea,” breathed the astonished girl, “is that true, Yeh Ling? Oh, how kind you have been.”

Tab saw tears in her eyes and wished that he, and not this uninteresting Chinaman, had been the person who excited her gratitude.

“Kindness is a relative term,” said Yeh Ling. He had brought his feet up on a chair and was rolling a cigarette; he had asked permission with his eyes and as Ursula nodded, he lit it with a quick flick of his fingers, a match having appeared, as it seemed, out of space, and carefully replaced the stalk in a matchbox. “Was it kindness that you saved the life of one who is to me the light of my eyes and the inspiration of my soul, if you will forgive what may seem to you, a writer, Mr. Holland, a piece of flowery orientalism, but which is to me the quintessence of sincerity.”

===Chinese===

“我一点儿也不知道。”惊讶的姑娘轻声说，“这是真的吗，叶凌？啊，你待我多好。”
塔布看见她眼里泛起泪光，只盼能激起她这般感激的人是自己，而不是这个乏味的中国人。
“好心是个相对的说法。”叶凌说。他已把两只脚抬上一把椅子，正卷着一支纸烟；他先用目光请示，乌苏拉点了点头，他便手指一弹点着了烟——一根火柴仿佛凭空出现在他指间——又把火柴杆仔细放回火柴盒。“您救了一条性命，而这条性命于我，是眼中的光、灵魂的启迪——请原谅，荷兰先生，这话在您这位作家听来，兴许是一段花哨的东方辞藻，可在我，这却是至诚的精髓。这难道算好心吗？”

===Original===

Then without preamble he told his story; a story which was only half-known to the girl.

“I was in this peculiar position,” he said, “that I was a rich man or a poor man, whichever way the great law of this country interprets an agreement I made with Shi Soh. Shi Soh you know as ‘Trasmere’ and that, of course, is his name. On the Amur River we called him Shi Soh. I came to this country many years ago and worked in the restaurant of which I am now proprietor. I do not mean the Golden Roof, but the little place in Reed Street. The man who owned it lost all his money at Fan-tan, and I bought it a bargain. You may wonder why a man of education, and the son of a great Clan should be here in this country, playing the humble part of waiter in a Chinese restaurant. I might tell you,” he said simply and without conscious humour, “that education in China, when it is applied to political objectives is not always popular, and I left China hurriedly. That, however, is all past. The Manchu has gone, the old Empress, the Daughter of Heaven is dead, and Li Hung is asleep on the Terraces of the Night.

===Chinese===

接着，他开门见山讲起自己的身世；这段身世，姑娘也只晓得一半。
“我当年正处在一个特别的境地，”他说，“我或者是富人，或者是穷人，全看这个国家的大法如何解释我与石索（Shi Soh）订下的一纸协议。石索，你们知道便是‘特拉斯米尔’，那当然就是他的名字。在阿穆尔河上，我们都叫他石索。多年前我来到这个国家，在一家餐馆里做工，如今那家餐馆已归我做东。我说的不是金顶楼，是里德街的那爿小店。原先的店主在番摊（Fan-tan）上把钱输了个精光，我贱价把它盘了下来。你们也许会奇怪，一个受过教育的人、大族的子弟，怎么会到这个国家来，在中国餐馆里屈就跑堂这样卑微的角色。我不妨告诉你们，”他说得平平淡淡，自己也并不觉得有什么俏皮，“在中国，学问一旦用于政治目的，就不总受人欢迎，我是匆匆离开中国的。不过，那都已过去。满人已去，老太后、那位‘天之女’死了，李鸿章（Li Hung）也在‘夜之台’上长眠了。

===Original===

“I was making slow progress when Mr. Trasmere came one night. I did not recognize him at first. When I knew him first he was a very strong, healthy man, with a reputation for being cruel to his employees. I have known him to burn men to death in order to make them reveal where they had hidden gold which they had stolen from the diggings. We talked of old times, and then he asked me if there was money to be made in the restaurant business. I told him there was, and that was the beginning of the partnership which lasted until the day of his death. Three-quarters of the profits of the Golden Roof was paid every Monday to Mr. Trasmere and that was our agreement. It was the only agreement that we had, except one which I myself wrote at his dictation and which placed on record this fact: that in the event of his dying, the whole of the property should come to me. It was signed by me with my ‘hong’ and by him with his ‘hong’ which he always carried in his pocket.”

“The ‘hong,’ ” interrupted Carver, “is a small ivory stamp with a Chinese character at the end. It is carried in a thin ivory case, rather like a pencil case, isn’t it?”

Yeh Ling nodded.

“I kept the document until a few days before his death, when he asked me to let him take it away with him to make a copy. It will be news to you, though not perhaps to you, Miss Ardfern, that Mr. Trasmere spoke and wrote Chinese with greater ease than I, who am almost an authority upon Mandarin. A few days later he was murdered. My only hope of saving myself from ruin was to find that agreement, which he had taken away in my little lacquer box.”

===Chinese===

“我的生意起色得很慢。一天夜里，特拉斯米尔先生来了。起初我没有认出他。我初识他的时候，他是个非常强壮健旺的人，以苛虐雇工出名。我知道他曾把人活活烧死，为的是逼他们说出偷藏的金子在何处——那金子是从矿场偷的。我们叙了叙旧，随后他问我，开餐馆这一行有没有钱可赚。我告诉他有，这便是我们合伙的开端，一直持续到他死的那一天。每逢星期一，金顶楼四分之三的利润都交到特拉斯米尔先生手里，这便是我们的协议。我们仅有这一份协议，只除了另一份——那一份是我亲自照他口授写下的，把一桩事实载明在案：他若身故，全部产业尽归我所有。我用我的‘行印’签的字，他也用他的‘行印’签的字，那枚印他素来揣在口袋里。”
“‘行印’，”卡弗插话道，“是一枚小小的象牙印章，末端刻着一个汉字。装在一只薄薄的象牙盒里，颇像铅笔盒，对不对？”
叶凌点了点头。
“那份文书我一直收着，直到他死前不几天，他要求带回去誊一份副本。有一件事诸位听了会觉新鲜——阿德芬小姐，您或许早已知道——特拉斯米尔先生说起中文、写起中文来比我还要流利，而我差不多算得上官话的权威。几天之后，他就遇害了。我免于倾家荡产的唯一指望，就是找回那份协议——他把它装在我那只小漆盒里带走了。”

===Original===

“But could they touch your restaurant? Are there any other documents in existence which would give Mr. Trasmere’s heir the right of interfering with you?”

Yeh Ling looked at him steadily.

“It does not need a document,” he said quietly. “We Chinese are peculiar people. If Mr. Lander came to me on his return from Italy and said﻿—‘Yeh Ling, this property is my uncle’s in which you have only a small share,’ I would reply, ‘that is true,’ and if the agreement which we two men had not signed was not discovered, I should make no effort at law to preserve my rights.”

===Chinese===

“可他们动得了你的餐馆吗？如今世上还有别的什么文书，能让特拉斯米尔先生的继承人来干预你吗？”
叶凌目不转睛地望着他。
“用不着文书。”他安安静静地说。“我们中国人是特别的族群。兰德先生从意大利回来，若是对我说——‘叶凌，这份产业是我伯父的，你只占很小的份子’，我会回答：‘这话不假。’倘若我们两人没有签字的那份协议未能寻回，我也决不会到法庭上费力保全我的权利。”

===Original===

And he meant it. Tab knew as he spoke, that he was telling the truth. He could only marvel that such an exalted code of honour could be held by a man who subconsciously, he regarded as of an inferior race and of an inferior civilisation.

“You found the agreement?”

“Yes, sir,” said Yeh Ling. “It had been taken out of the box in which I gave it to Mr. Trasmere and placed﻿—elsewehere. But I found it﻿—and other documents of no immediate interest. As to my coming here tonight﻿—apart from your letter, lady, I was anxious to meet the Black Man also. Yes. He has been watching me for many days. I am certain it is the same.” He made a little grimace and rubbed his bruised head. “I met him,” he said.

===Chinese===

他说的是真心话。他说话的时候，塔布就知道他说的是实话。他只能暗暗称奇：这样一套崇高的荣誉准则，竟会为一个人所持守——而此人在他的潜意识里，正是劣等种族、劣等文明的一员。
“那份协议你找到了？”
“找到了，先生。”叶凌说。“它已被人从我交给特拉斯米尔先生的那只盒子里取出，放到了——别处。可我找到了它——还找到几份别的文书，眼下无关宏旨。至于我今晚到这儿来——除了您那封信，夫人，我也急着要会一会那黑衣人。是的。他盯上我好些天了。我确信就是同一个人。”他微微做了个苦脸，揉了揉磕伤的脑袋。“我见着他了。”他说。

===Original===

Carver jotted down a few notes in his book and then putting the book away, he turned and faced the Chinaman squarely.

“Yeh Ling,” he said, “who murdered Jesse Trasmere?”

The Chinaman shook his head.

===Chinese===

卡弗在本子上草草记了几笔，随后把本子收起，转过身来，正面对着那中国人。
“叶凌，”他说，“是谁杀害了杰西·特拉斯米尔？”
那中国人摇了摇头。

===Original===

“I do not know,” he said simply. “To me it is amazing. There must be a secret passage that opens into the vault. I can think of no other way in which the murderer could have got in or out.”

“If there is a secret way,” said the detective grimly, “then it is the best kept secret I have known. It has certainly been kept a secret from the men who built the house and the vault, and the Clerk of the Works who was on the spot all the time it was being erected. No, Yeh Ling, you must get that idea out of your head. Either the man Brown or Walters is guilty. We shall know the method they employed when we get them.”

===Chinese===

“我不知道。”他坦然说。“在我看来，这事匪夷所思。一定有一条暗道通到保险库里。我想不出凶手还能有什么别的路子进去或出来。”
“倘若真有这么一条暗道，”侦探冷冷地说，“那它便是我平生所见保守得最好的秘密。盖这座宅子和保险库的工匠们固然一概不知，全程驻场的工程监工（Clerk of the Works）也肯定被瞒在了里头。不，叶凌，你还是趁早把这个念头从脑袋里撵出去。不是布朗便是沃尔特斯有罪。等抓到了他们，用的什么手法自然就知道了。”

===Original===

“Brown was not guilty,” said Yeh Ling quietly, “for I was with him when the murder was committed!”

They heard his pronouncement with astonishment, even the girl seemed surprised.

===Chinese===

“布朗没有罪。”叶凌平静地说，“凶案发生的时候，我正和他在一起！”
这话听得众人愕然，连姑娘似乎也吃了一惊。

===Original===

“Do you know what you are saying?”

“I know what I am saying, and I rather wish I hadn’t said it,” said the Chinaman with a quick smile. “Nevertheless, it is true. If the murder was committed on Saturday afternoon, then I certainly was with the man called Wellington Brown, but whom we called The Drinker or the Unemployed One, at that hour. It embarrasses me to say how or where, but it would embarrass me more if you were to ask me whether I know his whereabouts at the present moment. To that question I should answer: ‘No.’ ”

===Chinese===

“你可知道自己在说什么？”
“我知道自己在说什么，倒巴不得方才没说出口。”那中国人迅即一笑。“不过，这是实话。倘若凶案是星期六下午犯下的，那么在那个钟点，我必定正和那个名叫威灵顿·布朗的人在一起——也就是我们唤作‘酒鬼’、‘闲公’的那一位。怎样情形、在什么地方，说出来叫我难堪；可你们若问我此刻知不知道他的下落，那更叫我难堪。对那个问题，我的回答会是：‘不知道。’”

===Original===

“And you would lie,” said Carver quietly.

“I should lie,” was the calm answer. “Yet I tell you, Mr. Carver, that Wellington Brown was with me, under my eye, from half-past one o’clock in the afternoon of the Saturday on which Jesse Trasmere was killed until night.”

===Chinese===

“那你就得撒谎了。”卡弗轻声说。
“我就得撒谎。”回答从容不迫。“可我告诉您，卡弗先生，杰西·特拉斯米尔遇害的那个星期六，从下午一点半直到入夜，威灵顿·布朗一直在我跟前，在我眼皮底下。”

===Original===

Carver eyed him keenly.

“When he came to you,” he asked, “how was he dressed?”

The other shrugged his shoulders.

“Poorly. He has always been dressed poorly.”

===Chinese===

卡弗锐利地打量着他。
“他上你这儿来的时候，”他问，“穿的什么？”
对方耸了耸肩。
“寒酸。他一向穿得寒酸。”

===Original===

“Did he wear gloves?”

“No. He had no gloves. That was the first thing I noticed because he was, what do you call it in English﻿—fastidious to a degree. In the hottest days I have seen him wearing gloves. A shabby dandy! That is the expression I was seeking. I am sorry to disappoint you.”

“You haven’t disappointed me,” said Carver bitterly, “you have merely added another brick wall between me and my objective.”

===Chinese===

“他戴手套了吗？”
“没有。他没有手套。这是我头一件留意到的事，因为他这个人——英语里怎么说来着——爱洁成癖到了极点。顶热的天气我也见他戴着手套。一个寒酸的花花公子！对，我要找的就是这个说法。很抱歉叫您失望了。”
“你并没有叫我失望。”卡弗苦涩地说，“你不过是在我和目标之间又添了一堵砖墙。”

===Original===

Yeh Ling left soon after. He had bicycled down from town and cheerfully undertook the long return journey in preference to spending the remainder of the night at the cottage.

It was too late for Ursula to go to her hotel and they sat up all night, Carver playing an interminable game of Solitaire, whilst Tab and the girl walked about the garden in the growing light and talked oddly of incongruous things.

===Chinese===

过不多时，叶凌便告辞了。他是从城里骑自行车下来的，此刻宁愿快快活活蹬完那段漫长的归程，也不肯在石屋里消磨下半夜。
乌苏拉回饭店已嫌太晚，三人便彻夜未睡：卡弗打他那盘没完没了的单人纸牌，塔布和姑娘趁着渐亮的天色在园子里踱来踱去，说话也怪，净聊些全不相干的零碎事。

===Original===

As soon as it was light, Carver went out to find the place where the car had stood and to examine wheel-tracks. He gained little from his inspection, except that the tyres were new and that the car was a powerful one, which was hardly a discovery.

“The man who drove was not a skilled driver, or else he was very nervous. Halfway up the lane he nearly swerved into a ditch and came into collision with a telegraph pole, which must have damaged his mudguard severely. I found flakes of brand new enamel attaching to the damaged wood, so I guessed that the car also had not been long from the maker’s hands.”

Thus passed the second appearance of the Man In Black.

The third was to come in yet a more dramatic fashion.

===Chinese===

天一亮，卡弗就出门去找汽车停过的地点，查勘车辙。这一番查验下来所得无几，只看得出轮胎是新的、车子马力很足——这实在算不上什么发现。
“开车的不是个熟手，便是心里慌得很。在小巷上到半途，他险些一个甩身栽进沟里，还撞上了一根电线杆，挡泥板准给撞得不轻。我在磕坏的木头上发现粘着几片崭新的瓷漆，由此我猜这辆车出厂也没多久。”
黑衣人的第二次露面，就这样过去了。
第三次露面，将以更为戏剧性的方式到来。

## XX / 二十

===Original===

“There is the statement,” said Carver. “Not a line must be used; only the fact that the statement has been made can be published. What do you think of it?”
“It reads fairly honest to me,” said Tab, and the inspector nodded.
“It does to me also. I never had the slightest doubt in my mind that Walters, or Felling, was innocent. The references to Miss Ardfern’s visits are a little obscure, and in one sense rather remarkable, particularly the old man’s reference to the pin.”
“You are thinking of the pin we found in the corridor?” said Tab quickly.
Carver laughed softly.
“I was and I wasn’t,” he said. “The pin of which the old man spoke was obviously one of the jewels which were in the box, and as obviously he was taking an inventory of the jewel-case to see that everything was there.”

===Chinese===

“供词就在这儿。”卡弗说。“其中内容一个字也不许刊用；可以发表的，只有“供词已经作出”这一事实。你怎么看？”
“依我看，这供词读来相当诚实。”塔布说，探长点了点头。
“在我看也是。沃尔特斯——或者说费林——是清白的，这一点我心里从来不曾有过一丝怀疑。供词里提到阿德芬小姐来访的那几处，有点晦涩，从某种意义上说又颇不寻常，尤其是老头子提到别针的那一句。”
“你是不是在想我们那条走廊里发现的别针？”塔布急急地问。
卡弗浅浅一笑。
“也是，也不是。”他说。“老头子所说的那枚别针，显然是盒中首饰里的一件；同样显然，他这是在给首饰匣逐一清点，好核验东西一件不缺。”

===Original===

Tab was silent for a while.
“You mean that the jewels really belonged to Trasmere, that he loaned them to the girl and that she had to return them every night?” he asked quietly.
“There is no other explanation,” said Carver. “There is no other explanation, either, for her secretarial activities. Trasmere was in a score of enterprises and I have no doubt that he was the man who put up the money for Ursula Ardfern’s season. He was a shrewd old boy and probably had seen her acting. My own impression is that he made a fortune out of this girl﻿—”
“But why should she, a successful actress, consent to act as his midnight secretary? Why should she go on as though she were a slave to this man, instead of being, if your theory is correct, an earner of big money?”
Carver looked at him steadily.
“Because he knew something about Miss Ardfern, something that she did not wish should be known,” he said gently. “I am not suggesting it is anything discreditable to her,” he went on discreetly, detecting the cloud gathering on Tab’s face. “Some day she’ll tell us all about it I daresay. At present, it is unimportant.”

===Chinese===

塔布沉默了片刻。
“你的意思是，那些首饰本就是特拉斯米尔的，是他借给那姑娘的，她还得每夜归还？”他轻声问道。
“这再没有别的解释了。”卡弗说。“她那些充当秘书的活动，也同样再没有别的解释。特拉斯米尔插手的营生有几十桩，乌苏拉·阿德芬那个演出季的出资人就是他，这一点我毫不怀疑。他是个精明的老家伙，多半看过她演戏。我个人的印象是，他从这姑娘身上发了一笔财——”
“可她这个功成名就的女演员，为什么甘愿给他当半夜的秘书？为什么偏偏一直干下去，仿佛是这人的奴仆，而不是——倘若你的推论属实——一个挣大钱的人？”
卡弗定定地望着他。
“因为他知道阿德芬小姐的一桩隐情，一桩她不愿为人所知的隐情。”他温和地说。“我并不是说那是有损她名誉的事，”他察觉塔布脸上聚起阴云，便审慎地接着说，“总有一天她会把这一切告诉我们的，我敢说。眼下，这无关紧要。”

===Original===

He got up from his desk﻿—they were talking in his office﻿—and stretched himself.
“This concludes the day’s entertainment, gentlemen,” he said, “and if you are dissatisfied, your money will be returned to you at the doors.”
There were moments when Carver could be facetious.
“No, I’m not going home. I have a couple of hours’ work here. I shan’t be disturbed. Happily the station telephone is out of order. A tree fell across a line somewhere between here and the exchange. Remember, Tab, only the briefest notes of Walters’ arrest. Nothing about the charge, not a single item of his statement, beyond the fact that he has made one.”
Happily Jacques had gone home, or the news editor would have exploded at the meagre details with which Tab supplied his newspaper that night.

===Chinese===

他从办公桌后站起身——两人谈话是在他的办公室里——伸了个懒腰。
“诸位，本日演出到此结束。”他说，“若有不满，散场时门口退钱。”
卡弗也有那么爱打趣的时刻。
“不，我不回家。我在这儿还有两个钟头的活儿。不会有人打扰我。幸好局里的电话出了故障。从这儿到电话局之间的线路上，不知哪儿倒了一棵树。记住，塔布，沃尔特斯被捕一事只发最简短的报道。罪名一字不提，供词除“已作出”这一事实外，一个条目也不许登。”
幸而雅克已经回家，否则那天夜里塔布给自己报纸供稿的细节这样寒酸，这位新闻主编非气炸不可。

===Original===

He reached home at half-past eleven with a queer little ache at his heart. What was Ursula Ardfern’s secret? Why the mystery? Why must her mystery be interwoven with the greater and the more sordid mystery of the old man’s death?
As he pushed open the door he saw a telegram in the box which was common to the whole of the flats, once the entrance door was closed. It was for him and he tore open the envelope and unfolded its flimsy contents. It was handed in at Naples and was from Rex.
“Going on to Egypt. Quite recovered. Shall be back in a month.”
He smiled to himself and hoped that “quite recovered” referred to his youthful infatuation as well as his disordered nerves. He paused outside the door of his flat to find his key and as he did so, he thought he heard a sound. It may have come from one of the flats above, but he did not give it any importance, and inserting the key, he caught a momentary flash of light through the transom of his sitting-room. It was as if at the second he had opened the door the lights in the sitting-room had been extinguished.
It must have been an optical delusion, he thought, but the memory of the burglar came to him as he closed the door slowly behind him. For a second he hesitated, and then pushed open the closed door of the sitting-room. The first thing he noticed was that all the blinds were down, and he had left them up. He heard the sound of heavy breathing.

===Chinese===

十一点半他到了家，心口带着一点古怪的隐痛。乌苏拉·阿德芬的秘密究竟是什么？为何要这般神秘？她的秘密，又为何偏要同老头子之死那桩更大、更龌龊的谜团绞在一起？
他推开大门时，看见信箱格里有一封电报——临街大门一关，全楼住户便合用这只信箱。电报是发给他的；他撕开封套，展开那张薄薄的电报纸。电报从那不勒斯交发，发报人是雷克斯。
“续赴埃及（Egypt）。业已痊愈。一月后返回。”
他独自莞尔，但愿这“业已痊愈”既指他紊乱的神经，也指他那份少年人的痴恋。他在自家公寓门外停步寻钥匙，正在这时，他仿佛听见一声响动。声音也许来自楼上某套房间，他没把它当回事，把钥匙插进锁孔时，瞥见起居室的气窗里倏地闪过一道灯光。仿佛就在他开门的那一秒，起居室里的灯熄灭了。
想必是眼花，他想。可当他缓缓关上身后的门时，那个窃贼的影子浮上心头。他迟疑了一秒，随即推开掩着的起居室门。他注意到的头一件事，是百叶窗全都放下了，而他离开时分明是拉起的。他听见沉重的呼吸声。

===Original===

“Who’s there?” he asked and then reached out his hand for the switch.
Before his fingers could close upon the lever something struck him. He felt no pain, was conscious only of a terrific shock that brought him to his knees, incapable of thought or movement. Somebody pushed past him in the darkness. There was a slam of the flat door, a quick patter of feet on the stairs, and the street-door slammed.
Still Tab remained on his hands and knees, held there by his own invincible will. There was a trickle of warm blood running over his forehead and into the corner of his eye and the subsequent smart of it brought him at last to his senses. He got unsteadily to his feet and put on the lights.
It was a chair that had struck him; it lay overturned near the door. Tab felt gingerly at his forehead and then went in search of a mirror. The injury was a very slight one, the wound being superficial. He guessed that the chair must have caught against the wall and eased the blow, for one of the legs was broken and there was a long scratch on the wall. Mechanically he bathed his face, put a rough dressing on his forehead and then went back to the sitting-room to get a better idea of the confusion which reigned there, than he had been able to appreciate at first. Every drawer in his desk had been emptied. One which he kept locked and which contained his more private papers, had been forcibly broken open and the contents were scattered, some on the floor, a few on the desk. A little bureau by the wall had been treated with the same lack of courtesy and the floor was littered with its contents.

===Chinese===

“谁在那儿？”他问，随即伸手去够电灯开关。
手指还来不及扣住开关柄，什么东西砸中了他。他不觉得疼，只意识到一记天崩地裂般的重击，打得他双膝跪地，思想与动作一概不能。黑暗中有人从他身边挤了过去。公寓门砰的一声，楼梯上响起一串急促的脚步声，跟着临街大门轰然关上。
塔布仍旧手脚并用地趴在原地，全凭一股百折不回的意志撑着。一股温热的血从额头上淌下来，流进眼角，血随后的刺痛终于把他带回神志。他摇摇晃晃站起身，开了灯。
砸他的是一把椅子，翻倒在门边。塔布小心翼翼地摸了摸额头，去找镜子。伤势很轻，创口只破表皮。他猜椅子必是半路磕在墙上卸了力，因为一条椅腿断了，墙上留着一道长长的划痕。他机械地洗净脸，在额头上粗粗扎了一块敷料，便回到起居室，把满室狼藉看个比起初更清楚些。书桌的每一个抽屉都被倒空了。其中一只他一向上锁、存放较私密文件的抽屉被硬生生撬开，里面的东西撒得四处都是：一部分在地板上，几件在桌面上。靠墙一张小写字台也受了同样毫不客气的待遇，里面的东西扔了一地。

===Original===

He found the same in his bedroom; every drawer rummaged except his wardrobe, every box opened.
In Rex’s room the only thing that had been touched was the second trunk that the burglar had left on his previous visit. This was on the bed, opened, and its contents had been thrown around in confusion.
Tab’s gold watch and chain, which he had inadvertently left behind, was untouched. His cashbox had been wrenched open, but though the money had been emptied out, not a cent had been taken. Then he made a curious discovery. In one of the drawers of his desk he had a portfolio of photographs of himself which had been made a year before at the request of his many maiden-aunts. This had been removed and every photograph torn into four pieces. He found the debris with the other papers. It was the only wanton damage that the burglar had done. For what had he been searching? Tab puzzled his brains to remember the possession of something which might interest an outsider. What did Rex own that was worth all the trouble that this unknown visitor had taken?
He got on to the telephone and tried to reach Carver, and then remembered Carver had told him the station phone was out of order.

===Chinese===

卧室里的情形一模一样；除衣柜外每个抽屉都遭翻检，每只箱子都被打开。
雷克斯房里，被动过的只有上回窃贼光顾后剩下的那第二只衣箱。箱子搁在床上，敞开着，里面的东西被翻得七零八落，扔了一片。
塔布一时疏忽落在家里的金表和表链，原封未动。他的钱箱被硬撬开了，钱虽被倒了出来，却一分未取。接着他有了一个古怪的发现。书桌一只抽屉里放着他的一沓照片，是一年前应他那许多老姑娘姑母之请照的。这沓照片被抽了出来，每张都撕成四片。他在其余纸张中间找到了这些碎屑。这是窃贼干下的唯一一处无端毁损。他究竟在找什么？塔布绞尽脑汁，回想自己可有哪件东西会引起外人兴趣。雷克斯又有什么，值得这位不认识的访客如此大费周章？
他抓起电话想接通卡弗，随即想起卡弗说过局里的电话坏了。

===Original===

On the stroke of midnight Inspector Carver was tidying his desk, preparatory to leaving, when a dishevelled and damaged Tab made his appearance.
“Hullo,” said Carver, “been fighting?”
“The other fellow did the fighting,” said Tab. “Carver, I am going to sue the man who supplied us with our furniture. He said that the chairs were mahogany and they are only pine.”
“Sit down,” commanded the detective. “You seem to be a little out of your mind,” and then quickly, “you haven’t had another visit from your burglar?”
Tab nodded.

===Chinese===

午夜钟声正敲十二下，卡弗探长正收拾办公桌准备离开，衣冠不整、挂了彩的塔布现身了。
“哟，”卡弗说，“打架了？”
“打架的是那一位。”塔布说。“卡弗，我要去告那个供我们家具的家伙了。他说椅子是红木的，其实只是松木。”
“坐下。”这位侦探命令道。“你看起来有点神志失常。”随即又急急地问，“你的窃贼又来光顾了？”
塔布点了点头。

===Original===

“And what is more, I found him at home,” he said grimly, and related all that had happened in the flat.
“I’ll come along and see the damage, though I don’t think it will help us much,” said Carver slowly. “So he tore up your photographs, did he? That is rather interesting.”
“I guess he didn’t like me,” said Tab. “I have been trying to remember all the crooks I have annoyed, Carver. It can’t be young Harry Bolton, because he must be still in prison and it can’t be Lew Sorki because, if I remember rightly, he got religion in prison and he is now conducting a mission to the submerged. They are the only two people who expressed their intention of cutting short my young life.”

===Chinese===

“而且，我撞见他人还在家里。”他冷冷地说，随即将公寓里发生的一切讲述了一遍。
“我这就去看看损失，虽然我看帮不上多大忙。”卡弗缓缓地说。“这么说，他把你的照片撕了，是吧？这倒相当有意思。”
“我猜他不喜欢我。”塔布说。“我一直在回想我得罪过的那些歹徒，卡弗。不会是小哈里·博尔顿（Harry Bolton），因为他这会儿必定还在坐牢；也不会是卢·索尔基（Lew Sorki），因为——如果我没记错——他在牢里信了教，如今正在主持一个面向沉沦底层的布道会。表过态要把我年轻的生命缩短的，统共只有这俩人。”

===Original===

“It is neither of these.” Carver was emphatic on this point. “Tell me again, Tab, from the moment you opened the door to the moment you lost interest in the proceedings, just what happened. First, did you close the flat door behind you?”
“Yes,” said Tab surprised.
“And then you went into the sitting-room and he caught you a whack with the chair? There were no lights?”
“None whatever.”
“No light on the landing outside the flat door?” asked Carver eagerly.
“None.”

===Chinese===

“这两人都不是。”在这一点上卡弗语气斩钉截铁。“再给我讲一遍，塔布，从你开门那一刻，到你对眼前这桩事失去兴趣那一刻，到底发生了什么。第一，你随手关上身后的公寓门了吗？”
“关了。”塔布说，颇感意外。
“然后你就进了起居室，他抡椅子给了你狠狠一下？屋里没有灯？”
“一盏也没有。”
“公寓门外楼梯平台上也没有灯？”卡弗急切地问。
“没有。”

===Original===

“And he just rushed past you and was gone. You remember that very well, I suppose, although you were knocked out?”
“I remember his going and hearing the door slam,” said Tab wondering.
Carver was making notes on his blotting pad in that strange system of shorthand which nobody understood but Carver.
“Now Tab, think very carefully before you reply, was there anything in Lander’s box, any reference to his uncle, any document respecting his uncle, anything, indeed, that had to do, even remotely, with Trasmere, in that box of Lander’s? Because I am perfectly certain that there was the objective and that the search of your room was an afterthought. In fact, it is proved by the circumstances of the thief in your room when you arrived﻿—he had evidently left that search to the last.”

===Chinese===

“他就这么从你身边冲过去，人就没影了。尽管你被砸懵了，我想你对这个记得清清楚楚吧？”
“我记得他跑出去，也听得见门摔上的声音。”塔布说，心里纳罕。
卡弗在吸墨纸簿上做笔记，用的还是那套除他本人外无人能懂的怪异速记。
“听着，塔布，回答之前仔细想一想：兰德的箱子里可有任何东西，有任何一处提到他叔叔，有任何一份关于他叔叔的文件，乃至任何同特拉斯米尔沾得上一点边的东西，在那只兰德的箱子里？因为我完全确信，那才是此行的目标，搜你房间只是临时添的枝节。事实上，你到家时窃贼还在你房里，这情形便是明证——那一步搜查，他显然留到了最后。”

===Original===

Tab concentrated his mind upon Rex and all Rex’s belongings.
“No,” he confessed. “I can’t remember anything.”
Carver nodded.
“Very good,” he said rising, “and now we’ll go along and look at this trouble of yours. When did it happen?”
“About half-an-hour ago; maybe a little more,” he looked up at the clock, “yes, it was nearer an hour ago. I tried to get you on the phone﻿—”

===Chinese===

塔布凝神细想雷克斯，想遍雷克斯的全部家当。
“没有。”他只得承认，“我什么都想不起来。”
卡弗点了点头。
“很好。”他说着站起身，“现在我们去瞧瞧你这桩祸事。什么时候出的？”
“约莫半个钟头以前，兴许再长一点。”他抬头看了看时钟，“是啊，快一个钟头了。我想打电话找你来着——”

===Original===

“The machine is out of order, it always is out of order,” said the fatalistic Carver, “when there is real trouble around. In fact, if I obeyed my impulse, I should double the men on duty every time that phone falls down.”
They were in front of the station, and the cab that Carver had called was pulling up to the curb when another cab came dashing toward them, swerved to the sidewalk and stopped dead. Out of the cab’s interior tumbled a man who was sketchily attired, and whose pyjama coat showed where his shirt should have been. Mr. Stott had arrayed himself hurriedly and for once in his life, was careless of appearance.
He fell almost into Carver’s arms and his mouth opened and closed like a fish out of water. When he did speak his voice was a squeak.
“They’re at it again! They’re at it again!” he piped.

===Chinese===

“那机器坏了，它一向专挑时候坏。”听天由命的卡弗说，“真有乱子临头的时候它准坏。其实，我若依着自己的冲动，每逢那电话一瘫，我就该把当班的人手加倍。”
两人已到警察局门前，卡弗叫的出租汽车正靠向路边，另一辆出租汽车却朝他们疾驰而来，一甩弯上了人行道旁，戛然停死。车里跌出一个人，衣衫穿得潦草，衬衣的位置上露着的是睡衣上装。斯托特先生这是仓促间胡乱穿戴，平生头一遭顾不得仪表了。
他几乎跌进卡弗怀里，嘴巴一张一合，活像条离水的鱼。待到开口，嗓音尖细得像哨叫。
“他们又干上了！他们又干上了！”他尖声嚷道。

## XXI / 二十一

===Original===

Mr. John Stott had discovered to his gratification that the association of his name with the Trasmere case had enhanced, rather than detracted from his social standing. It is true that the newspapers, having long ceased to take the slightest interest in the murder, seemed oblivious to the part he had played, or the startling discovery which he had to his credit, but a more important circle of public opinion, that circle which met daily at Toby’s and discussed an expensive lunch and such matters of public interest as deserved attention, had applauded Mr. Stott’s decision to place in the hands of the police authorities the information which, up to that moment, had been confined to some twenty commercial gentlemen, their wives, their wives’ families, their servants, the servants of their wives’ families, the families of the servants, to say nothing of personal friends of all and each, their servants and attachments.

===Chinese===

约翰·斯托特先生颇为得意地发现，自己的名字同特拉斯米尔案扯上了关系，社会地位非但没有受损，反倒水涨船高。诚然，各家报纸早已不再对这桩凶案有丝毫兴致，似乎把他扮演过的角色、记在他名下的那桩惊人发现忘了个干净；但有一圈更要紧的舆论——就是每天在托比家碰头，一面品评着一顿价钱不菲的午餐、一面议论种种值得留意的公共事务的那一圈人——却对斯托特先生把情报交到警方手里的决定鼓掌称善；而在那之前，这份情报的知情范围不过二十来位商业界先生、他们的太太、太太们的娘家、太太们娘家的仆人、仆人们的家眷，更不必说这各色人等彼此的私交友人、友人的仆役和依附人口了。

===Original===

“So far as I am concerned, the matter is ended,” said Mr. Stott one day at Toby’s. “The police have behaved very shabbily. I have neither been thanked by the Commissioners nor their underlings.”
It is true that Mr. Stott had never expected thanks; it is true that he had expected a long term of imprisonment and had shivered every time the door bell rang, lest the callers be minions of the law, armed with warrants for his arrest. It is true that he had dismissed and reengaged Eline at least twice a day, for having dragged him into this unenviable position. He had expected, at least, the severest censure and condemnation from all those who had to do with the administration of the law, but he had never expected a vote of thanks.
“I told this fellow Carver,” said Mr. Stott, “and Carver, I might say, in passing, is one of those thickheaded, unimaginative men that have made the police force what it is, I told him, ‘Don’t ever expect any further information from me. If you do, you will be disappointed.’ ”

===Chinese===

“就我这方面来说，这件事已经了结了，”有一天，斯托特先生在托比家这样说。“警察办事可太不地道。警务长官们不曾向我道一声谢，他们手下的喽啰也不曾。”
不错，斯托特先生从未指望过道谢；不错，他倒是料想过要吃一场漫长的牢狱，因而门铃每响一回便哆嗦一回，生怕来客是执法的爪牙，怀里揣着拿他的拘票。不错，他因为艾琳把自己拖进这等不体面的境地，一天里至少两回把她辞退、又两回重新雇回。他至少还料想过，凡与执法有涉的人都要给他最严厉的申斥与责难，唯独不曾指望过一纸表决通过的谢辞。
“我跟卡弗那家伙说过的，”斯托特先生说，“卡弗么，不妨顺带提一句，正是那种脑袋不开窍、毫无想象力的角色，警察能落得今天这副样子，全亏了这号人。我跟他说：‘往后休想再从我这儿得到半点消息。你要是存了这个指望，准得失望。’”

===Original===

“What did Carver say?” asked one of his fascinated audience.
Mr. Stott shrugged his broad shoulders.
“What could Carver say?” he asked enigmatically, and nobody seemed to be able to supply an answer on the spur of the moment.
“In my opinion,” said Mr. Stott impressively, “if a business man had had charge of this case, we should have had the murderer by the heels and executed by this time!”

===Chinese===

“卡弗怎么说？”听得入神的听众里有一人问。
斯托特先生耸了耸宽阔的肩膀。
“卡弗还能说什么？”他高深莫测地问。一时之间，似乎谁也临场答不上来。
“依我之见，”斯托特先生说得好不郑重，“这桩案子若是交给一位生意人经手，凶手这会儿早给我们揪住脚脖子拿下了，人也已经正法了！”

===Original===

Here every one of the business men at the long table was in complete agreement. They shared a common faith that a man who can make money by selling sugar, or can acquire a competence by trading in margins, must necessarily be the best type of mind to tackle every problem, however obscure. It was their wont to shake their heads sadly at every mistake the administration made, and hypothesize the same situation if business men had been in control. It was accepted without dispute, that no government or government department came up to the business man’s standard of requirements.

===Chinese===

长桌边在座的生意人听了，个个深表赞同。他们共守一个信条：凡是卖糖卖得来钱、做保证金买卖挣得一份殷实家当的，必然是攻克一切难题——无论何等冷僻——的最佳头脑。当局每出一回岔子，他们照例要黯然摇头，假想同一局面若换生意人执掌又会怎样。任何政府、任何政府部门都够不上生意人的要求标准——这在他们已是无可争辩的公论。

===Original===

“They had their chance and they missed it,” said Mr. Stott, “when the Chinaman and the woman were in the house and I was holding them﻿—well I was practically holding them﻿—the police could have caught the whole gang if they had arrived in time. As it was, they allowed them to slip through their fingers. I hate to say so, though it has struck me since, that probably the police were in it!”
“In the house?” asked a foolish man.
“No,” snapped Mr. Stott, “in the plot, man! Anyway I’ve washed my hands of the whole affair.”

===Chinese===

“人家机会到了手又错过了，”斯托特先生说，“那中国人和那女人还在宅子里、我把他们扣住的那阵——唔，我实际上是把他们扣住了——警察若是来得及时，满可以把整伙人一网打尽。如今倒好，任凭人家从指缝里溜走。这话我本不愿出口，不过事后我忽然想到：警察说不定也掺在里头！”
“掺在宅子里？”一个蠢人问。
“不，”斯托特先生厉声道，“是掺在阴谋里，老兄！反正这整个烂摊子我已经洗手不干了。”

===Original===

Mr. Stott was in the habit of washing his hands of the whole affair twice a day, once at lunch and once after dinner. He washed his hands that night to his placid wife, not only of the Trasmere case, but of Eline’s tooth, and he washed them with such effect on Eline herself, that she reluctantly agreed to have the offending ivory extracted on the following morning.
She did this after making the most searching enquiries as to whether people told their most intimate secrets when they were under the effects of anæsthesis.

===Chinese===

斯托特先生素有习惯，一天两回把这整个烂摊子洗手不干：午餐时一回，晚饭后一回。当天夜里，他向自己那位恬静的太太洗了手，洗掉的不仅有特拉斯米尔案，还有艾琳那颗牙；这手洗到艾琳本人身上也这般灵验，害得她勉勉强强应承，第二天一早把那颗作怪的牙拔掉。
应承之前，她先做了一番极为透辟的打听：人受了麻醉的效力之后，会不会把自己最贴心的隐私说出来。

===Original===

Mr. Stott went up to bed at eleven o’clock, had a bath and got into his pyjamas. The night was warm﻿—indeed it was oppressively hot, and bed was very uninviting. He opened the French window of his room and stepping out on to the small balcony, he seated himself in a cane chair which occupied exactly one half of the balcony space, and enjoyed what little breeze there was. His partner having gone to bed to sleep, was behaving according to plan. Mr. Stott remained contemplating the deserted street and then crept downstairs and brought back his cigar-case.

===Chinese===

十一点钟，斯托特先生上楼就寝，洗过澡，换上睡衣。夜很暖和——简直是闷热难当，床铺了无吸引力。他推开卧室的落地长窗，跨上小阳台，在一张藤椅里坐下——那椅子不多不少，恰好占去阳台地面的一半——享用仅有的那一丝微风。他的太太已经上床睡去，正按部就班。斯托特先生对着空寂的街道凝望了一阵，随后蹑手蹑脚下楼，取回了雪茄烟盒。

===Original===

He smoked enjoyably for half-an-hour, watched the Manders return from the theatre and duly noted that Mr. Trammin, who lived three doors away from him, returned home in a state of intoxication and offered to fight the cabman for his fare. He saw old Pursuer’s car stop at “Flemington” and when these interests were exhausted, and his cigar was nearly through, he saw two men walking slowly toward him along the opposite sidewalk. He failed to identify them and had ceased to be interested in their movements, when they turned into the gateway of Mayfield.
Instantly Mr. Stott was alert. They might be police officers, only﻿—the sound of a large voice came to him.

===Chinese===

他怡然自得地抽了半个钟头，看着曼德斯夫妇（the Manders）从戏院回来；又如常记下一笔：住在隔第三个门的特拉明先生（Mr. Trammin）醉醺醺地回了家，还为车钱要跟马车夫干上一架。他瞧见老珀苏尔（Pursuer）的汽车停在“弗莱明顿”（Flemington）宅门前。待这些热闹一一阅毕，雪茄也抽得差不多了，他望见两个人沿着对面的人行道朝他缓缓走来。他认不出是谁，对他们的一举一动也失了兴味；恰在此时，两人一转折进了梅菲尔德的大门。
斯托特先生霎时警觉起来。他们兴许是警察——只是，一个洪亮的嗓门传到了他耳边。

===Original===

“Let me tell you, my dear fellow, that Wellington Brown is a good friend and a bad enemy!”
Mr. Stott nearly fainted. Wellington Brown! The man whose portrait had been in the newspapers; the man for whom the police were searching.
The other said something in a voice which did not reach the balcony.
“I am not threatening,” said the strident voice of Wellington Brown.
They walked up the steps to the door of Mayfield and disappeared from view.

===Chinese===

“告诉你，亲爱的伙计，威灵顿·布朗是好朋友，也是死对头！”
斯托特先生险些晕厥。威灵顿·布朗！报上登过相片的那个人；警察正在缉拿的那个人。
另一个人说了句什么，声音传不到阳台上来。
“我不是在威胁。”威灵顿·布朗那尖利的嗓音说。
两人踏上台阶，走到梅菲尔德的门前，从视野里消失了。

===Original===

Mr. Stott rose with knees that trembled. In the shortest space of time he was at the telephone.
