“I should have given it to you, old man,” he said impulsively, “what a wedding gift for a pal! But you shall have a house that is worthy of you, if I have to build the darned thing myself! As an architect I am a failure,” he went on, “my views are too eccentric. Poor old Stott swooned at the sight of some of my designs,” he chuckled to himself. “I’m not going to give up the attempt to carry my great idea into effect,” he told Tab at parting, “I shall see Yeh Ling at the earliest opportunity. I may be able to persuade him to sell.”

===Chinese===

“这事的前前后后，你可得原原本本讲给我听。”雷克斯说，手里忙着摆弄铁丝剪，“当然，我要做你的伴郎，一手操办这个小村子多少年没见识过的最气派的婚礼。”他絮絮叨叨说个没完，塔布乐得由他说去。
不多时，话题又回到房子上。雷克斯毫不掩饰自己的失望——那块理想的地段已经叫人占了。
“那地方要是在我手里，我就送给你了，老兄。”他一时冲动地说，“给哥们儿的结婚礼，多够意思！可你照样会有一座配得上你的房子，哪怕那该死的东西得我亲手来盖！当建筑师，我是个失败者。”他接着说，“我的见解太离经叛道。可怜的老斯托特，一看见我的几张设计图就晕了过去。”他自顾自地嘿嘿直笑。临别时他对塔布说：“把我的伟大构想付诸实施，这番尝试我不会放弃。我要尽早去见叶凌，兴许能劝得他肯卖。”

===Original===

Tab went down to Hertford the next afternoon and never had his bicycle moved more leisurely.
“I told Rex,” he blurted out and he saw her face fall.
“He wasn’t hurt,” said Tab, anxious to relieve her mind, “in fact, he behaved like a brick! Do you mind very much? My telling him, I mean?”
“No,” she said quietly. “He wasn’t hurt?”
Tab laughed.
“It may sound uncomplimentary to you but I am sure that Rex was only temporarily infatuated.”

===Chinese===

第二天下午，塔布骑车去赫特福德，从没有哪一回，他的车轱辘转得这般从容。
“我告诉雷克斯了。”他脱口而出，随即看见她的脸沉了下来。
“他没受什么伤。”塔布急着替她宽心，“说实在的，他表现得够朋友极了！你很介意吗？我是说，我把这事儿告诉了他？”
“不。”她轻声说，“他真的不难受？”
塔布笑了。
“这话听着也许对不住你，可我敢肯定，雷克斯不过是一时着了迷。”

===Original===

He saw a smile dawning and took her face between his hands.
“If I were Rex,” he said, “I should hate Tab Holland.”
“Rex is stronger minded,” she said. “Let us go into the garden. I have been thinking things out, and I feel that there is something that you ought to know, and the longer I put it off, the harder it will be to tell.”
He followed her, carrying an armful of cushions, arranged her chair, and sat upon its arm; and then, in the most unconcerned voice, holding no hint of the tremendous statement she was to make, she said:
“I killed Jesse Trasmere.”

===Chinese===

他看见一丝笑意浮上她的脸，便把她的脸捧在两手之间。
“我要是雷克斯，”他说，“准会恨透了塔布·荷兰。”
“雷克斯的意志坚定些。”她说，“咱们到园子里去吧。这些事我已经想了许久，我觉得有一件事你应当知道；拖得越久，就越难出口。”
他抱着满怀的靠垫跟在她身后，替她安顿好椅子，自己在椅子扶手上坐下；接着，她用一副再若无其事不过的声调——听不出半点她将要说出的那句话有丝毫惊天动地——开口道：
“杀死杰西·特拉斯米尔的，是我。”

## XXVII / 二十七

===Original===

He leapt to his feet.

“What?” he gasped.

“I killed Jesse Trasmere,” she repeated, “not directly, with my own hands, but I am responsible for his death, almost as assuredly as if I had shot him.” She caught his hand and held it. “How white you are! I was a brute to put it that way. In our profession we love these dramatic﻿—no I don’t mean that, Tab.”

===Chinese===

他霍地跳起身来。

“什么？”他倒抽一口气。

“杀死杰西·特拉斯米尔的，是我。”她重复道，“不是我亲手直接下的手，可他的死要算在我头上，差不多就像我亲手开枪打死了他一样确凿。”她一把抓住他的手，紧紧握住。“瞧你，脸都白了！我那样说法，真是太狠了。咱们干这一行的，就好这种戏剧性——不，我不是那个意思，塔布。”

===Original===

“Will you tell me what you do mean?”

She signalled him to sit on the footrest of the chair.

“I’ll tell you something, but I don’t think I’ll tell you any more about the murder,” she said, “and this is the something which you ought to know, and which I intended you should know. I had not the slightest intention of saying what I did. The spirit of tragedy seems to haunt me,” she said, staring straight ahead, “I was cradled in that atmosphere of violence and wickedness. I once told you, Tab, that I had been in service as a tweeny maid and I think you were startled. I went there from a public orphan’s home, an institution where little children are taught to be born old. Tab﻿—my mother was murdered, my father was hanged for her murder!”

There was no pain in her eyes, just a little hardness. He took both her hands in his and held them.

===Chinese===

“那你肯不肯告诉我，你到底是什么意思？”

她示意他在椅前的脚踏上坐下。

“有些事我要告诉你，但关于那桩谋杀，我想我不会再多讲了。”她说，“而这句是你应当知道的，也是我本来就打算让你知道的。我原先丝毫没有要说方才那话的意思。悲剧的精灵像是缠住了我不放。”她说着，两眼直望着前方，“我就是在那种暴戾与罪恶的空气里被摇大的。塔布，我曾对你说过，我帮过佣、做过打杂的小丫头，我记得你当时吃了一惊。我去帮佣，是从一所公立孤儿院出来的——那种教小小年纪的孩子未老先衰的机构。塔布——我母亲是被人谋杀的，我父亲因为那桩谋杀被绞死了！”

她眼中没有痛楚，只有一点点冷硬。他把她的双手都握进自己手里，攥着不放。

===Original===

“I don’t remember anything about it,” she went on, “my earliest recollections was the long dormitory where about forty little girls used to sleep, a very fat matron, and two iron-faced nurses, and the why and wherefore of my being at Parkingtons Institute only came to me late in life. One of the little girls had heard the matron tell the nurse, and I had to piece together the fact that I was an orphan by the act of my father and that after his trial and execution I had been sent to this home to be brought up and educated for the profession which all good little girls follow, and which had, as its supreme reward, an appointment as undercook. I was not so fortunate. I am afraid my cooking was rather vile, for when I came out of the Institute, it was to take a place as under-housemaid and general help in the kitchen of a great society leader, who spent thousands of pounds upon charity, but weighed the very bread that her servants ate. I had only been in this place for three months when Mr. Trasmere made his appearance. It was on a cold windy afternoon, I remember it as distinctly as though it were yesterday, when one of the parlourmaids came and said I was to go up into the drawing-room. I found Mr. Trasmere alone and I was rather frightened at the sight of him, for he did not speak, but sat with a little scowl on his face, taking me in from head to foot.

===Chinese===

“那件事我什么都不记得了。”她接着说，“我最早的回忆，是大约四十个小姑娘同睡一间的大宿舍、一位胖得滚圆的女总管，还有两个铁板着脸的保姆。至于我究竟为什么会进帕金顿学院（Parkingtons Institute），直到很大了才弄明白。有个小姑娘偷听到女总管对保姆讲的话，我这才一点一点拼出了实情：我是被父亲的所作所为变成了孤儿的；在他受审、处决之后，我被送进这所慈善学校，在这里受教养，学一门所有乖巧小姑娘都走的行当——而这门行当至高的褒奖，是熬上一个二厨的位子。我没有那份福气。只怕我烹调的手艺实在糟糕，因为我从学院出来，谋到的不过是一份二等女仆兼厨房打杂的差事，去侍奉一位执社交界牛耳的贵妇——她成千上万镑地捐给慈善事业，却要把仆人吃的面包一块块过秤。我在这家才做了三个月，特拉斯米尔先生露面了。那是一个又冷又刮风的下午，我记得清清楚楚，恍如昨日：一位客厅侍女来传话，叫我上楼到客厅去。客厅里只有特拉斯米尔先生一个人，我一见他就有些发怵，因为他一言不发，微微皱着眉坐在那儿，把我从头到脚打量。

===Original===

“I was between twelve and thirteen then, a sensitive child, to whom life, as it came to me, was a veritable hell. He asked me what my age was, and whether I was happy and I told him the truth. Apparently he had seen the authorities at the Institute, for I was allowed to go away with him, and he took me to a poor apartment house and placed me under the care of a woman who either owned or rented the house, and sublet the rooms furnished to the queerest lot of people I have ever seen congregated under one roof. Knowing him now much better, I am inclined to think that Mr. Trasmere owned the house himself and that the woman was his nominee. I did not see him again for nearly two months. I had a room to myself and he sent me school books to read and study and it was whilst I was here that I first met Yeh Ling, who, as I have told you, was a poor waiter at a Chinese restaurant.

“At the end of the two months, Mr. Trasmere came for me, and his coming was heralded by the arrival of a huge box of clothes, the like of which I had never seen, let alone worn. He left a message that I was to be dressed and ready to go with him, and that afternoon he called and took me down into the country, to a preparatory school which, after the Institute, was a heaven upon earth. On the way down, he told me he had heard about me from some friends of his, and he wanted to give me an education which would fit me to take the position which he had for me, and I was so overcome by his kindness that I cried all the way to our destination.

===Chinese===

“我那时十二三岁，是个敏感的孩子，就落到我头上的日子而言，那真是不折不扣的地狱。他问我的年纪，问我过得快活不快活，我照实说了。显然他先前已见过学院的主事人，因为我获准随他离去。他把我带进一栋寒酸的公寓楼，把我托付给一个女人——那房子不是她自置的便是她租下的，她把连家具的房间一间间分租出去，房客是我平生仅见挤在同一屋顶下最古怪的一伙人。如今我把他看得透多了，倒倾向于认为，那房子本就是特拉斯米尔先生自己的产业，那女人不过是替他顶名。此后将近两个月，我没有再见到他。我独自住一间屋，他差人送来课本供我研读。也正是在住那儿的日子里，我头一回遇见了叶凌——我对你说过，他那时不过是中国餐馆里一名穷侍者。

“两个月期满，特拉斯米尔先生来接我。他来之前，先到的是一只硕大的衣箱，满满一箱衣裳，那样的衣服我见都未曾见过，更不必说上身。他留下话，叫我穿戴齐整、备好随他动身。当天下午他来接了我，带我下乡，进了一所预备学校——比起学院来，那里简直是人间天堂。一路下乡，他告诉我，他从几位朋友那里听说过我，他要给我一种教育，好叫我胜任他为我预备的位置。他的好意叫我感动得无以自持，一路哭到了目的地。

===Original===

“The three years that I spent at St. Helen’s seem, even now, like a beautiful dream. I was happy, I made many friends, and my whole outlook on life changed. The year I left, Mr. Trasmere came down to our Commemoration and saw me acting in a play which the school dramatic society had produced, and from what he saw, was evolved this extraordinary arrangement. Knowing what I do, I know he was not wholly disinterested. It was his practice to take up projects and finance likely people. Once he told me that he had intended settling in this country and living the life of a gentleman﻿—to use his own words﻿—but that he was so unutterably bored, that to give himself an interest, he took up the most extraordinary of schemes.

“Do you know at one time he financed twelve tearooms and collected his share of the takings every day? Do you know that he was behind three doctors and took his profits from each? He was Yeh Ling’s backer, and in time he came to be mine. I was with him six months, acting as his secretary in a tiny office he hired for the purpose, and to which he never came until five o’clock in the afternoon.

===Chinese===

“我在圣海伦（St. Helen’s）度过的那三年，直到如今回想起来，仍像一场美梦。我很快乐，交了许多朋友，整个人生观都为之一变。离校那一年，特拉斯米尔先生下乡来参加我们的校庆典礼，看了我在学校戏剧社排演的一出戏里扮演角色，由着他所见到的这一幕，后来便演化出了那套异乎寻常的安排。如今我既知道底细，便明白他并非全然无私。他素来的做派，便是物色门路、资助有出息的人。有一回他对我说，他本打算在这个国家定居，过——用他自己的话来说——绅士的日子；可他实在无聊得说不出口，为了给自己添个兴致，便揽起了顶顶离奇的种种营生。

“你可知道，他一度注资开了十二家茶室，每天亲自去收他名下那一份进项？你可知道他给三位医生当后台，从每一位身上分取利润？他是叶凌的出资人，到后来也成了我的。我在他手下做了六个月，在他专为这事租下的一间小办公室里当秘书——那间办公室，他总要到下午五点才肯光临。

===Original===

“Then it was, that he suggested that I should go on to the stage and sent me away with a touring company. Of course, he was financially interested in it and it was my duty to send him a daily return showing the amount of money we took every night. On Saturdays I paid the salaries and expenses and remitted the remainder to him. When the tour was finished I came back to town to find that he had already made arrangements in his furtive, secretive way, to start a season with me as the principal attraction. My salary! You would laugh if I told you. It was hardly enough to keep body and soul together, only, as an excuse for his parsimony, he agreed that he would pay me one half of the profits over a certain amount.

“To my astonishment, as well as to his, I became not only a respectable success, but a great financial success. The profits on my seasons were enormous, they exceeded to an incredible extent the amount he had fixed. And, of course, he paid. Jesse Trasmere’s word was more than his bond. It was his oath.

===Chinese===

“就是从那时起，他提议我登台演戏，把我送进一个巡回剧团。当然，他在里头是有钱份的，我的本分便是每天给他寄一份日报单，开明我们每晚进账的钱数。每到星期六，我发完薪水、付清开销，把余款汇给他。巡演结束回到城里，我才发现他已用他那偷偷摸摸、遮遮掩掩的做派安排妥当，要以我为头牌，开演一个演出季。我的薪水！说出来你要笑话的。那点钱连糊口都难。只不过，为了给他那份吝啬寻个托词，他允诺超过一定数目的利润，分我一半。

“叫我吃惊、也叫他吃惊的是，我不但成了个像模像样的成功，还成了棵大大的摇钱树。我各演出季的利润大得惊人，远远超出了他划定的那个数目，超得出奇。当然，他付了。杰西·特拉斯米尔说出口的话，比债券还要硬。那就是他的誓词。

===Original===

“His code was the code of the Chinese business man. When you know what that means, Tab, you will realise how very punctilious he was in such matters. He made exactly the same arrangements with Yeh Ling. There was the curious bond which bound us together, Yeh Ling and I﻿—our shares were enormously in excess of his estimate. But he paid loyally. Between him and me there was never an agreement of any kind. In the case of Yeh Ling there was an agreement, as you know. But the most bizarre aspect of my success was that I was compelled to continue as his secretary. Every night when the theatre was closed, I motored to his house, dealt with his correspondence and answered his letters. Sometimes I was so weary after a heavy evening, that I could scarcely drag myself up the steps of Mayfield. But Jesse was inexorable. He never let up on any bargain he made, any more than he evaded the terms of any agreement which proved adverse to him.

“When I began to get talked about, he insisted upon my making a ‘show’ as he called it and brought a lot of jewels which he told me should be mine at his death. Whether he bought them﻿—they did not look new to me﻿—or whether he acquired them in one of those deals of his which nobody knew anything about, I am unable to say with certainty. They were beautiful﻿—but they were not mine until his death. Every night I dined with him at Yeh Ling’s, and he handed to me the jewel-case which he had taken from his bag, and every night I carried those jewels back to the house and gave them into his care.”

===Chinese===

“他的规矩，是中国生意人的规矩。等你懂得这话的分量，塔布，你便会明白他在这些事上是何等一丝不苟。他同叶凌订下的正是如出一辙的章程。我和叶凌——把我们拴在一处的，正是那条古怪的纽带：我们分得的份子，都大大超出了他的预估。但他照付不误，毫不含糊。他与我之间，从来不曾有过任何形式的契约；叶凌那头是有协议的，这你知道。可我的成功最古怪的一面在于：我竟不得不继续当他的秘书。每晚戏园子落了幕，我便坐汽车去他宅里，处理他的往来信函，替他回信。有时一个繁重的夜晚下来，我累得几乎爬不上梅菲尔德的台阶。可杰西说一不二。他做下的交易从不含糊放过，同样，哪份契约的条款到头来于他不利，他也从不躲避。

“等我渐渐有了名声，他非要我——用他的话说——‘摆一摆场面’，还弄来一大批首饰，告诉我说，他百年之后这些都归我。这些首饰是他买的——依我看不像新货——还是从他那些没人知道底细的交易里弄来的，我说不准。首饰确乎美——可在他死之前，终究不是我的。每天晚上，我都在叶凌的金顶楼陪他用饭，他把从提包里取出的首饰匣递到我手上；每晚我把这些首饰带回梅菲尔德，交还他收管。”

===Original===

“Did the old man ever tell you how he came to seek you out?”

She nodded and a faint smile came and went.

“Jesse Trasmere was very frank. That was one of his charms. He told me he knew my deplorable history and he wanted somebody about whom he knew a few discreditable facts! He said that in almost those identical words: ‘You’ll have to go along as I want you,’ he said, ‘and the higher you get, and the more successful you become, the less you will want the news published that your father was a murderer.’ And yet, curiously enough, he never objected to my taking my own name, for Ardfern is my name, for professional purposes. I don’t suppose anybody at that dingy Institute associates me with the skinny little girl who used to scrub and peel and toil at uninteresting lessons from morning until night.”

===Chinese===

“老头子可曾告诉过你，他当年是怎么把你寻出来的？”

她点点头，一丝浅浅的笑意在脸上一掠而过。

“杰西·特拉斯米尔为人非常坦率，这是他的动人之处。他告诉我，他知道我那段可悲的身世，他要的，正是一个让他握有几件见不得人之事的人！他差不多就是用这一模一样的话说的：‘你得照我想要的样子走下去，’他说，‘而且你爬得越高、越有出息，就越不愿意让人家把新闻登出去，说你父亲是个杀人凶手。’然而说来也怪，他从不反对我用本来的姓——阿德芬本就是我的姓，拿来做艺名罢了。我想，那所阴沉沉的学院里，谁也不会把我同当年那个干瘦的小姑娘联想到一处——那孩子从前从早到晚又洗又擦、削皮剁菜，埋头苦熬那些索然无味的功课。

===Original===

“What was your father?” asked Tab with an effort, for he expected that any reference to her parents must still wound her.

To his surprise she answered readily.

“He was an actor,” she said, “and I think he was a clever actor until he took to drink. It was in drink that he murdered my mother. That much I learnt at the home﻿—I have not troubled to enquire since. What are you thinking about, Tab?”

His forehead was knit.

===Chinese===

“你父亲是干什么的？”塔布费了些劲才问出口，他还只当但凡提到她父母，必定仍会刺痛她。

出乎他的意料，她答得爽爽快快。

“他是个演员。”她说，“我想，在沾上杯中之物以前，他是个挺高明的演员。他是醉后杀死我母亲的。这一点我是在孤儿院里听来的——后来我也懒得费心去打听。你在想什么呢，塔布？”

他眉头紧锁。

===Original===

“I am trying to recall the execution of any person named Ardfern in the last twenty years, I know them all by name,” he said slowly. “Have you a telephone?”

She nodded.

In three minutes Tab was talking to the news editor of the *Megaphone*.

“Jacques,” he said, “I want some information. Do you remember any person named Ardfern being executed for murder in the last﻿—” he looked round at the girl﻿—“seventeen or eighteen years?”

===Chinese===

“我在回想，过去二十年里处决过的人里头，有没有姓阿德芬的——这些人我个个叫得出名姓。”他慢慢地说，“你这儿有电话吗？”

她点点头。

不到三分钟，塔布已经同《扩音器报》的新闻主编通上了话。

“雅克，”他说，“我要打听一件事。你可记得，过去——”他回头望了望那姑娘——“十七八年里，有姓阿德芬的人因谋杀罪被处决过？”

===Original===

“No,” was the instant reply. “There was a man named Ardfern against whom a coroner’s verdict of manslaughter was returned but he skipped the country.”

“What was his first name?” asked Tab eagerly.

“I am not sure that it was Francis or Robert. No, it was Willard﻿—Willard Ardfern. I remember there were two ’ards’ in it,” said the information bureau.

“In what town was this crime committed?”

Jacques answered without hesitation, giving the name of a small country town that Tab knew well.

===Chinese===

“没有。”回答来得飞快。“倒是有过一个姓阿德芬的，验尸陪审团作出了过失杀人的裁决，可他逃出国去了。”

“他叫什么名字？”塔布急切地问。

“我记不准是弗朗西斯还是罗伯特。不对，是威拉德（Willard）——威拉德·阿德芬。我记得这名字里有两个‘德’字。”这位“活字典”说道。

“这桩案子是在哪个镇上犯下的？”

雅克毫不迟疑地作了回答，报出一座乡下小镇的名字，那地方塔布熟得很。

===Original===

He hung up the receiver and turned to the girl.

“What was your father’s name?” he asked.

“Willard,” she replied without hesitation.

“Phew!” whistled Tab, and wiped his streaming forehead. “Your father was not hanged.”

===Chinese===

他挂上听筒，转过身来看着姑娘。

“你父亲叫什么名字？”他问。

“威拉德。”她毫不迟疑地答道。

“嚯！”塔布打了个呼哨，抹了一把汗涔涔的额头。“你父亲没有被绞死。”

===Original===

He saw her go red and white.

“Are you sure?” she asked.

“Perfectly sure. Old Jacques never makes a mistake. Besides which, he had the name pat when I asked him. Willard Ardfern. He was indicted for manslaughter. I fear that your unhappy mother died of his violence but Willard Ardfern himself left the country and was never arrested or tried.”

===Chinese===

他看着她的脸红了又白。

“你敢肯定？”她问。

“千真万确。老雅克从来不出错。何况我一开口问，他张口就把名字报了出来。威拉德·阿德芬。他被控的是过失杀人。只怕你那不幸的母亲确是死于他的暴行，可威拉德·阿德芬本人逃离了这个国家，从未被捕，也从未受过审。”

===Original===

His arm went round her in support, she had gone suddenly white and ill-looking.

“Thank God,” she whispered. “That seemed﻿—worse﻿—than killing my poor mother. Oh Tab, it has been such a nightmare to me! Such a dreadful, dreadful weight. You can’t know how I felt about it.”

“Was it that?﻿—” he hesitated, “something I had said that made you feel bad when we talked of Mr. Trasmere’s will?”

She looked at him steadily but did not give an answer.

===Chinese===

他伸手揽住她，给她一点支撑——她霎时间变得面无血色，一脸病容。

“感谢上帝。”她喃喃道，“那——比杀死我可怜的母亲——还要可怕。噢，塔布，这些年来，它一直是我的一场噩梦！那样一个可怕的、可怕的重负。你不会知道，我心里是什么滋味。”

“是因为那个吗——”他迟疑着说，“我们谈起特拉斯米尔先生遗嘱的时候，是我说的什么话让你难受了？”

她定定地望着他，却没有作答。

===Original===

“I used to hate this nightly borrowing of jewels,” she went back to her relations with Jesse Trasmere. “I had enough money to buy my own, though I have no particular leaning toward jewelry, but old Trasmere would not hear of it. Any movement towards my independence he checked ruthlessly,” she stopped suddenly and her mouth made a little “O” of surprise. “I wonder if he heard﻿—in China?” she asked. “Yes, that is it! He must have met my father. That is how he came to know about me! I am sure Yeh Ling knows, because Mr. Trasmere had a habit of making elaborate notes﻿—I wonder,” she said speaking to herself. Impulsively she threw out her hands and caught his. “Tab, the night you came into my dressing-room I felt instinctively that you were a factor in my life. I could never have dreamt how big a part you were going to be.”

For once in his life Tab could not think of an appropriate rejoinder.

===Chinese===

“我从前恨透了这种每夜借还首饰的营生。”她把话头又拉回到她同杰西·特拉斯米尔的关系上。“我的钱足够自己买首饰——虽说我对珠宝本来没有特别的偏好——可老特拉斯米尔连听都不要听。我但凡有半点自立的动向，都被他毫不留情地掐灭。”她蓦地收住话头，嘴巴惊讶地张成了一个小小的“O”形。“他会不会——在中国——就听说过了？”她问。“对了，正是这样！他必定遇见过我父亲。他就是这样知道我底细的！我敢肯定叶凌知道，因为特拉斯米尔先生有个习惯，爱写一份份细致入微的札记——我不免想……”她自言自语道。她情不自禁地伸出双手，抓住了他的手。“塔布，那天晚上你走进我的化妆间，我本能地觉出，你是我生命里的一个变数。可我做梦也不曾想到，你会成为多么重大的一个角色。”

平生头一回，塔布想不出一句得体的回话。

## XXVIII / 二十八

===Original===

There came to police headquarters a tall, ruddy-skinned man of middle age. He wore a suit which was evidently not made for him, and he seemed a little depressed by his surroundings.
“I have an appointment with Inspector Carver,” he said and passed a letter across the desk to the police clerk, who read it and nodded.
“Inspector Carver is expecting you,” he said and called a messenger.

===Chinese===

一个身材高大、面色红润的中年男子来到了警察总部。他身上那套西装分明不是照他的身量做的，周遭的环境似乎又让他有些局促。
“我同卡弗探长有约。”他说着，把一封信从柜面上递给警察局文书；文书读罢，点了点头。
“卡弗探长正候着您。”他说着，唤来一名传达。

===Original===

Carver looked round as the door opened and viewed his caller with a speculative eye. Then he jumped up.
“Of course!” he said. “Sit down please.”
“I hope﻿—” began the man, “there isn’t going to be any trouble.”
“Not for you,” said Carver, “but I rather fancy there is trouble coming for somebody.”
The messenger closed the door and left them together.

===Chinese===

房门开处，卡弗回过头来，用一副审度的目光打量来客。随即他一跃而起。
“哎呀，果然是您！”他说，“请坐。”
“我希望——”那人开口道，“但愿别出什么乱子才好。”
“对您不会，”卡弗说，“不过我倒是觉得，有人要大祸临头了。”
传达掩上房门，留下他们两人。

===Original===

Half an hour later Inspector Carver telephoned for the office stenographer, and when the harassed man with the fresh face and ill-fitting clothes left the police office after a three-hour examination, Inspector Carver had material for much cogitation.
Tab called in the ordinary way of duty and they discussed the latest tragedy, but never once did Inspector Carver make reference to his visitor of the morning. That was his secret and too precious a one, in the circumstances, to breathe to a soul.

===Chinese===

半个钟头后，卡弗探长打电话叫来局里的速记员。等这位被折腾得疲惫不堪、面色红润、衣衫不合身的男子熬过三个钟头的盘问、走出警察局时，卡弗探长手里已经攥下了够他反复琢磨的许多材料。
塔布照常因公来访，两人谈起最近那桩惨案，可卡弗探长对上午那位来客连提都没提一句。那是他的秘密，在眼下这种情势下太珍贵了，不能向任何人吐露半个字。

===Original===

He drove that afternoon to the detention prison where Walters was awaiting trial and had a long talk with him. Yeh Ling was in his parlour, halfway through his long weekly letter to his son, when Inspector Carver was announced. He put down his brush and gazed impassively upon the servant who had brought in the inspector’s card.
“Is this man alone?” he asked.
“Yes, Yeh Ling. There is no man with him.”
Yeh Ling tapped his white teeth with his well-manicured nails.
“Come,” he said laconically and there was something in Carver’s face which told Yeh Ling all that he wanted to know. But there was a fight to be made yet and he was not without hope that this matter of the Trasmere murder and the tragedy that had followed, would be settled in a manner more consonant with his keen sense of obligation.

===Chinese===

当天下午，他驱车前往还押监狱——沃尔特斯正在那里候审——同他长谈了一番。禀报卡弗探长到访时，叶凌正坐在自己的客厅里，给他儿子的每周一封的长信才写到一半。他放下毛笔，木然望着那个递进探长名片的仆人。
“这人是独自来的？”他问。
“是的，叶凌。他没有带别人来。”
叶凌用修剪得整整齐齐的指甲，轻轻叩着雪白的牙齿。
“带他进来。”他言简意赅地说。卡弗脸上的某种神色，已把叶凌想知道的一切都告诉了他。不过，还有一场硬仗要打；对于特拉斯米尔谋杀案连同随后那桩惨案，他并非没有指望——指望事情最终能以一种更合乎他那强烈义务感的方式了结。

===Original===

The inspector did not come to the point at once. He accepted a cigar that the Chinaman offered to him, spoke jocularly of Yeh Ling’s letter writing, asked a question or two about Ursula Ardfern, and at last hinted at the object of his visit.
“Yeh Ling,” he said, “I think the Trasmere case is coming to a solution.”
Yeh Ling’s eyelids did not so much as flicker.
“In fact,” said the inspector, carefully examining the ash of his cigar, “I have found the murderer.”
Yeh Ling said nothing.
“I need very little confirmatory evidence to put the man who killed Jesse Trasmere on the trap,” Carver went on.

===Chinese===

探长没有一上来就直奔正题。他接过这位中国人敬上的雪茄，就叶凌写信的事说笑了几句，问了一两个关于乌苏拉·阿德芬的问题，最后才隐隐点出来意。
“叶凌，”他说，“依我看，特拉斯米尔案快要水落石出了。”
叶凌的眼皮连一下都没有颤动。
“事实上，”探长说着，仔细端详着自己雪茄上的烟灰，“凶手我已经找到了。”
叶凌一言不发。
“只消再添上极少的一点补强证据，就能把杀害杰西·特拉斯米尔的人送上绞架了。”卡弗接着说。

===Original===

“And you have come to me to furnish that evidence,” said Yeh Ling with a touch of irony.
Carver shook his head and smiled.
“I don’t know﻿—I didn’t think you would,” he said, and then almost sharply: “Where are the documents you took from the Trasmere house the night you went there with Miss Ardfern?”
The Chinaman got up without hesitation, unlocked a small safe in the corner of the room and brought out a thick packet of papers.
“They are all there?” asked Carver, shooting a suspicious glance at the other.
“All except two,” was the cool reply, “one of which has reference to my interest in the Golden Roof and that is with my lawyer﻿—”

===Chinese===

“于是您就上我这儿来，要我供上这些证据。”叶凌的话里带一丝讥诮。
卡弗摇了摇头，莞尔一笑。
“这我说不准——我本来也没指望您肯。”他说，随即语气近乎凌厉，“那天夜里您同阿德芬小姐到特拉斯米尔宅子里取走的那些文件，如今都在哪里？”
这位中国人毫不迟疑地起身，打开房间角落里一只小保险柜，取出一厚沓文件。
“都在这里头了？”卡弗问，朝对方射去一道狐疑的目光。
“只少了两份，”回答得冷冷静静，“其中一份关乎我在金顶楼的权益，在我律师手里——”

===Original===

“And the other?” asked the detective.
“That deals with matters of a sacred nature,” said Yeh Ling in that precise English that sounded almost affected.
Carver bit his lip.
“You know that this is the document I particularly want?” he asked.
“I guessed that,” was the reply. “Nevertheless, Mr. Carver, I cannot give it to you, and if you know so much,” for a second a ghost of a smile lit his brown eyes, “then you will know why it is not forthcoming.”

===Chinese===

“另一份呢？”这位侦探问。
“那一份涉及一些神圣性质的事务。”叶凌用他那字斟句酌、几乎听来做作的英语说。
卡弗咬了咬嘴唇。
“您知道，我特别想要的就是这一份吧？”他问。
“这个我猜到了。”对方答道，“然而，卡弗先生，我不能把它交给您。您既已知道这么多——”刹那间，一抹笑影点亮了他褐色的眼睛，“——那您也该知道，它为什么拿不出来。”

===Original===

“Does Miss Ardfern know?”
Yeh Ling shook his head.
“She is the one person who must not know,” he said emphatically. “If it were not for her﻿—” he shrugged his shoulders, “you might see it.”
Carver knew that he was opposed by a will greater than his own and that neither threats nor promises would move this impassive man from the attitude he had taken up.

===Chinese===

“阿德芬小姐知道吗？”
叶凌摇了摇头。
“她正是唯一不可以知道的人。”他加重语气说，“若不是为了她——”他耸了耸肩，“您原本是可以看的。”
卡弗明白，自己面对的是一道比他更坚强的意志；无论威胁还是许诺，都不能叫这个不动声色的人把他抱定的态度挪动分毫。

===Original===

“What does it matter whether you see this paper or not?” asked Yeh Ling. “You say you know the murderer, that you have sufficient evidence to put him on the trap﻿—but have you?”
His look was a challenge.
“You cannot convict a man on supposition, Mr. Carver. You must prove beyond any doubt whatever that Jesse Trasmere was killed by somebody who had the means of getting in and out that locked vault, and leaving the key on the table. It is not enough to say: ‘I am certain that this prisoner killed his﻿—benefactor.’ It is not sufficient that you can show motives. You must produce the means! Until you can say the murderer obtained admission to the vault by this or that door, in this or that way, or that he employed these or those means to restore the key to the table from the outside of a locked door through which no key could pass, you cannot secure a conviction. That is the law. I studied law at Harvard, and I have the rules of evidence at my fingertips,” he smiled faintly. “You see, Mr. Carver, that the confirmatory evidence you require cannot possibly be supplied by me.”

===Chinese===

“这张纸您看得着看不着，又有什么要紧？”叶凌反问，“您说您知道凶手是谁，说您手里的证据已足以送他上绞架——果真如此吗？”
他的目光形同一纸挑战书。
“单凭臆测，您是定不了一个人的罪的，卡弗先生。您必须证明得毫无疑义：杰西·特拉斯米尔死于某人之手，而此人有办法进出那间锁死的保险库，并把钥匙留在桌上。光说一句‘我确信这名囚犯杀死了他的——恩主’，是不够的。您能摆出动机，也不够。您必须拿出作案的手段来！直到您能说清凶手是从这扇门还是那扇门、以这种途径还是那种途径进入的保险库，说清他用了这样或那样的什么手段，把钥匙从一扇上了锁、钥匙根本无法穿越的门外送回到了桌上，您才拿得下有罪判决。法律便是如此。我在哈佛学的是法律，证据规则我可烂熟于心。”他淡淡一笑。“您瞧，卡弗先生，您所需要的补强证据，无论如何不可能由我来供给。”

===Original===

Carver knew that he was speaking no more than the truth; that he was against a dead wall unless some human eye had witnessed the murder, and the method by which the murderer escaped.
The logic of the Chinaman’s criticism was irresistible, and Carver, who had seen success within reach, experienced a sense of failure at the very moment when he thought that all his efforts were coming to fruition.
“Then tell me this,” he said. “I understand that on several occasions you have been followed by this Man in Black. Have you any idea who he is?”
“Yes,” said the other without hesitation, “but what is the value of my ideas? I could not swear to any facts, and facts are the meat and drink of juries, Mr. Carver.”

===Chinese===

卡弗知道他句句是实情：除非有一双世人的眼睛亲眼目睹了凶案、目睹了凶手脱身的手法，否则自己便是撞在一堵死墙上。
这位中国人评论的逻辑无可辩驳。卡弗本眼看成功触手可及，却在自以为心血即将结出果实的那一刻，尝到了挫败的滋味。
“那么请您告诉我一件事，”他说，“据我所知，那个黑衣人已经尾随过您好几回。他是什么人，您心里可有数？”
“有数。”对方毫不迟疑地答道，“可我的想法又值什么？任何事实我都无从起誓，而事实，才是陪审团吃惯喝惯的饭食，卡弗先生。”

===Original===

Carver got up and sighed heavily, and hearing him Yeh Ling broke into a fit of silent laughter.
“I am sorry,” he apologised, “but I am thinking of my favorite poem,” his eyes twinkled. “You remember the other gentleman who ’rose with a sigh, and he said, ‘Can this be? We are ruined by Chinese cheap labour.﻿ ﻿…’ ”
“I shall certainly not ‘go the Heathen Chinee,’ ” said Carver good-humouredly, “not for the moment. What I like about you, Yeh Ling, is your refreshing sanity. I don’t know that I have ever dealt with a man﻿—shall I say fought with a man?﻿—who would have given me greater pleasure to fence.”
The Chinaman performed a deep kowtow and his mock humility amused Carver long after he had left the shadow of the Golden Roof.

===Chinese===

卡弗站起身，重重叹了口气。叶凌听见了，忽然发作起一阵无声的大笑。
“失礼了，”他赔罪道，“只是我想起了我心爱的那首诗。”他两眼发亮，“您总还记得诗里另外那位先生吧——他‘叹了口气站起身来，说道：“竟会这样？我们全叫中国廉价劳工给毁了……”’”
“我是断断不会‘败于异教中国佬’的，”卡弗和颜悦色地说，“至少眼下还不会。叶凌，我喜欢您的一点，就是您那份令人神清气爽的清明通达。我说不好，我平生打过交道的——不妨说交过手的——人里头，可曾有过哪一位，比您更让我觉得交锋是一件快事。”
这位中国人深深行了一个磕头礼；他那佯装的谦卑，让卡弗在走出金顶楼的楼影之后许久，心里还觉得好笑。

===Original===

Yeh Ling, who seldom made any personal effort for the comfort of his guests, paid particular attention to the preparations which were being made in No. 6 that night. The Italian waiters, to whom the proprietor was almost unknown, were both nervous and annoyed, for nothing seemed to please Yeh Ling. He had the flowers changed half-a-dozen times. He had new cloths brought and at the last minute insisted upon the table being laid all over again. He brought the rarest of glass to adorn the board, unearthed unsuspected treasures in chinaware, and substituted them for the crockery of the restaurant. This done, he summoned to his room the maître d’hôtel and the wine chef, and chose the dinner with the most exquisite care.

===Chinese===

叶凌向来很少为宾客的舒适亲自费神，那天晚上却对 6 号房的备办格外上心。那些几乎没见过老板本人的意大利侍者，一个个又紧张又气恼，因为似乎没有一样东西能合叶凌的意。插花他叫人换了六回；新桌布也叫人取来，到了最后一刻，他还坚持把整张餐桌重新摆放一遍。他又取出最珍罕的玻璃器皿来装点桌面，翻出瓷器中无人知晓的珍藏，把餐馆的日常陶器统统换了下来。这一切办妥，他才把侍者总管和司酒师傅召到房里，以最精细的心思点定了菜式。

===Original===

“Yeh Ling has really done himself proud,” said Tab admiring the table.
The girl nodded. She had hoped that Yeh Ling would have chosen another room, but she had no real feeling of repugnance and besides she had been here since Trasmere’s death.
“It is very thrilling to be dining alone with a young man,” she said, handing her wrap to the waiter, “and I can only hope the scandal of it doesn’t get into the newspapers!”
“Shall we see Yeh Ling?” asked Tab, halfway through the dinner.

===Chinese===

“叶凌这回可真给自己挣足了体面。”塔布打量着餐桌，赞叹道。
姑娘点了点头。她原本希望叶凌会另选一间房，可她心里并无真正的不快，况且，特拉斯米尔死后她一直在这间房里用餐。
“单独同一位年轻男子吃饭，真是够刺激的。”她把披肩递给侍者时说道，“我只盼这桩丑闻别登上报纸才好！”
“咱们能见着叶凌吗？”吃到一半，塔布问。

===Original===

She shook her head.
“He never appears. He has only been in this room twice to my recollection.”
“It is our first appearance together in public,” said Tab solemnly. “I can count on our boys, but if any of those *Herald* thugs hear and catch a glimpse of your expensive ring, there are going to be scare lines in that deplorable rag﻿—the ‘Herald’ has no reticence on decency.”
She laughed softly and looked at the “expensive ring” that glittered and sparkled in the light of the shaded lamp.
“I asked Carver if he would come along after dinner,” said Tab, “but he is busy. He sent the most flowery and poetical messages to you﻿—really Carver is a surprising person; there is a whole world of romance hidden behind that somewhat unpleasing exterior, if you will pardon the journalese.”

===Chinese===

她摇了摇头。
“他从不露面。就我所记得的，他统共只进过这间房两回。”
“这是咱们俩头一回一起公开露面。”塔布郑重其事地说，“自家报馆的弟兄我信得过；可要是叫《先驱报》那帮恶客听了风声，再瞥上一眼您那枚昂贵的戒指，那份糟糕透顶的破报上就要登出耸人听闻的大字标题来了——《先驱报》在体统上是毫无忌惮的。”
她轻轻笑了，低头看那枚在灯罩滤下的光里熠熠闪烁的“昂贵戒指”。
“我请卡弗饭后来一趟，”塔布说，“可他忙。他托我向您转致最花团锦簇、最富诗意的问候——卡弗这人真是出人意料；他那副不怎么讨人喜欢的外表底下，藏着一整个浪漫世界，恕我借用一句报馆行话。”

===Original===

But if Carver could not come, they had a visitor. There came a tap and the door opened slowly.
“Great Moses!” said Tab springing up. “How the dickens did you know that we were here, Rex?”
“I spotted you,” said Rex Lander reproachfully, “slinking in at the side door like two guilty souls! May I offer my congratulations, Miss Ardfern, and lay at your feet the fragments of a broken heart?”
She laughed nervously at his jest.
“No, I can’t stay,” said Rex, “I have a party and, moreover, I am entertaining a man with terrific ideas on architecture. Isn’t that queer! Now that I am no longer arcing, I have conceived a passion for that unhallowed profession! Even old Stott is becoming an admirable personage in my eyes. Have you forgiven me, Miss Ardfern?”

===Chinese===

卡弗虽然来不了，他们却等来了一位访客。门上响起轻轻的叩声，房门缓缓开了。
“我的老天爷！”塔布一跃而起，“你到底怎么知道我们在这儿的，雷克斯？”
“我可是亲眼瞧见的，”雷克斯·兰德话里带着嗔怪，“瞧你们俩跟两个亏了心的魂灵似的，从边门溜了进来！请容我道贺，阿德芬小姐，并将一颗碎心的残片敬奉于您的足下。”
对他这句玩笑，她笑得有些不安。
“不，我不能久留，”雷克斯说，“我还有个饭局，况且我正陪着一位对建筑学怀有惊人高见的先生。怪也不怪！如今我不再干建筑这一行了，反倒对那门亵渎神明的行当起了满腔热忱！连老斯托特在我眼里都渐渐成了可敬可佩的人物。您原谅我了吗，阿德芬小姐？”

===Original===

“Oh yes,” she said quietly, “I have forgiven you a very long time ago.”
Rex’s baby eyes were very kindly, his plump face was wrinkled in a smile of amiable reflection.
“ ‘When a young man’s fancy﻿—’ ” he began and caught a reflection in the mirror.
From where Tab and the girl sat they could see nothing. Rex saw reflected the half-open door and a figure that stood motionless outside. He spun round with an exclamation.

===Chinese===

“哦，原谅了。”她平静地说，“很久很久以前，我就原谅你了。”
雷克斯那双娃娃眼盛满和善，胖乎乎的脸皱起一团笑纹，透着可亲的沉思之色。
“‘当年轻人的情思——’”他刚起了个头，就瞥见了镜子里的映影。
从塔布和姑娘所坐的位置望去，什么也看不见。雷克斯却在镜中望见那扇半开的门，望见一个在门外一动不动伫立的人影。他惊呼一声，猛地转过身去。

## XXX / 三十

===Original===

He woke with a start. There was a rush of feet in the sitting-room and then﻿—crash!
He was out of bed in a second and into the sitting-room. Carver was nowhere to be seen and he felt by the draught that the door of the flat was wide open. He put his hand on the light switch and a voice from the darkness said:

===Chinese===

他猛地惊醒。起居室里一阵急促的脚步声，跟着——砰的一声巨响！
他转瞬便翻身下床，冲进起居室。卡弗不见了踪影，从灌进来的穿堂风里他觉出公寓大门敞得大开。他伸手去摸电灯开关，黑暗里一个声音说道：

===Original===

“Don’t touch that light!”
It came from outside the door and it was Carver who was speaking.
Below came the thud of the street-door closing.

===Chinese===

“别碰那灯！”
声音从门外传来，说话的正是卡弗。
楼下传来临街大门关上的闷响。

===Original===

Carver came hurriedly into the room, passed him, ran to the window and looked out.
“You can put it on now,” said Carver. A red welt was slashed across his face and it was bleeding slightly.
He put up his hand and looked at it.

===Chinese===

卡弗急匆匆进得屋来，从他身边擦过，奔到窗前向外张望。
“现在可以开了。”卡弗说。他脸上横着一道红肿的伤痕，微微渗着血。
他抬手碰了碰，端详着那道伤。

===Original===

“That was a narrow squeak,” he said. “Yes, he’s gone. I could have taken a chance and run downstairs after the door slammed, but even that might have been a fake to lure me into the open.”

===Chinese===

“真是险之又险。”他说，“没错，人走了。大门摔上之后，我本可以碰碰运气追下楼去，可就连那一下，说不定也是使的障眼法，要把我诱到明处去。”

===Original===

The whole building was awake now. Tab heard the sound of unlocking doors and voices speaking from above and below.
“It was the cigar that gave me away,” said Carver ruefully. “I was a fool to smoke. He must have seen the red end in the darkness, and on the whole I think he shot pretty accurately.”

===Chinese===

这会儿整座楼都醒了。塔布听见四下里开锁的声响，楼上楼下人语嘈杂。
“是雪茄把我卖了。”卡弗懊悔地说，“我真不该抽烟。他必定看见了黑暗里那点红头；而且总的说来，我看他打得相当准。”

===Original===

There was a small Medici print hanging by the side of the window. The glass was shattered. A round bullet-hole showed on the white shoulder of Beatrice D’Este.
Carver fingered the hole carefully.

===Chinese===

窗边挂着一小幅梅迪奇版画（Medici print，按：梅迪奇印制社系伦敦著名的美术印刷品商），玻璃打得粉碎，贝亚特丽斯·德斯特（Beatrice D’Este）白皙的肩头上现出一个圆圆的弹孔。
卡弗细细摩挲那个弹孔。

===Original===

“That looks to me like an automatic,” he said. “He is getting quite modern. The last time he killed a man he used a type of revolver which was issued by the Chinese government to its officers some fifteen years ago. We know that from the shape of the bullet,” he went on unconcerned. “There is somebody at the door, Tab. You had better go and explain we have had another attack of burglaritis.”

===Chinese===

“依我看，这是自动手枪。”他说，“他越来越新派了。上一回杀人，他用的还是十五年前中国政府发给军官们的那种左轮手枪。这一点，我们从弹头的形状就认得出来。”他毫不在意地接着往下说，“门口有人，塔布。你最好去解释一下，就说咱们这儿又发作了一回‘入室盗窃炎’。”

===Original===

Tab was gone some ten minutes, quieting the tenants of the flats. When he returned he found Carver examining the track of the second bullet, which had struck the lower window sash and which had drilled a neat little hole.
“Probably hit the wall opposite,” said Carver, squinting through.

===Chinese===

塔布出去了约莫十分钟，才把楼里的住户安抚下来。回来时，他见卡弗正在查验第二颗子弹的路径——那一弹打在下窗扇上，钻出一个利落的小孔。
“八成打在对面的墙上了。”卡弗眯着眼往孔里瞧。

===Original===

“The man below found this on the stairs,” said Tab.
It was a small green-handled knife in a lacquered scabbard.
“Pseudo-Chinese,” said Carver. “It may even be the genuine article.” He pulled out the knife, tested the razor-like edge, “And sharp,” he said. “I had an idea he didn’t mean to use his gun.”

===Chinese===

“楼下的住户在楼梯上捡到了这个。”塔布说。
那是一把绿柄小刀，装在一只漆鞘里。
“仿中国货。”卡弗说，“说不定还是正宗的真家伙。”他拔出刀来，试了试那剃刀也似的锋刃。“还挺快。”他说，“我原本就疑心，他没打算用枪。”

===Original===

“Now,” said Tab, facing the detective squarely, “we will dispense with all light and airy persiflage and come down to sober affidavits. You expected this attack. That is why you came tonight with your fake story of a literary-minded nephew.”

===Chinese===

“好了，”塔布直视着侦探，“咱们这就撇下那些轻飘飘的俏皮话，来听听正儿八经的证词。你早就料到会有这场袭击——所以今晚才编出个‘想当新闻记者的侄子’的托辞，上这儿来过夜。”

===Original===

“I did and I didn’t,” said Carver frankly. “When I told you that the attack would be made on me, I half believed it, but as I couldn’t find an excuse for getting you to stay with me, and, moreover, as I have no accommodation for a man of your luxurious habits, I decided on the whole I’d take a chance by staying here.” He looked at his watch. “Two o’clock,” he said. “He must have come about a quarter of an hour ago, and I will give him this credit, that I did not hear the door open. Fortunately there was a clothes hook behind the door and sometime or other you hung an old hat there. It was hearing this hat fall that made me realise that either I was growing deaf, or else the stealthy personage was unusually soft-footed. He must have seen first my cigar, and then my outline as I rose, for like a fool, I hadn’t pulled the settee away from the window. He was back in the lobby in a flash and before I knew what had happened he had fired twice, slammed the door and gone. He was still in the hall when I went out, but it was so dark that I could see nothing.”

===Chinese===

“算是，也不算是。”卡弗坦白地说，“我对你说袭击会冲着我来，这话我自己也只信了一半；可我既找不出个由头请你上我那儿住，何况我那儿也安置不下你这么一位讲究舒服的爷，权衡再三，我决定还是冒险住到你这儿来。”他看了看表。“两点钟。”他说，“他必定是一刻钟前来的。有一样我得给他记一分功劳：我竟没听见开门的动静。幸亏门后有一个挂衣钩，也不知你什么时候在上头挂过一顶旧帽子。正是听见这顶帽子落地，我才醒悟过来：不是我耳朵背了，就是那位蹑手蹑脚的贵客脚下轻得出奇。他必定先瞧见我的雪茄，接着又瞧见我起身时的轮廓——都怪我犯傻，没把长沙发从窗口挪开。他一闪身便退回了穿堂，我还没弄明白是怎么回事，他已连开两枪，砰地摔门而去。我追出去时他还在楼下大厅里，可那里黑得什么也看不见。”

===Original===

“I thought I heard the door first.”
“Because you were asleep,” smiled the detective, “and you hear the last sound first. No, I will give you a guarantee that he shot at me before he shut the door.” His eyes narrowed. “I wonder,” he said softly.

===Chinese===

“我还当自己先听见的是开门声。”
“那是因为你睡着了，”侦探含着笑说，“睡梦里的人，把最后一声当成了头一声。不，我可以向你担保：他是先朝我开的枪，然后才摔的门。”他眯起眼睛。“我倒想弄明白一件事。”他轻声说。

===Original===

“What?”
“I wonder if your friend has had a duplicate of this attack? Where is he staying?”

===Chinese===

“想弄明白什么？”
“我是想，你那位朋友会不会也挨了同样的一遭？他这会儿住在哪儿？”

===Original===

“I think we ought to warn him, anyway,” said Tab. “Our visitor came in the first place to burgle Rex’s trunks and probably he doesn’t know that Rex isn’t staying here. He is at the Pitt Hotel.”

===Chinese===

“不管怎样，咱们总该给他递个信。”塔布说，“咱们这位访客头一回来的本意就是撬雷克斯的衣箱，他多半还不知道雷克斯已经不住在这儿了。他眼下住在皮特饭店（Pitt Hotel）。”

===Original===

Carver got the telephone directory and discovered the number. It was some time before he had an answer, for the clerks at the Pitt Hotel are not accustomed to calls at that hour of the morning. Presently he got into touch with a porter.

===Chinese===

卡弗取来电话号码簿，查到了号码。过了好一阵才有回应——皮特饭店的柜台职员可不习惯清早这个时辰接电话。不多时，他便同一位门房通了话。

===Original===

“I don’t know whether he is staying here, but I will find out,” said that official.
It was ten minutes before he had made the discovery.

===Chinese===

“他是否住在本店，我说不准，不过我可以去查一查。”那位当值的说道。
查了十分钟，才有了结果。

===Original===

“Yes, he is in Room 180. Shall I put you through?”
“If you please,” said Carver. He heard the click and clug of the connection being made and after an appreciable delay Rex’s sleepy voice answered him.

===Chinese===

“查到了，他住 180 号房。给您接过去吗？”
“有劳。”卡弗说。他听见线路接通时的咔嗒声响，隔了好一阵，雷克斯睡意浓重的嗓音才应了过来。

===Original===

“Hullo, who is that? What the devil do you want?”
“I’ll talk to him,” whispered Tab and took the receiver from the detective’s hands.

===Chinese===

“喂，哪位？这么晚到底要干什么？”
“我来跟他说。”塔布压低嗓子，从侦探手里接过听筒。

===Original===

“Is that you Rex?”
“Hullo, who is that, Tab? What’s the idea?”

===Chinese===

“是你吗，雷克斯？”
“喂，是谁呀——塔布？这是唱的哪一出？”

===Original===

“We have had a visitor,” said Tab. “You remember I told you about the burglar? Well, he came again tonight.”
“The devil he did.”

===Chinese===

“咱们家来了位客人。”塔布说，“记得我跟你说过的那个贼吧？嗯，他今夜又来了。”
“好家伙，他真来了。”

===Original===

“In fact, we’ve turned the old flat into a shooting gallery,” said Tab, “and Carver wonders whether you have had a similar experience.”
“Not I,” was the cheerful reply. “It is as much as a man’s life’s worth to wake me out of my sleep.”

===Chinese===

“岂止是来了，咱们索性把老公寓改成打靶场了。”塔布说，“卡弗想问问，你那边是不是也领教过同样的遭遇。”
“我可没有，”对方答得兴致冲冲，“把人从睡梦里硬叫起来，那可是要人命的事。”

===Original===

Tab grinned.
“Keep your door locked.”

===Chinese===

塔布咧嘴一笑。
“把门锁好。”

===Original===

“And my telephone receiver off,” said the other. “I’ll let you know if anything happens. Is Carver there?”
“Yes,” said Tab.
Carver went to the phone.

===Chinese===

“还要把电话听筒摘下来。”对方说，“有个风吹草动我就告诉你。卡弗在跟前吗？”
“在。”塔布说。
卡弗走到电话跟前。

===Original===

“He wants to speak to you.”
Carver had made a signal and now he took the receiver in his hand.

===Chinese===

“他要跟你说话。”
原来卡弗方才早打了个手势，这时便把听筒接到手中。

===Original===

“I am sorry you have been disturbed, Mr. Lander,” he said, “but I’d like you to know officially that we warn you that an attempt has been made to get into this flat at﻿—well ten minutes ago. What time would that be?”

===Chinese===

“搅了您的清梦，实在抱歉，兰德先生。”他说，“不过我要正式知会您一声：就在——嗯，十分钟前，有人试图闯入这套公寓。那会儿是几点来着？”

===Original===

“That would be about a quarter of two, I guess,” said Rex’s voice. “Thank you for telling me, Inspector, but I am not at all scared.”
Carver put the receiver on the hook and rubbed his hands.

===Chinese===

“我琢磨着，大约是一点三刻。”雷克斯的声音说，“多谢探长关照，不过我一点儿也不怕。”
卡弗挂上听筒，搓了搓双手。

===Original===

“Do you think they will go there? What on earth is amusing you?” asked Tab irritably.
“I am intensely amused, I admit,” said Carver, “at the queer and simple error that our murderer made.”

===Chinese===

“你说他们会上他那儿去吗？你究竟在乐些什么？”塔布不耐烦地问。
“不瞒你说，我真是乐不可支。”卡弗道，“咱们这位凶手，犯了一个又古怪又低级的错误。”

===Original===

Early in the morning Carver called at the Pitt Hotel and personally interviewed a sleepy-eyed Rex, who sat up in bed in violently striped pyjamas and expostulated with commendable mildness upon the interruption to his night’s sleep.

===Chinese===

清晨，卡弗到皮特饭店登门，亲自会见了睡眼惺忪的雷克斯。雷克斯坐在床上，身穿条纹扎眼的睡衣，以堪称难得的温和，就搅了他一夜安睡这件事抗辩了几句。

===Original===

“I am one of those people,” he said severely, “who require at least twelve hours’ heavy slumber. Heaven having endowed me with the means whereby I can gratify my wishes in this respect, it is a little short of an outrage that Tab and you should call me up even to tell me that the flat has been burgled again.”

===Chinese===

“我是那种人，”他板起面孔说，“至少得沉沉睡足十二个钟头。上苍既然赐了我资财，让我在这上头都能称心遂愿，那么哪怕是为了告诉我公寓又进了贼，塔布和你竟把我从床上叫起来，这也就简直近乎暴行了。”

===Original===

Reporting his interview on his return to the flat, Carver offered a few remarks on the vagaries of masculine fashions, particularly in relation to pyjamas, and came back at a tangent to the very serious events of the past twelve hours.

===Chinese===

回到公寓复述此行经过时，卡弗就男式时髦衣着的种种怪状发了几句议论——尤其是关于睡衣的——随后话锋一转，又回到过去十二小时里那些十分严重的事件上来。

===Original===

“I think you’ll be all right tonight,” he said. “At any rate, I am leaving you to your own devices. Bolt the door and put a trip wire between a couple of chairs.”
“Oh nonsense!” said Tab. “He will not come again tonight.”

===Chinese===

“我看今晚你不会有事。”他说，“不管怎样，我就不奉陪了，你自己见机行事。把门闩上，再在两把椅子之间拉一道绊线。”
“咳，瞎操心！”塔布说，“他今夜不会再来了。”

===Original===

Carver scratched his chin.
“What is tonight.”

===Chinese===

卡弗挠了挠下巴。
“今晚是什么日子。”

===Original===

“Saturday.”

===Chinese===

“星期六。”

===Original===

“The fatal Saturday eh?” he said. “No, perhaps not. What are you doing today?”
“I am driving a friend into the country, or rather she is driving me,” said Tab promptly. “It is my weekend off, but I shall be back in town tonight.”

===Chinese===

“正是出事的那个星期六，嗯？”他说，“不，或许不会来了。你今天打算做什么？”
“我要开车送一位朋友去乡下——说得更准些，是她开车送我。”塔布应声答道，“这个周末我歇班，不过今夜就回城。”

===Original===

Carver nodded.
“Ring me up the moment you are back. Will you promise that?”
And Tab laughed.

===Chinese===

卡弗点了点头。
“你一回到城里就给我来电话。能答应我吗？”
塔布笑了。

===Original===

“Certainly I will if it is any satisfaction to you.”

===Chinese===

“只要能让你宽心，我一定打。”

===Original===

“If you don’t ring me, I shall ring you at intervals throughout the night,” threatened Carver. “I have already warned Lander. Above all, Lander must not sleep at this flat.”

===Chinese===

“你要是不打，我就整夜隔一阵打一回，非把你吵醒不可。”卡弗以威胁的口吻说道，“兰德那边我已经知会过了。最要紧的一条：兰德千万不可睡在这套公寓里。”

===Original===

“Don’t you think that by now they will have discovered that he is no longer living in the flat?” asked Tab.
“They may or may not,” was the reply, “and﻿—” he hesitated, “I don’t think I should talk about this to Miss Ardfern. In fact, I would rather you didn’t.”

===Chinese===

“你以为他们到现在还没发觉，他已经不住在这套公寓里了吗？”塔布问。
“发觉也罢，没发觉也罢。”对方答道，“还有——”他迟疑了一下，“这事我看不必对阿德芬小姐提起。说实在的，我宁可你别提。”

===Original===

Tab had had no intention of alarming Ursula, and he could make that promise without reservation.

===Chinese===

塔布本来就没打算惊动乌苏拉，这个承诺他答应得毫无保留。

## XXXI / 三十一

===Original===

She came to Doughty Street and picked him up and they were at Stone Cottage in time for lunch. The weather was still unsettled, but Tab had passed the stage where weather made any difference to him.
Though he did not tell her of his sensational experience, he did mention his dream.
“Ursula?” he asked. “You like Yeh Ling, don’t you? So do I, as a matter of fact, but do you absolutely trust him?”

===Chinese===

她来到道蒂街接上他，两人赶回石屋，正好赶上午饭。天气仍是阴晴不定，不过塔布早已过了会把天气当回事的阶段。
他虽没有对她讲自己那番惊心动魄的遭遇，却提到了那个梦。
“乌苏拉？”他问道，“你喜欢叶凌，是不是？说实在的，我也喜欢他。可你能毫无保留地信得过他么？”

===Original===

She considered before she spoke.
“Yes, I think I do,” she said. “He has been a most faithful friend. Think, Tab, without my knowledge, all these years he has been watching over me. I should be a most ungrateful girl if his loyalty did not move me.”
Tab thought that there might be some other explanation of Yeh Ling’s devotion but wisely said nothing.
“Do you know,” she said, “that he keeps a man watching this house day and night? I only discovered it by accident when I was engaged in revolver shooting. Perhaps Yeh Ling told you that I nearly shot one of his sentinels.”

===Chinese===

她思量片刻，方才开口。
“是的，我想我信得过。”她说，“他一直是个极其忠实的朋友。想想吧，塔布，这么多年来他一直在暗中护着我，而我竟毫不知情。倘若他的这份忠心都不能打动我，我可就成了个顶顶忘恩负义的姑娘了。”
塔布心想，叶凌这份殷勤背后未必没有别的缘故，但他识趣地闭口不言。
“你可知道，”她说，“他派了个人日夜看守着这所房子？我是练手枪射击的时候偶然发现的。没准叶凌跟你说过了，我险些开枪打中了他的一个哨探。”

===Original===

“He is a strange man,” admitted Tab, “but my dream rather impressed me﻿—”
“Even the first part of your dream hasn’t come true yet,” she suggested demurely and he picked her up in his arms there and then.
Happily the so-easily scandalized Mr. Turner was engaged elsewhere.

===Chinese===

“他是个古怪的人。”塔布承认道，“可我那个梦着实让我心里发毛——”
“就连你那梦的前一半，不也还没应验么？”她故作端庄地打趣道。他便当场把她一把抱进了怀中。
幸而那位动辄大惊小怪的特纳先生正在别处有事。

===Original===

His heart was full of love and gratitude when he left her in the sweet-smelling dusk and mounting his bicycle, which he had brought strapped to the back of Ursula’s car, started on his leisurely way home.
Halfway he had a puncture which delayed him, and it was nearly ten o’clock when he wheeled the machine into the garage where it was maintained.
The last part of the journey was made through a heavy driving rain and he was wet through by the time he reached Doughty Street.
A hot bath and a change of clothing brightened him and he was filling his cigarette case preparatory to going out to take a meal, when he was called to the telephone. He expected to be greeted by Carver but it was Rex who was speaking and his voice was eager and urgent.

===Chinese===

他在芬芳的暮色中辞别了她，满怀着爱与感激；随后跨上自行车——那车是捆在乌苏拉汽车后座上捎来的——悠然踏上了归途。
半路上车胎扎破了，耽搁了他一阵；等他把车子推进平日存放保养的那座车库，已快十点了。
最后一程是在瓢泼的骤雨中赶完的，到道蒂街时，他已浑身湿透。
一个热水澡、一身干净衣裳让他精神一振；他正往烟盒里装烟，预备出门吃饭，却有人来叫他听电话。他满以为会听到卡弗的声音，开口的却是雷克斯，嗓音急切而紧张。

===Original===

“Is that you Tab? My boy, I’ve made the most wonderful discovery!”
“What is that?” asked Tab wondering.
“You are not to breathe a word to Carver, you understand, Tab? This is the most extraordinary discovery. Tab!” his voice shook, “I have found how the murder was committed!”
“The Trasmere murder?”
“Yes,” came the quick reply. “I know how the man got in and out of the vault. I was in there this afternoon inspecting the work that has been done and I found it by accident. It is all so simple, Tab, how the key got on to the table, and﻿—everything. Can you meet me at Mayfield?”

===Chinese===

“是你吗，塔布？老弟，我有了一项了不得的大发现！”
“什么大发现？”塔布纳闷地问。
“你可不能向卡弗走漏半个字，明白吗，塔布？这是一项天底下最离奇的发现。塔布！”他的声音发颤，“我发现那桩谋杀是怎么干的了！”
“特拉斯米尔那桩谋杀案？”
“正是。”回答来得飞快，“我知道那人是怎么进出保险库的了。今天下午我在里头查看已完工的活计，无意间发现了它。一切简单得很，塔布——那把钥匙怎么会到桌上，还有——一切的一切。你能到梅菲尔德来见我吗？”

===Original===

“At Mayfield?”
“I’ll be waiting outside the door for you. I don’t want any of Carver’s men to see us.”
“Why not?” asked Tab.
“Because,” said Rex’s voice deliberately, “Carver is in this murder up to his neck!”
Tab nearly dropped the receiver from his hands.

===Chinese===

“在梅菲尔德？”
“我在门外等你。我不想让卡弗手下的任何人看见咱们。”
“为什么不能？”塔布问。
“因为，”雷克斯的声音一字一顿地说，“这桩谋杀案，卡弗连脖子都陷进去了！”
塔布手里的听筒险些跌落。

===Original===

“You are mad,” he said.
“Am I? You shall judge for yourself. And Yeh Ling is in it﻿—hurry!”
Tab ran to the larder and pushed a handful of biscuits into his pocket, put on his raincoat, and went out into the vile night, his mind in a state of chaos.
Carver!
And Yeh Ling was in it too!

===Chinese===

“你疯了。”他说。
“是吗？你自己来判断吧。叶凌也脱不了干系——快来！”
塔布奔到食品间，抓了一把饼干塞进口袋，穿上雨衣，走进那恶劣的夜色里，脑子里乱作一团。
卡弗！
连叶凌也牵连在内！

===Original===

The wind had risen and half a gale swept through the deserted Peak Avenue as he strode along to the house of mystery. He did not see Rex until he passed through the gate. That young man was standing under the shelter of the portico by the door. Nearby in the concrete yard Tab saw a car.
“We’ll find our way on in the dark. I’ve got a pocket lamp,” he whispered, and Tab stepped into the dark, deserted hall, with its musty scent and its strangely oppressive atmosphere of decay and neglect.
Rex’s voice was trembling with excitement.
“We can put the lights on after we get into the corridor,” he said.

===Chinese===

风已经刮了起来，半场狂风扫过空寂无人的皮克大街，他迈开大步，朝那座神秘的宅子走去。直到进了大门，他才看见雷克斯。那位年轻人正立在门口门廊的遮蔽之下。近旁的水泥院子里，塔布看见一辆汽车。
“咱们摸黑往里走。我带了手电。”他耳语道。塔布迈步走进那昏暗冷落的门厅——霉味阵阵，弥漫着一种古怪而逼人的荒废冷清气息。
雷克斯的声音激动得直打颤。
“进了走廊咱们再开灯。”他说。

===Original===

He found his way across the room by the light of the lamp, unlocked the door and led the way into the passage.
“Shut that door, Tab,” he hissed and when Tab had obeyed he turned on all the lights.
Near the end of the corridor Tab saw a great heap of bricks and a board covered with mortar; the work of bricking up the vault had begun and the first course stretched across the open doorway of the vault.
Rex stepped over the brickwork and illuminated the empty interior.

===Chinese===

他借着手电的光穿过房间，开了锁，领头走进那道走廊。
“把门关上，塔布。”他压着嗓子嘶声说。塔布照办之后，他把灯全开了。
临近走廊尽头，塔布看见一大堆砖，还有一块摊满灰浆的木板；封砌保险库的工程已经动工，头一层砖正横在保险库敞开的门口。
雷克斯跨过砖砌，照见了空空如也的库内。

===Original===

“There!” said Rex triumphantly and pointed to the table.
“What is it?” asked Tab in amazement.
“Hold both sides of the table and pull.”
“But the table is fixed on to the floor; we noticed that before,” said Tab.
“Do as I tell you,” said Rex impatiently.
Tab leant over the table, and gripping both edges firmly pulled.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

“瞧这儿！”雷克斯得意洋洋地说，指着那张桌子。
“是什么呀？”塔布惊异地问。
“握住桌子两边，往外拉。”
“可这桌子是固定在地板上的，咱们以前就注意到了。”塔布说。
“照我说的做。”雷克斯不耐烦地说。
塔布俯身向桌，两手紧紧扣住桌沿，用力一拉。……

## XXXII / 三十二

===Original===

He awoke to consciousness with a sense of dull pain at the back of his neck and a feeling of restriction. He was sitting against the wall propped up, and when he tried to bring up a hand to rub his aching neck, he found they would not move. He opened his eyes and looked around and the first thing he noticed was that his feet were strapped together. He stared stupidly at the fastening, and tried to move his hands﻿—but they were in a curious position. He was handcuffed behind. To the connecting links a cord had been fastened and passed under him to the strap.

===Chinese===

他恢复了知觉，后颈一阵隐隐作痛，还有一种动弹不得的束缚感。他被人撑扶着靠墙坐着，想抬起一只手揉揉发疼的脖子，却发现两只手都动弹不得。他睁开眼睛四下张望，头一眼注意到的事，是他的双脚被皮带捆在了一起。他傻愣愣地盯着那处捆缚，又试着挪动双手——可它们摆在一个古怪的位置上。他的双手被反铐在身后，手铐的连接环上系着一根细绳，绳子从他身子底下穿过，一直拴到脚上那条皮带扣上。

===Original===

“What﻿—” he began and heard somebody laugh softly.
Looking up he saw Rex. That young man was sitting on the edge of the table smoking.
“Feel better?” he asked politely.
“What is the meaning of this, Rex?”
“It means that, as I promised, you have found the murderer of dear Uncle Jesse,” said Rex, his baby blue eyes gleaming. “I killed Jesse Trasmere. I also killed that drunken beast Brown. I didn’t intend killing Brown,” he went on reflectively. “Unfortunately he left me no alternative. He recognised me in the park at the time when I was supposed to be in Naples.”

===Chinese===

“什么——”他刚一开口，就听见有人轻轻一笑。
抬头一看，是雷克斯。那位年轻人正坐在桌沿上抽烟。
“好受些了吗？”他客气地问。
“这是什么意思，雷克斯？”
“意思是，正如我答应过的，你已经找到了杀害亲爱的杰西叔叔的凶手。”雷克斯说，他那双婴儿蓝的眼睛灼灼放光。“是我杀了杰西·特拉斯米尔。我还杀了布朗那个醉畜生。我本无意杀布朗，”他若有所思地接着说，“不幸的是，他没有给我留半点选择的余地。他在公园里认出了我——而在那个时候，世人都以为我在那不勒斯。”

===Original===

“Didn’t you go abroad?” gasped Tab, the minor deception for the moment bulking largely.
Rex shook his head.
“I didn’t go any farther than the mouth of the river,” he said. “I came off with the pilot. The cables and wireless messages that I sent were despatched by the steward, whom I paid for that purpose. I never left town.”
Tab could say nothing.

===Chinese===

“你没有出国？”塔布倒抽一口气；那桩小小的欺瞒，此刻竟膨胀成了头等大事。
雷克斯摇了摇头。
“我到河口就再没往前走。”他说，“我是跟引水员一同下的船。我那些越洋电报和无线电讯，都是那侍应生代发的，我为此花钱雇了他。我压根儿就没离开过本城。”
塔布一句话也说不出来。

===Original===

“If you had done as I wished,” said Rex with an odd note of reproach in his voice, “I should have made you a rich man, Tab, but like the sneaking swine you are, you took the woman who was foreordained to be my wife! Your beastly lips have touched hers, my goddess!” His voice quavered.
Tab, staring at him, realized that he was in the presence of a madman.
“You think I am mad,” said Rex, as though he was guessing the other’s thoughts. “Perhaps I am, but I adore her. I killed Jesse Trasmere because I wanted her, could not wait for her, needed the money to possess her.”

===Chinese===

“当初你要是依了我的心意，”雷克斯说，声音里带着一缕古怪的责备，“我本会让你做个有钱人，塔布。可你这条鬼鬼祟祟的猪猡，竟夺走了命里注定要做我妻子的女人！你那张畜生般的嘴碰过她的嘴，我的女神！”他的嗓音直打颤。
塔布盯着他，意识到自己面对的是一个疯子。
“你以为我疯了。”雷克斯说，仿佛猜中了对方的心思，“也许我真疯了，可我崇拜她。我杀杰西·特拉斯米尔，是因为我要她，等不及她，非有了钱不能占有她。”

===Original===

In a flash there came to Tab, Ursula’s words: “I killed Jesse Trasmere. I was the indirect cause.”
So she knew! That was the explanation of her strange attitude when Rex had come into the room.
And Yeh Ling knew, and had come soft-footed to the door of the private dining-room ready to leap upon the visitor if he showed any signs of hostility. Yeh Ling, the watchful, the soft-footed one, the everlasting guardian﻿—in his heart Tab Holland thanked God for Yeh Ling.

===Chinese===

霎时间，乌苏拉的话闪过塔布心头：“杀死杰西·特拉斯米尔的，是我。我是间接的肇因。”
原来她早知道！雷克斯走进房间时她那副异样的态度，这一下有了答案。
叶凌也早知道，还蹑着脚步摸到了私室雅座的门外，只等来客稍露敌意，便扑上去将他拿下。叶凌，那警醒的、脚步轻悄的、永恒的守护者——塔布·荷兰打心底里感谢上帝，幸而有叶凌。

===Original===

Rex went out of the vault and was gone for five minutes. When he came back he was carrying a writing-pad which he put on the table, pulled up a chair and sat down.
“I am going to give you the scoop of your life, Tab,” he said. There was no mockery in his voice, it was, if anything, very serious and gentle.
“I am going to write a full confession of how I killed all three of you.”

===Chinese===

雷克斯走出保险库，去了五分钟。回来时他手里拿着一本便笺簿，放在桌上，拖过一把椅子坐下。
“我要送你一条你这辈子最大的独家新闻，塔布。”他说。话音里没有一丝嘲弄；若说有什么，反倒极其认真、极其温和。
“我要写一份完完整整的自白书，交代我是怎样把你们三个人统统杀掉的。”

===Original===

Tab said nothing. This whimsical act of the man was in keeping with the theory of lunacy. For half-an-hour, he listened to the scratching of a pen and the rustle of paper as one by one the sheets were covered, blotted, and neatly put aside. What was his end to be? Rex would kill him; he had no doubt whatever on this score. The man was impervious to appeal and it was senseless to call for help. His voice would never escape the confinement of that underground room.
Carver and he had made an experiment after Trasmere’s death. He had stayed in the vault and fired a blank cartridge whilst Carver was outside the house listening, but no sound had come out.
Tab looked round for the sign of a weapon, but if Lander had brought one, it was not visible.

===Chinese===

塔布一言不发。此人这般异想天开的举动，正与疯癫之说不谋而合。足足半个钟头，他听着笔尖的沙沙声和纸页的窸窣声，眼看一页页纸写满、吸干墨迹、整整齐齐地搁到一旁。他会有怎样的下场？雷克斯会杀了他；这一点他毫无疑虑。这人对哀求无动于衷，呼救也全然无谓。他的声音无论如何也透不出那间地下斗室。
特拉斯米尔死后，卡弗和他做过一回试验：他待在保险库里，放了一发空包弹，卡弗在宅子外头听着——结果一丝声响也没有透出来。
塔布环顾四周，寻找凶器的踪影；可兰德纵然带了，也不见踪影。

===Original===

“There, I’ve said everything, and here it shall stay on the table and when they find your bones they will know why you died.”
Watching him, Tab saw him sign his name with a flourish, the old flourish which had often amused him.
“What are you going to do, Lander?” he asked quietly and Lander smiled.
“Have no fear,” he said, “I am not going to disfigure your athletic body or do you any violence. You are going to stay here and die.”

===Chinese===

“好了，我全都交代了，它就搁在这张桌上。等他们找到你的骸骨，就会知道你是为什么死的。”
塔布望着他龙飞凤舞地签下名字——还是那一手从前常逗他发笑的老派花体。
“你打算怎么办，兰德？”他平静地问，兰德笑了笑。
“不必害怕，”他说，“我不会毁伤你那运动员的身子，也不会对你施加什么暴力。你就待在这儿，等死。”

===Original===

Tab fixed him with an unwavering glance.
“You don’t suppose﻿—” he began, but thought better of it.
“I don’t suppose that your friend Mr. Carver will not come in search of you, that was what you were going to say, but believe me, Mr. Carver will never find you. In the first place he would not come here, for nobody knows that you are here. He didn’t even suspect that I was your visitor last night.”

===Chinese===

塔布用毫不游移的目光定定地盯住他。
“你该不会以为——”他开了个头，又觉得还是不说为妙。
“你想说的是——你那位朋友卡弗先生不会不来找你。可是相信我，卡弗先生永远也找不到你。首先，他不会到这儿来，因为谁也不知道你在这儿。他甚至连昨夜到你寓所去的访客就是我，都不曾起过疑。”

===Original===

“Have you a clock in your room?” said Tab, a light dawning on him.
The other frowned.
“In my bedroom, at the hotel?” he was surprised into saying.
“You haven’t!” said Tab triumphantly. “Good old Carver! He asked you the time when he was talking on the telephone, didn’t he? And you replied. He knew you were the man who came into the flat. He knew that when he called you up, you would be fully dressed and have a watch in your pocket.”
“Oh,” said the other blankly, and then: “He came to see me this morning, damn him! It was to discover whether there was a clock in the room eh?” he grinned, but there was no humour in those bared teeth. “He doesn’t know you are here, anyway,” he said. “Goodbye, Tab. Do you remember how you tried to make a reporter of me, and how I used to sit at the office studying crime? Well, I found a new trick in those cuttings, and I have been waiting years to put it into practice.”

===Chinese===

“你房里有钟吗？”塔布说，心里豁然一亮。
对方皱起了眉头。
“你是说饭店里我那间卧室？”他吃了一惊，脱口说道。
“你没有钟！”塔布得意洋洋地说。“好一个老卡弗！他在电话里同你讲话的时候，问了你时间，是不是？你回答了。他就此知道，进那套房间的就是你。他知道，他打电话找你的时候，你必定衣冠齐整，兜里揣着一块表。”
“哦。”对方木然应了一声，随即又道：“今天上午他是来看我的，这该死的！原来是要查明我房里有没有钟，嗯？”他咧嘴一笑，可那两排露出来的牙齿上不见半点笑意。“反正，他不晓得你在这儿。”他说，“再见了，塔布。你还记得不，你从前怎么想把我调教成个记者，我又怎么老坐在报馆里研读罪案？告诉你，我在那些剪报里发现了一个新花招，这么多年来，我一直在等着把它付诸实践。”

===Original===

No other word he spoke, but took something from his pocket, it was a reel of stout cotton. Then from his waistcoat he produced a new pin, and with great care and solemnity tied the thread to the end of the pin, Tab watching him intently. And all the time he was working, Rex Lander was humming a little tune, as though he were engaged in the most innocent occupation. Presently, he stuck the point of the pin in the centre of the table, and pulled at it by the thread he had fastened.
Apparently he was satisfied. He unwound a further length of cotton and when he had sufficient, he threaded the key upon it, carrying it well outside the door. The end he brought back into the vault, pushing it through one of the air-holes. Then he closed the door carefully. He had left plenty of slack for his purpose and Tab heard the click of the lock as it was fastened and his heart sank. He watched the door fascinated and saw that Lander was pulling the slack of the cotton through the air-hole. Presently the key came in sight under the door. Higher and higher came the sagging line of cotton and the key rose until it was at the table’s level, slid down the taut cotton and came to rest on the table. Tighter drew the strain on the thread, and presently the pin came out, passed through the hole in the key, leaving it in the exact centre of the table.

===Chinese===

他再没多说一个字，只从口袋里取出一样东西——那是一轴粗棉线。接着他又从马甲里摸出一枚新别针，小心翼翼、郑重其事地把棉线拴到针尾上；塔布目不转睛地瞧着。干活自始至终，雷克斯·兰德都哼着一支小调，仿佛手头做的是天下最清白无害的营生。不多时，他把针尖扎进桌子正中，牵着拴好的棉线扯了扯那枚针。
看样子他是满意了。他又放出一段棉线，估摸着够用了，便把钥匙穿到线上，一路送出门外老远。线头又带回保险库里，从一处通风口塞了进去。然后他小心翼翼地关上了门。为着这番用场，他留足了富余的线，只听锁舌咔哒一声扣死，塔布的心随之一沉。他出神地盯着那扇门，看见兰德正把富余的棉线从通风口一点点往里拽。不多时，钥匙在门底下现了身。松垂的棉线越升越高，钥匙也随之上升，直到与桌面齐平，便顺着绷直的棉线滑下来，稳稳当当停在桌上。线上的拉力越绷越紧，不一会儿，那枚别针拔了出来，从钥匙孔里滑脱而过，把钥匙端端正正留在了桌子的正中央。

===Original===

Tab watched the bright pin as it was pulled across the floor and through the ventilator.
That was the secret of the pin!
The last time the thread must have slipped or possibly the point of the pin had caught in the woodwork of the door and had fallen where he had found it. Or the man may have left it in the vault and it had been left in the passage after Trasmere’s death to add mystery to mystery.

===Chinese===

塔布望着那枚亮闪闪的别针被拽过地面，拽出通风口。
这就是那枚别针的秘密！
上一次，准是棉线滑了脱，要么就是针尖勾在了门上的木活里，才掉在他后来发现它的地方。又或者，那人索性把它留在了库里，特拉斯米尔死后它又给撂在走廊上，为的是给谜团再添一层谜团。

===Original===

“Did you see?” Lander’s voice shook with pride. “Simple, eh? And quick, Tab?”
Tab did not answer.
“I am a rotten architect, eh, Tab, but by Jingo, I’m a good bricklayer! Have you seen me lay bricks, Tab? I know so much about it that I fired the two workmen today, and said I was going to get somebody else to finish the job, Tab, I’m finishing it.”

===Chinese===

“看清了吗？”兰德的声音因得意而发颤。“简单吧，嗯？还快呢，塔布？”
塔布没有答话。
“我是个蹩脚的建筑师，是吧，塔布？可是老天在上，我是个好瓦工！你见过我砌砖么，塔布？这行当我懂得太多了，所以今天我把那两个工匠辞了，说是要另找人来做收尾。塔布，我这就来收这个尾。”

===Original===

Tab crossed his hands and tried to snap the connecting links of the cuff, but he could not get purchase. He had been so tied that he could hardly move. His head was aching terribly and he knew the cause; one of the first things he had seen on recovering consciousness, was the sandbag which Rex Lander had used as he was leaning across the table, fooled into believing that some secret passage would be revealed when he pulled.
Rex was singing softly and mingled with his voice came the click and ring of trowel on brick, that scraping sound that bricklayers make, that tap, tap, tap of the trowel as it knocked the bricks into place.

===Chinese===

塔布交叠双手，想掰断手铐的连接环，可怎么也使不上劲。他被捆得几乎动弹不得。他的头疼得厉害，缘由他也清楚：刚恢复知觉时最先看见的东西里，就有雷克斯·兰德使的那条沙袋——当时他正俯身在桌上，还傻乎乎地指望着一拉之下，就会显出什么暗道来。
雷克斯轻轻地唱着歌，和着他的嗓音，传来瓦刀敲砖的叮当声响、瓦工刮浆的那种沙沙声，还有瓦刀把砖敲到位时一记一记的嗒、嗒、嗒。

===Original===

“I shall probably be working all night,” Lander interrupted his singing to say, talking with his mouth against the ventilator. “I ought to have put the light out, but it is too late now.”
“You poor lout,” said Tab contemptuously. “You poor cheap lunatic! I can’t be angry with you, you unspeakable fat man!”
He heard the quick intake of the other’s breath and knew that he had touched him on the raw.
“Don’t you realise,” said Tab remorselessly, “that the very first place Carver will look will be this vault and when he finds it is bricked up, the very first thing he will do, will be to tear it down and all your fine explanations will not stop him. And then what will he find? The confession, which, in your crazy vanity, you have made, and my statement.”
“You’ll be dead,” howled Lander and went to work frantically.

===Chinese===

“我多半得干个通宵。”兰德停下歌，嘴贴着通风口说。“我本该把灯灭掉的，现在太迟了。”
“你这可怜的粗坯。”塔布轻蔑地说，“你这可怜的廉价疯子！我连气都气不起来，你这无可名状的胖子！”
他听见对方猛地倒抽一口气，知道自己戳到了他的痛处。
“你难道不明白，”塔布毫不留情地说，“卡弗头一个要找的地方准是这间保险库；等他发现库被砌死了，他头一件事就是把它拆掉，你那一套漂亮的解释拦不住他。到那时他会找到什么？你那份疯狂虚荣之作的自白书，还有我的书面陈述。”
“到那时你早死了！”兰德嚎道，随即发疯似的砌起砖来。

## XXXIII / 三十三

===Original===

Tab’s brain was clearing now; he was taking a cold survey of the position. Rex Lander was mad﻿—up to a point. Mad as men of abnormal vanity are mad. Vanity inspired the bravado which made him leave in the deathroom a statement which would surely hang him when it was found. Vanity and hurt pride led him to his present dreadful act, even as it had led him to search amongst Tab’s papers at the flat for Ursula’s nonexistent love letters and to tear and mutilate the portrait of the man who had won her love.
Rex was the burglar. Who else could have found his way unerringly in the dark? And Carver had known!
Madness in relation to crime fascinated Tab. In his younger and more confident days, he had written a monograph on the subject which, amidst much profitless speculation had contained one gem of reasoning﻿—the demand for corroborative evidence of criminal insanity. “Not evidence of a number of acts showing that an accused person is insanely cruel or pursues someone, apparently mad, course; but proof that in other relations he is abnormal. It is corroboration of homicidal tendencies, that a man insists on wearing odd boots of a different colour, or that he is in the habit of walking in the street without his trousers.”

===Chinese===

塔布的脑子此刻清醒过来；他正冷眼打量着眼下的处境。雷克斯·兰德是疯了——疯到一定的地步。正如天下那些虚荣心反常之人的疯法。是虚荣激起了那份逞勇的做派，让他把一纸自白留在了死亡之室（deathroom）——那东西一旦被找出来，准保送他上绞架。也正是虚荣和受挫的自尊，把他引到了眼下这桩骇人的举动上，正如当初引着他去塔布的公寓里翻检文件、搜寻乌苏拉那些根本不存在的情书，又把那个赢得她爱情的男人的相片撕毁糟蹋一般。
雷克斯就是那个窃贼。除了他，还有谁能在黑暗中把路摸得那样一准不差？而且卡弗早已知道了！
犯罪与疯狂之间的关系，向来叫塔布着迷。年轻气盛、自信满满的那会儿，他就这个题目写过一篇专论，在满纸不得要领的空论之中，倒嵌着一粒推理的明珠——主张对犯罪性精神失常提出佐证。“并不是罗列一桩桩行为，证明被告残忍得发狂，或是死心塌地奉行某种显然疯狂的行径；而是要证明他在其他关系上已然失常。一个人执意要穿两只颜色各异的靴子，或是惯于不穿裤子就上街，这才算得上杀人倾向的佐证。”

===Original===

By this standard Rex was sane.
So thought Tab with one half of his brain; the other half was taking stock of his immediate hope of escape. He was handcuffed behind; around his legs was a strap that was beyond the reach of his teeth. Between the links of the handcuffs and the strap a cord had been fastened and pulled tight so that his knees were doubled up without hope of straightening, unless he could succeed in breaking the cord. If that were possible, there was the key within reach. He made an effort, pulling up his legs still further and then jerking out his feet violently. The pain of it nearly made him faint, tough as he was. It seemed as if both his shoulders were dislocated. He could feel the cord; it was stout﻿—perhaps with finger and nail he could pick it into shreds, fibre by fibre, or cut it with his thumb nail.
After the wall was up his time would be very short unless the vault contained some other ventilator which neither Carver nor he had discovered. And yet even if the cord was broken he must wait until Lander had completed his work. It would be fatal, handcuffed as he was, to break out whilst Rex was on hand. His only chance was to free himself of the trussing cord whilst the work outside was in progress, get the key and by some contortion, unlock the door and employ his great strength to push through the newly laid bricks. The time would be short, but the cord was unbreakable.

===Chinese===

照这个标准衡量，雷克斯神志健全。
塔布的半个脑子里转着这些念头；另外半个脑子却在估量眼下脱身的希望有几成。他两手反铐在背后；腿上捆着一道皮带，牙齿够不着它。手铐链与皮带之间又系了一根绳索，勒得紧紧的，害得他双膝蜷折，再无伸直之望——除非他能弄断那根绳索。倘若做得到，钥匙就在够得着的地方。他使劲试了一把，把双腿再往上提，接着猛力向外挣蹬双脚。那阵疼痛几乎叫他晕死过去，尽管他筋骨结实。两条肩膀仿佛都脱了臼。他摸得着那根绳索；它很粗壮——或许凭手指和指甲，他能把它一丝一缕地拆散，或是用大拇指的指甲把它割断。
墙一旦砌死，他的时间就会所剩无几——除非保险库里另有一处他和卡弗都不曾发现的通风口。然而，即便绳索弄断了，他也必须等兰德把活儿干完。像这样两手反铐，趁雷克斯还在跟前时贸然脱身，无异于自寻死路。他唯一的机会，是趁外头砌墙的工程还在进行，先挣脱这根捆缚的绳索，弄到钥匙，再扭动身子开了锁，凭他的神力推垮新砌的砖层。时间会很紧，可那绳索偏偏弄不断。

===Original===

He rolled over on one side and bracing his feet against the leg of the table and his head against the wall, succeeded in getting on to his knees. Bound as he was, his eyes were at the level of the tabletop. Shelves, steel shelves﻿—perhaps there was a rough edge somewhere. He hobbled along on his knees and saw a promising place.
Again he rolled over, this time on his back, raising his feet until, by straining, he brought the cord against the shelf. And all the time came the ring of the trowel and the crooning song of Rex Lander. He knew at once that it was a hopeless proposition. The sharp edge was beneath the shelf, he could only reach the upper surface. Crossing his legs to get a better purchase, he felt the strap slip upward. By pushing at the strap he brought it to below his knees and he could have yelled his delight for now the cord was slack and he would, he thought, at least be able to stand.
The sound of amateur bricklaying ceased suddenly and Rex came to the grating.

===Chinese===

他侧身翻滚过去，双脚抵住桌腿，头顶着墙，总算撑起身子跪了起来。虽是这般捆着，他的眼睛恰与桌面齐平。架子，钢架子——说不定哪处有道毛糙的棱边。他跪着一步步挪动，看到一处大有指望的地方。
他又翻了个身，这回仰面朝天，把双脚高高抬起，攒足了劲，让绳索蹭上架子。而这整个工夫，瓦刀的叮当声和雷克斯·兰德低声哼唱的歌一直不绝于耳。他立刻明白，这条路走不通：锋利的棱边在架子底下，他只能够着上面那层。他把双腿交叠起来好借上力，却觉出那道皮带往上一滑。他推顶着皮带，把它挪到了膝盖以下——他简直要乐得叫出声来，因为绳索这下松了，他心想，自己至少能站得起来了。
外行砌砖的动静戛然而止，雷克斯来到了格栅跟前。

===Original===

“You’re wasting your time doing all those funny tricks,” he said confidently. “I practised that tie all one evening and you’ll not get away. If you come out you’ll be sorry!”
“Avaunt fat man!” snarled Tab. “Get to your fleshpots, gross feeder!”
Rex chuckled.
“Partial to tab lines, eh?”

===Chinese===

“你耍这些滑稽把戏，纯粹是白费工夫。”他满有把握地说，“这个捆法我练了整整一个晚上，你休想脱身。你要是出得来，有得你后悔的！”
“退下，胖子！”塔布咬牙嘶声骂道，“觅你的酒池肉林去吧，饕餮之徒！”
雷克斯呵呵一笑。
“爱搬小报腔的台词，是吧？”

===Original===

“Get out of my sight,” said Tab, “you theatrical poseur! All the money you have couldn’t make you a gentleman﻿—”
He was interrupted by the torrent of rage which swept down upon him from the impotent man outside.
“I wish I’d killed you,” he screamed. “My God, if I could get in﻿—”
“But you can’t,” said Tab, “that is why the position is so remarkably free from anxiety. Carver knows﻿—don’t forget that. Carver will have you on the trap﻿—he has promised himself that treat, though I can’t see how they’ll hang a crazy man,” he went on. Lander clawed at the steel plate, sobbing in his rage.

===Chinese===

“从我眼前滚开，”塔布说，“你这装腔作势的戏子！把你全副家财堆起来，也堆不出一个绅士——”
话未说完，一阵狂怒便当头浇了下来——正是外头那个无计可施的人发出来的。
“恨不得当初杀了你！”他尖叫道，“老天爷，我要是能进去——”
“可你进不来，”塔布说，“所以眼下这局面才这么出奇地高枕无忧。卡弗知情——别忘这一条。卡弗会把你送上绞架——这顿好戏他早给自己许下了。只不过我看不出，他们怎么能绞死一个疯子。”他接着说。兰德抓挠着钢板，狂怒中呜呜咽咽。

===Original===

“I’m not mad,” he screamed. “I’m sane! Nobody can put me away﻿—I’m not mad, Tab, you know I’m not.”
“You are just the maddest thing that ever lived,” said Tab inflexibly. “Thank God I saved Ursula﻿—” the words were out of his lips before he regretted them.
He had turned the mind of the man at the door in the last direction he wanted it to go.
“Ursula﻿—mine! Do you hear, she’s mine now.”

===Chinese===

“我没疯，”他尖叫道，“我神志清楚！谁也不能把我送进疯人院——我没疯，塔布，你知道我没疯。”
“你简直就是开天辟地以来头一号大疯子。”塔布斩钉截铁地说，“感谢上帝，我救下了乌苏拉——”话一出口，他就后悔了。
这一句话，把门口那人的心思引到了他最不愿意它去的地方。
“乌苏拉——是我的！你听见没有，她如今是我的了。”

===Original===

Tab heard the clash of the trowel as it was thrown down and the sound of hurrying feet, growing fainter.
Tab wriggled himself to his knees, threw back his weight and came to his feet. It was a terrible strain to support himself, but he was standing, doubled up grotesquely but free to move his feet a few inches at a time. So he crept to the table and leaning over, pulled the key toward him with his chin. He brought it carefully to the edge, then gripped the handle in his teeth and shuffled to the door. But the lock was set so close to the wall that he could not get his head into position to insert the key. He tried twice, and then what he feared, happened. The key dropped from his teeth with a clang to the floor.
He was on the point of kneeling when he heard somebody moving about, Rex opened the door to the sitting-room and shouted something; what it was, Tab could not hear, but there came to him a noise as if somebody was breaking sticks. Crack, crack, crack! it went, and then he sniffed. It was a faint smell of burning petrol he had detected, and he knew that for him the worst had happened. Mayfield was on fire.

===Chinese===

塔布听见瓦刀摔在地上的当啷一声，还有渐渐远去的急促脚步声。
塔布扭动身子跪起来，上身后仰，一较劲，竟站了起来。撑住自己已是万分吃力，可他到底站着了，身子佝偻得怪模怪样，双脚却能一次挪上几英寸。就这样，他蹭到桌边，俯下身去，用下巴把钥匙一点点拱向自己。他小心翼翼把它拱到桌沿，然后用牙咬住钥匙柄，一步一步蹭到门边。可锁头安得紧贴墙壁，他的脑袋怎么也摆不到能把钥匙插进锁眼的位置。他试了两回，随后，他最怕的事情终于发生了。钥匙当啷一声从他齿间脱落，掉在地板上。
他正要跪下去，忽听得有人在四处走动。雷克斯打开了通向起居室的那扇门，喊了句什么；喊的是什么，塔布听不清，但一阵声响传到他耳中，像是有人在掰断树枝。噼啪、噼啪、噼啪！响个不停。接着他抽了抽鼻子。他嗅出的是一缕汽油燃烧的淡淡气味，于是他明白，对他而言，最坏的厄运已经临头。梅菲尔德起火了。

## XXXIV / 三十四

===Original===

“No answer,” said Exchange.
Mr. Carver rubbed his nose irritably and glanced up at the clock. Then he lifted the instrument again.
“Give me Hertford 906,” he said.
In five minutes the call was signalled.

===Chinese===

“没人应答。”接线生说。
卡弗先生烦躁地揉了揉鼻子，抬眼望了望时钟。然后他又拿起了听筒。
“给我接赫特福德 906。”他说。
五分钟后，电话接通了。

===Original===

“Miss Ardfern﻿—Carver speaking, I’m very, very sorry﻿—got you out of bed, did I﻿—so sorry! What time did Tab leave﻿—half-past eight﻿—you don’t say so? Oh yes, he’s all right﻿—gone to the office﻿—oh yes, he does some Saturday nights. Don’t worry﻿—not at all. Only he promised to call﻿—can’t trust love-smitten young men, eh﻿—certainly I’d call you if there was anything wrong.”

===Chinese===

“阿德芬小姐——我是卡弗。实在对不住、太对不住——把您从床上叫起来了吧——真抱歉！塔布是什么时候走的——八点半——当真？哦，他没事——去报馆了——哦对，有几个星期六晚上他是要去报馆的。别担心——一点儿也不用担心。只是他说好要来电话的——热恋中的年轻人靠不住啊，是不是——真有什么事，我准会打电话告诉您。”

===Original===

He put the instrument back and looked up at the clock. Then he pressed a bell. The sergeant who answered was dressed as if he expected to go out into the storm at any moment.
“Men ready﻿—good. Pitts Hotel; two men to each entrance, one to the upper floor in case he breaks that way. Four good men for his room﻿—men sharp enough to dodge his quick firing batteries﻿—he’ll shoot.”
“Who is the man, sir?”
“Mr. Rex Lander. I want him for murder and forgery; attempted murder and burglary. If he’s not at home it will be easy. We’ll take him as he comes into the hotel. One of the night porters is probably being well paid by him. He was the fellow who stalled me last night and gave Lander a chance to get to his room and use the telephone. So we’d better get there before the room clerk goes off duty. And don’t forget to impress upon the men that Lander will shoot! If the night porter is on duty we’ll take him. He’s not to get to the telephone. Beat his head off if he tries. I’ll be with you in five minutes.”

===Chinese===

他放下听筒，抬头望了望时钟，然后按了按铃。应铃而来的巡佐一身行头，仿佛随时准备冲进风暴里去。
“人手齐了——好。皮特饭店：每个门口各派两人，楼上再派一个，防他从那条路脱身。他房间安排四个得力的人——手脚要利索，躲得开他的连环快枪——他会开枪的。”
“目标是哪一位，长官？”
“雷克斯·兰德先生。我要抓他，罪名是谋杀和伪造，外加谋杀未遂和入室盗窃。他要是不在，事情就好办。等他回饭店时动手。夜班门房里多半有一个拿了他的重金。昨晚就是那家伙把我搪塞过去，让兰德得了空回房打电话。所以咱们最好赶在客房登记员下班之前到。还有，别忘了叮嘱弟兄们：兰德会开枪！夜班门房要是在当班，连他一并拿下。不能让他挨着电话。他敢去碰，就砸烂他的脑袋。我五分钟后就来。”

===Original===

He made another attempt to get in touch with Tab but was no more successful. Then a thought struck him. He remembered that Tab had told him the name of the sporting tenant who occupied the flat below. But Tab had also told him that this gentleman was seldom at home. Still, there was a chance.
He waited, the receiver at his ear.
“Is that Mr. Cowling? Why, I am sorry to disturb you. I’m Inspector Carver, a friend of Holland’s, who lives above you. You haven’t any idea whether he is at home? I’ve been trying to get him﻿—you’ve heard the phone going? Yes, that was me.”
“He came in about an hour ago,” said the tenant’s voice, “then somebody called him. I can hear the phone very plainly from my room. Bex or Wex or some such name.”
“Rex?” asked the inspector quickly, “yes, yes﻿—he went out did he? Thank you.”
He sat staring down at his blotting pad for a minute, then he got up and pulled on his raincoat.

===Chinese===

他又一次设法联系塔布，还是没有结果。忽然他灵机一动：他记起塔布跟他说过楼下那位租户的名字——那是个好赌玩乐的先生。不过塔布也说过，这位先生难得在家。可无论如何，总归有一线机会。
他把听筒贴在耳边等着。
“是考林（Cowling）先生吗？哎呀，打扰您了，真过意不去。我是卡弗探长，荷兰的朋友，就住在您楼上。他在不在家，您知道吗？我一直打不通他的电话——电话铃响您听见了吧？对，那是我打的。”
“他大概一个钟头前回来的，”那租户的声音说，“随后有人打电话找他。我在自己屋里听电话听得一清二楚。什么贝克斯还是韦克斯，反正就是这么个名字。”
“雷克斯？”探长急问，“对，对——他出去了，是吗？谢谢。”
他坐着低头盯了吸墨纸垫一分钟，然后站起身，套上雨衣。

===Original===

His squad were getting into cabs as he came out of the station and he entered the first of these.
Had he left it too long, he wondered? The warrant had been issued, after he had taken the sworn statement of the man Green, formerly butler to Jesse Trasmere. He had brought this witness from Australia, had cabled to him the very day that Trasmere was found murdered; Green’s reply had confirmed his suspicions.
Too late now to regret his delay. Accompanied by his sergeant he strolled into the hotel. The lounge was empty, half the lights had been extinguished and, as he had expected, the room clerk had gone, leaving a stalwart night porter in charge.

===Chinese===

他走出警察局时，部下们正纷纷登上出租汽车，他坐进了头一辆。
他是不是拖得太久了？他心里犯着嘀咕。逮捕令已经签发——那是在他录下格林先生的宣誓证词之后，此人从前是杰西·特拉斯米尔的贴身男仆。他把这位证人从澳大利亚带了回来；发现特拉斯米尔遇害的当天他就发了电报，格林的复电证实了他的怀疑。
如今再悔恨耽搁已经太迟。他带着巡佐，缓步走进饭店。休息厅里空无一人，半数的灯已经熄了；不出他所料，客房登记员已经下班，只留一个魁梧的夜班门房当值。

===Original===

“Mr. Lander, sir? No, I don’t think he is in. I’ll get through to his room.”
“Don’t touch that telephone!” said the inspector, “I am an officer of the police. Show me to his room.”
The man hesitated the fraction of a second and then:
“If you monkey with the switchboard I’ll put you where the rats won’t bite you,” said Carver sharply. “Come out of that!”
The man obeyed sulkily.
“I’ve done no wrong,” he said, “I was only trying to﻿—”

===Chinese===

“您找兰德先生？我看他不在。我这就往他房间接个电话。”
“别碰那电话！”探长说，“我是警务人员。带我去他房间。”
那人迟疑了不到一秒，随即——
“你敢碰一下交换机，我就把你塞到连耗子都咬不着你的地方去。”卡弗厉声道，“从那儿出来！”
那人乖乖照办，一脸悻悻。
“我没干什么坏事，”他说，“我只是想——”

===Original===

“Watch this man,” said Carver. “Now give me the key of Lander’s room.”
The man took down a key from the hook and threw it on the counter.
Rex Lander’s suite was empty as Carver had expected.
“I want a thorough search made of these rooms,” he said to the sergeant. “I will leave you a man to help you. All stations must be maintained until they are withdrawn. He may come in late.”

===Chinese===

“看住这个人。”卡弗说，“把兰德房间的钥匙给我。”
那人从挂钩上取下一把钥匙，扔在柜台上。
果然不出卡弗所料，雷克斯·兰德的套房空无一人。
“这几间房要彻底搜一遍。”他对巡佐说，“我给你留个人帮忙。各处岗哨保持不动，直到下令撤岗。他也许会晚回来。”

===Original===

He waited for half-an-hour inside the hall but though there was a constant procession of cars and cabs laden with people returning from the theatres, there was no sight of Rex Lander.
The hall porter became informative.
“I’ve got a wife and three children. I don’t want to get into any trouble. What do you want Mr. Lander for?”
“I’ll not tell you,” said Carver curtly.

===Chinese===

他在门厅里守候了半个钟头，尽管小汽车和出租汽车络绎不绝、一辆辆载着看戏归来的人们，却始终不见雷克斯·兰德的影子。
那门房话渐渐多了起来。
“我家里有老婆和三个孩子。我不想惹什么麻烦。你们找兰德先生到底是为了什么事？”
“我不告诉你。”卡弗没好气地说。

===Original===

“If it is anything very serious I know nothing about it,” said the porter. “I might as well tell you that I did him a favour the other night.”
“Last night was it?”
“Yes. He was in the hall when somebody telephoned through for him, and he asked me to keep them waiting whilst he went up to his room. He told me it was a lady friend that he wasn’t on good terms with. That’s all I know about it. He is a very nice man,” he added in justification.
“A perfect peach,” agreed Carver sardonically. “Well?”

===Chinese===

“要真是人命关天的大事，那我可一概不知。”门房说，“不妨跟您说了吧，前几天晚上我还帮过他一个忙。”
“是昨天晚上？”
“是。他正在门厅里，有人打电话来找他，他叫我让对方等着，自己上楼回房去了。他跟我说，是他一位合不来的女性朋友。我知道的就这么多。他这人挺不错的。”他又补了一句，算是给自己辩白。
“真是颗十全十美的水蜜桃。”卡弗讥诮地应道，“还有呢？”

===Original===

One of the men he had left to search the room was coming hurriedly across the lounge. He drew Carver aside and produced from his pocket a long-barrelled revolver of an old-fashioned make.
“We found this in one of the drawers,” he said.
Carver examined it curiously. The moment he saw it he knew what it was, even before he found the Chinese characters engraved on the steel of the butt.
“I thought so,” he said. “It is a Chinese issue, the sort they served out to their army officers about twelve years ago. I think you’ll find it was the property of Trasmere.” He snapped it open. It was fully loaded, containing four live and two used cartridges. “Keep that carefully apart. Wrap it in paper and have it photographed for fingerprints,” he ordered. “You found nothing else?”

===Chinese===

他留下搜房的人里，有一个正快步穿过休息厅走来。他把卡弗拉到一旁，从口袋里掏出一支旧式长管左轮手枪。
“我们在一个抽屉里找到的。”他说。
卡弗好奇地细察这支枪。一看见它，他就知道了它的来路——还不等发现枪柄钢材上镌着的中国字。
“果然不出我所料。”他说，“这是中国的制式枪，大约十二年前配发给他们陆军军官的那一种。我想你们会查明，这是特拉斯米尔的物件。”他啪地甩开弹巢。枪里满装着子弹：四发实弹，两发已击发。“把这支枪单独妥存。用纸包好，送去拍照提取指纹。”他吩咐道，“没发现别的？”

===Original===

“There’s a receipted bill from Burbidges for a sapphire ring,” said the man and Carver smiled faintly.
